SEC-TLS-05 and SEC-DAT-03 made `ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD` the gate for an export that holds password or encrypted fields; this path sends the same rows off the server and reads no permission. The Admin map says an archive's download "is later, under the sealed `ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD`". In the seed `ADMIN` holds the permission, so the path adds nothing there; it matters where the two are held apart (a System operator group, or `ADMIN` with the permission ended, as the demo data does).
- **Evidence:** `backup.rs:86-90` `let remote_id = param_text(parameters, "systemMessageRemoteId");` ... `match mirror_options(parameters).and_then(|options| mirror(facade, &remote_id, &options, &outcome))`; `:219-222` `let Some(remote) = find_one(facade, REMOTE, "systemMessageRemoteId", remote_id)?` ... `let send_url = field(&remote, "sendUrl");`
- **Evidence:** `backup.rs:110-113` `let dir = backup_dir(&param_text(parameters, "backupDir"), &moqui_lib::env::get_prop_or_env(DIR_PROPERTY));`; `ServerServices.xml:102` `authenticate="false" transaction="ignore">`
- **Evidence:** `ConnectionDetail.xml:46-48` `<transition name="updateMessageRemote">` ... `<service-call name="update#moqui.service.message.SystemMessageRemote"/>`; `JobDetail.xml:75-77` `<transition name="createJobParameter">` ... `<service-call name="create#moqui.service.job.ServiceJobParameter"/>`; `:105-107` `<transition name="runJobNow">` ... `<actions><script>ec.service.job(jobName).run()</script></actions>`
- **Evidence:** `rg ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD crates runtime/components/base` (tests left out): the only check is `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:1754` `writer.full(crate::user::has_permission("ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD").unwrap_or(false));` and the data writer it sets; `framework/data/SecurityTypeData.xml:112` `<moqui.security.UserGroupPermission userGroupId="ADMIN" userPermissionId="ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD" fromDate="0"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** what `raw()` (`tools/template/AdminMount.js`) asks beyond the user's own grant on System; whether the demo's view-only `SYSTEM_APP` refuses these transitions; whether any static route serves a directory a `backupDir` could name (the premise of SEC-TLS-07), which would be a download with no remote at all. Looked for and not found: a permission check in `backup_embedded_database`, `mirror` and `mirror_s3` (only `fence::check_effect` and the sim-mode refusal); an EECA or a `sensitive.rs` list that holds `ServiceJobParameter` (`SystemMessageRemote` is in `SECRET_CONFIG_ENTITY_KEYS`, which stops the generic tools, not the System screen's own entity-auto transition). The delegated path holds: `ConfigurationConnections.js:730-745` keeps a remote of a type with a `sendPath` read only, and the backup job has no `JobRegistration`, so `store#Job` refuses it.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat the mirror destination and the backup directory as the download they are: refuse a write of the backup job's `backupDir`, `systemMessageRemoteId` and `mirror*` parameters and of the mirror remote's `sendUrl` and credentials without `ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD` (a security EECA, as for the sealed-permission rows, skipped by seed loads), and have `backup#EmbeddedDatabase` called by name with a `backupDir` or remote other than the job's stored ones ask for the permission when a user is signed in.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `service/backup.rs:84-113`: the service takes `backupDir` and `systemMessageRemoteId` from its parameters and mirrors to that remote; no permission is read in these lines. Not read by the lead: `:213-271`, the three doors named (ConnectionDetail, JobDetail, Service Run) and what each requires. It bites only where `ADMIN` and `ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD` are held apart (the seed gives both).

#### SEC-TLS-84. Data Load's check shows a Tools view holder the stored value of any field of any record, restricted entities and password hashes included

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/load_feed/loader.rs:431-437`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/load_feed/check.rs:358-430`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:1902-1915`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Load.xml:200-229`, `:262-263`. Reports: round 3 `B4.md` and `B11.md`.
- **Reached by:** a holder of view on the Tools app, by a GET of `/apps/tools/Data/Load` with `runCheck=true` and `xmlText` (or `jsonText`, `csvText`, `location`); also the `load` transition with `checkOnly`. In the demo the screen is replaced (`dev/demo/test/Demo.spec.js:27`).
- **What happens:** The check section runs as the screen renders, so under view. It builds a loader, sets `restrictSensitiveEntities(true)` and calls `checkInfo`. In a check `store_record` returns `check_record` before it looks at the restriction, so no entity is refused. `check_tree` finds the record by the primary key in the text through the entity facade and, for each field whose given value differs, adds a row with `dbValue`, the stored value, which the form shows and the message text repeats. So `<moqui.security.UserAccount userId="..." currentPassword="x" passwordSalt="x"/>` answers the hash and salt, and the same works for `DatabaseConnection`, `moqui.basic.email.EmailServer` (`mailPassword`), `SystemMessageRemote` (`sharedSecret`), `UserAuthcFactor` (`factorOption`), `DatabaseHost.adminPassword`, `DatabaseAudit`, and mr-mantle's `CreditCard` (`cardNumber`). B11 reads the facade's find as decrypting, so an encrypted field comes back in clear. These are the values the fixes of SEC-DAT-03 and SEC-TLS-05 took out of REST, the grid and an export made without `ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD`. In the seed `ADMIN` holds that permission, so the gap is for a deployment that grants Tools for view, or `ADMIN` without the sealed permission.
- **Evidence:** `loader.rs:432-436` `if super::check::checking() {` / `return super::check::check_record(facade, &record);` / `}` / `if options.restrict_sensitive_entities {` / `refuse_restricted(facade, &record, options)?;`
- **Evidence:** `check.rs:380` `let mut find = facade.find(&record.entity)?;`, `:384` `find.one()?`; `:410` `let stored = db.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null);`; `:418` `row.insert("dbValue".to_string(), stored);`; `:422` the message, "Field {}.{name} did not match; check (file) value [{}], db value [{}] ..."
- **Evidence:** `tools.rs:1905` `let source = || load_source(&facade, &factory, &text, &locations, &load_options);`, `:1907` `crate::entity::load_feed::check_info_with(source)`
- **Evidence:** `Load.xml:200` `<section name="CheckDiffSection" condition="runCheck == 'true'">`, `:225` `edl.restrictSensitiveEntities(true)`, `:229` `fieldsChecked = edl.checkInfo(diffInfoList, messages)`, `:262-263` `<field name="dbValue"><default-field><display/></default-field></field>`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. That the authorization inherited from the Tools screen lets the find of `moqui.security.UserAccount` through for a view-only holder (the other Tools browse screens read it the same way). Whether an `encrypt` field comes back in clear here: likely for B4, read as such by B11. A `location` is any local path and is never fetched (both reports), so a file on the server that is entity data is compared with the database too. Looked for and not found: a restricted check or a field mask in `check_record`, `check_tree` and `list_tree` (`check.rs` has no use of `sensitive`); a secret-field filter on the diff rows; a permission in the section or in `load_data` (`tools.rs:1891-1915`).
- **Suggested fix:** When `restrict_sensitive_entities` is set, run `refuse_restricted` before the `checking()` branch in `store_record` and at the other two `checking()` sites (`loader.rs:533`, `:735`), and leave out of the comparison, or report with no value, the fields `sensitive::generic_access` hides (password and encrypted). Run the check from a `post` transition with `authz-action="all"` rather than at render.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `load_feed/loader.rs:431-436`: `store_record` returns `check_record` when checking, before `refuse_restricted`. Confirmed for the order. Not read by the lead: `check.rs:410-418` (`dbValue`), decryption.

#### SEC-TLS-85. Service Run calls the entity-auto services of the entities the generic tools refuse (SEC-TLS-02's and SEC-TLS-11's fixes do not hold on this path)

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Service/ServiceRun.xml:21-25`, `:43-47`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/call.rs:1522-1538`
- **Reached by:** a holder of all actions on the Tools app (`ADMIN` in the seed), through the `run` or `runJson` transition with `serviceName=update#moqui.server.instance.InstanceImageTypeEnv` (or `create#`, `store#`, `delete#` of another such entity).
- **What happens:** The transition calls the named service with the request's parameters. For an entity-auto name `run_entity` refuses only `is_audit_entity`. The host-configuration entities (SEC-TLS-02), the service-written ones (`EntityAuditLog`, `RecoveryAction`, `RecoveryControl`, `IngestKey`, `ScrapeTarget`; SEC-TLS-11) and the secret-config ones are refused by entity REST, Service REST, the loader, the grid and ETL, each in its own code, and not here. So an `ADMIN` without `INSTANCE_ADMIN` still plants an `envValue` template that `create#AppInstance` expands as script for a holder, or changes `DatabaseHost.hostAddress` under a stored `adminPassword`; one without the `MONITOR_*` permissions writes a recovery row or an ingest key.
- **Evidence:** `ServiceRun.xml:25` `serviceResults = ec.service.sync().name(serviceName).parameters(context).call()`
- **Evidence:** `call.rs:1534` `if crate::entity::sensitive::is_audit_entity(noun) {` (the one refusal of `run_entity`)
- **Evidence:** the callers of `is_generic_write_refused`: `service/rest.rs:676`, `script/host/tools.rs:3230`, `moqui-tools/src/grid.rs:947`, `etl/access.rs:182`, `entity/query_rest.rs:232`, `load_feed/loader.rs:487`; not `service/call.rs`
- **Not confirmed:** the binding from `ec.service.sync().call()` to `sync_with` and `run_entity` (`call.rs:1445`) was not read line by line; the same reach through System › Operations `runJobNow` was not followed. Looked for and not found: a check in `ServiceRun.xml`; an entity ECA for `moqui.server.instance` or `moqui.monitor` under `runtime/components/base/*/entity/*.eecas.xml`.
- **Suggested fix:** Put the refusal in one place: have `run_entity` refuse the `is_generic_write_refused` nouns unless the caller is the entity's own service (a flag the framework's own calls set), as it already does for the audit keys.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `service/call.rs:1529-1538`: `run_entity` refuses only `is_audit_entity`; the identity, host-config and service-written lists (SEC-TLS-02, SEC-TLS-11) are not asked there. Confirmed for the rule. Not read by the lead: `ServiceRun.xml:25`, and whether another gate stands in front of a Service Run call.

#### SEC-TLS-86. A read-only SQL run on the embedded cluster is the bootstrap superuser's, so it runs programs and reads files of the host

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/info.rs:880-904`; `crates/moqui-drivers/src/embedded.rs:742`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/sql.rs:187`, `:935-942`, `:1184`, `:1341-1353`
- **Reached by:** a holder of `SQL_RUNNER_WEB` (sealed, `ADMIN_ADV`) with all actions on Tools, through `Tools/Sql` `run` with `allowChanges` false; the read parts also through a grid SQL source (`get#DataGridRows`).
- **What happens:** A read-only run is one statement in a transaction set `READ ONLY`. On MySQL only a query runs that way; on Postgres any statement does, and the classifier is not asked. The embedded cluster's connections are the bootstrap superuser's. A read-only Postgres transaction refuses table writes, not `COPY (query) TO PROGRAM '...'`, `COPY ... TO '/path'`, `pg_read_file`, `lo_export`, `pg_terminate_backend` or `pg_notify` (from knowledge of Postgres, not from this code). So with no token and no typed database name the holder starts a process as the server's operating-system user and reads `runtime/.env`, and a grid SQL source (`SELECT pg_read_file(...)`) reads files. That is the reach `TERMINAL_HOST` and `CONF_EDIT` seal, asked here only as `SQL_RUNNER_WEB`. `pg_notify('moqui_cluster', ...)` reaches the cluster channel of `cluster_transport=notify` (`crates/moqui-host/src/cluster_notify.rs:185`). The audit row of such a statement has `isChange` N. On MySQL `SELECT ... INTO OUTFILE` is a query by its first word and is classified a plain read.
- **Evidence:** `info.rs:893-897` `if syntax == SqlSyntax::Mysql && !is_query(sql) { return Err(Error::new("only a query runs read only on MySQL and MariaDB ...` and no such test for Postgres; `:899-903` `tx.with_connection(|conn| conn.set_transaction_read_only())?;` / `tx.discard_connection_at_end()?;` / `run_statement(tx, sql, syntax, &mut options)`
- **Evidence:** `embedded.rs:742` `PostgresDriver::unix_socket(BOOTSTRAP_SUPERUSER, database, socket)`
- **Evidence:** `sql.rs:941-942` `let result = if read_only || statement.own_transaction {` / `runner.run_one(statement, sql, params)` (no use of `statement.changes` to refuse); `:1184` `is_change: !self.read_only && statement.changes`
- **Evidence:** `sql.rs:187` `"select" => !words.iter().any(|word| word == "into") || syntax == SqlSyntax::Mysql && !writes(&words),`
- **Not confirmed:** that Postgres allows each named function and `COPY ... TO PROGRAM` in a read-only transaction, on the embedded version; whether a cluster message taken from `NOTIFY` is authenticated. The role of an external (not embedded) Postgres datasource is the deployment's. Looked for and not found: a statement allow list for a read-only Postgres run; a lesser role for Tools' SQL (the group's own transaction manager is used, `catalog.rs:190-192`). Several statements in one text are not possible: the drivers prepare each statement (`postgres.rs:731`, `mysql.rs:450`), and `split_sql_as` reads quotes, dollar quotes and comments as the server does.
- **Second reading:** unit B12 (`round3/B12.md`, at `b248ef77c`) read the cluster's roles: the embedded cluster has one role, the bootstrap superuser `postgres` (`embedded.rs:742`, `entity_facade.rs:93`), and it is the connection of the Tools SQL runner, grid SQL and ETL SQL sources; a read-only run only sets `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY` (`driver.rs:206-208`). What Postgres then allows in a read-only transaction was not read.
- **Suggested fix:** Run Tools' SQL on the embedded cluster as a role that is not a superuser (no `pg_execute_server_program`, `pg_read_server_files`, `pg_write_server_files`), or refuse `COPY`, `DO`, `CALL` and the file and program functions in a read-only run and take a read-only Postgres run only for what `plain_read` accepts. Say in the map that `SQL_RUNNER_WEB` on a superuser connection is host access.

#### SEC-TLS-87. A Tools data load sets any account's password hash

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:1868`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/load_feed/loader.rs:449-476`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Load.xml:18-51`
- **Reached by:** a holder of all actions on Tools (`ADMIN`), through `Tools/Data/Load` `load` with `xmlText`, `jsonText` or `csvText`.
- **What happens:** The loader a script makes never sets `refuse_password_fields`; only EntitySync's does (SEC-DAT-08). `restrictSensitiveEntities(true)` refuses the restricted entities, and `UserAccount` is not one, so a record `<moqui.security.UserAccount userId="..." currentPassword="..." passwordSalt="..." passwordHashType="..."/>` is stored. The account screens' rule (a caller does not change an account that holds access the caller lacks) is not asked, so an `ADMIN` signs in as an `ADMIN_ADV` member.
- **Evidence:** `tools.rs:1868` `refuse_password_fields: false,`
- **Evidence:** `loader.rs:468-471` `if options.refuse_password_fields` / `&& crate::entity::sensitive::is_user_account_entity(&full_name)` ...
- **Evidence:** `Load.xml:41` `edl.xmlText(xmlText)`, `:51` `edl.restrictSensitiveEntities(true)`
- **Not confirmed:** `prepare_fields` was not read; a sign-in with a loaded hash and the second factor were not traced. Looked for and not found: another strip of the password fields in `store_record` and `write_tree` (`loader.rs:431-446`, `:491-500`); an entity ECA on `UserAccount`.
- **Suggested fix:** Set `refuse_password_fields` whenever `restrictSensitiveEntities` is set from a script (seed loads by type or component do not set it), or key it to a sealed permission.

#### SEC-TLS-88. A database job runs whatever SQL its statement rows hold, and the generic tools write those rows

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-tools/src/jobs.rs:508-546`, `:641-642`, `:852-859`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/sensitive.rs:72-82`, `:107-118`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/call.rs:1534`; `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/DatabaseEntities.xml:48-66`
- **Reached by:** (a) a `DATABASE_ADMIN` holder who does not hold `SQL_RUNNER_WEB`: writes a `DatabaseJobStatement` row (Tools › Data's record editor, the grid's save, an ETL entity target, entity REST, a data load), then runs `run#DatabaseJob` through `Tools/Database/JobDetail.xml`. (b) an `ADMIN` of the Tools app without `DATABASE_ADMIN`: changes the `statementText` of a plan another administrator made, or adds a statement to it, and that holder's Run executes it.
- **What happens:** `run#DatabaseJob` checks `DATABASE_ADMIN`, reads the job's statements from the table and executes each text on a connection of its own, outside a transaction, with authorization off. Nothing ties the text to the plan the server made: no hash, no creator check, no check of the statement's form. The entities are in none of the lists that refuse a generic write, and `run_entity` refuses only the audit entities. So the SQL runner's rules (`SQL_RUNNER_WEB`; read only unless Allow Changes with a one-time token; the typed database name on production) are gone around, and the `DbaJob` audit row names the runner, not who wrote the text. The module's comment says the opposite: "no service takes statement text from the caller, so a job is not a way around the SQL runner's own permission" (`jobs.rs:16-18`).
- **Evidence:** `jobs.rs:512` `if !require_admin("Running a database job") {` (the only check of the run)
- **Evidence:** `jobs.rs:641-642` `let sql = field(statement, "statementText");` / `let outcome = run_statement(facade, &db, job_id, &seq, &sql, lock_timeout);`; `:858` `conn.execute(sql, &[])`
- **Evidence:** `sensitive.rs:72-82` `const AUDIT_ENTITY_KEYS: &[&str] = &[ "databaseaudit", "sqlruntoken", "databaseconnection", "etlmapping", ...` (no `databasejob`, no `databasejobstatement`); `:107-118` `SERVICE_WRITTEN_ENTITY_KEYS` names neither
- **Evidence:** `call.rs:1534` `if crate::entity::sensitive::is_audit_entity(noun) {`; `DatabaseEntities.xml:53` `<field name="statementText" type="text-very-long"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** that Tools › Data's record screens and the grid offer these entities (they use `is_generic_write_refused`, which does not name them); a generic write was not driven; whether the pool's database role may run, for example, `COPY ... TO PROGRAM`. Looked for and not found: `databasejob` in `crates/moqui-entity/src` and `crates/moqui-core/src` outside tests; a hash or signature of the plan on the job row (`DatabaseEntities.xml:24-47`); a check in `run_claimed` that the statements are the ones `create_plan_job` stored (`jobs.rs:549-736`); an owner check (`userId` is read only for the notification, `jobs.rs:740`).
- **Suggested fix:** Add `databasejob` and `databasejobstatement` to `AUDIT_ENTITY_KEYS` (only `jobs.rs` reads and writes them, with authorization off already), and have `run#DatabaseJob` refuse a job whose statements do not match a digest `create_plan_job` stores on the job row.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `jobs.rs:641-642` (the row's `statementText` is run) and `:852-858` (`conn.execute(sql, &[])`), and `sensitive.rs:46-119`: no job or statement entity is in any list. Confirmed for the run. Not read by the lead: `call.rs:1534`; a generic write was not driven.

#### SEC-TLS-89. An ETL entity source and an ETL lookup answer encrypted fields in clear

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/source.rs:236-279`, `:345-359`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/transform.rs:324-373`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/service.rs:764-767`; `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/mapping.rs:353-425`
- **Reached by:** a holder of all actions on the Tools app (`ADMIN`), with no sealed permission: `Tools/Etl.xml` `etlCheck` with an inline mapping whose `sourceTypeEnumId` is `EtlsEntity`, or a run that loads the rows into an entity of the caller's choice, or a field with `lookupEntityName` and `lookupResultField`.

The other generic tools hide them: the grid masks an encrypted value, and Export writes none without the sealed `ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD` (SEC-TLS-05). `encrypt` does not occur in `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/`.
- **Evidence:** `source.rs:261-268` `let mut values: BTreeMap<String, Value> = value.fields().iter().filter(|(name, _)| { !super::access::is_secret_field(name) && !self.secrets.iter().any(|secret| **secret == ***name) })`
- **Evidence:** `service.rs:764-767` `map.insert( "source".to_string(), Value::Map(row.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.clone(), v.clone())).collect()), );`; `transform.rs:355-356` `.map(|row| match &result_field { Some(result) => row.get(result).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null),`
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/field_rw.rs:132-134` `pub fn after_read(...) -> Result<FieldMap, Error> { decrypt_fields(facade, entity, values) }`; compare `crates/moqui-tools/src/grid.rs:1094` `Some(value) if field.encrypt && !matches!(value, Value::Null) => Value::string(MASK),`
- **Evidence:** `sensitive.rs:17` "or encrypted, which no generic answer, condition, order or export holds."; `:154-159` `is_restricted_generic_entity` (identity-admin, secret-config, audit, host-config)
- **Not confirmed:** the call from `list()` to the facade's decryption was not traced. That every entity above is open to the caller's entity authorization under the Tools frame (`access.rs:262-272` pushes the Tools screen for all actions, which `ADMIN` holds as inheritable). Whether an ETL source condition or `matchFields` on an encrypted field gives an oracle. Looked for and not found: `encrypt` in `etl/`; a call of `sensitive::generic_access`, `conceal` or `encrypted_fields` from ETL (`access.rs:178-219` uses `is_restricted_generic_entity`, `is_restricted_entity_or_view` and `secret_fields`); a permission beyond Tools all actions for an entity source (`service.rs:95-109`: only a SQL source and an expression ask one).

- **Re-check:** The lead read `etl/source.rs:255-268`: a row's fields are filtered by `access::is_secret_field(name)` and the source's own `secrets` list, so a filter exists; the claim is that an `encrypt="true"` field whose name is not a secret name (`secretValue`, `targetToken`, `accessToken`, `apiKey`, `privateKeyPem`) passes it. Not read by the lead: `access.rs` (what the name rule and `secrets` cover), `transform.rs:348-369`, `service.rs:764-767`.

#### SEC-TLS-90. A schedule's transfer hands the new owner every definition it references, an alert rule included, active and unchecked

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-report/src/delivery.rs:2027-2062`; `crates/moqui-report/src/definitions.rs:230-261`; `crates/moqui-report/src/alert.rs:1431-1465`; compare `crates/moqui-report/src/alert_delivery.rs:1011-1039`
- **Reached by:** `transfer#DefinitionOwner`, called for one's own schedule by anyone who holds the service, or for another user's by a holder of `REPORT_OWNER_CHANGE` (`delivery.rs:1922-1930`). In mr-boss the identity operator and the administrator hold it and call it through the lifecycle participant when a user is offboarded (`BossSetupData.xml:4609-4621`). The one who gains is the rule's author: a report author whose schedules will be handed on, or who is offboarded.
- **What happens:** The transfer walks the schedule's definition and every `DefinitionReference` row below it and sets `ownerId` to the new owner on each one the leaving owner owns, whatever its type. `references` records any `definitionId`, `panelRef`, `datasetId` or `dashboardRef` key anywhere in a body, so a dashboard that holds `{"definitionId": "<an alert rule>"}` in a panel references the rule. Only a telemetry alias is held back (`hands_over`). The schedule is paused (`transfer-pending`); the rule is not, and its state and contact points are kept. From the next minute `evaluate_ReportAlerts_minute` signs in as the rule's `ownerId`, now the new owner, runs the leaving author's query under the new owner's dataset grants and row filters, and delivers the labels and values to the contact points the author wrote: an `email` contact's free `toAddresses`, or a `webhook` if the new owner holds `org.moqui.report.contact.webhook`. A business rule's recipients are judged again against the new owner's scope at each notification; a technical rule's contact points are not. `transfer#AlertRuleOwner` exists to prevent this: it pauses the rule until the new owner activates it and deletes its state.
- **Evidence:** `delivery.rs:2044-2051` `let owned = field(&row, "ownerId") == from && field(&row, "readOnly") != "Y"; let goes = owned && definitions::row_json(&row).is_ok_and(|definition| crate::alias::hands_over(ec, &definition)); if goes { row.set("ownerId", Value::string(to)); row.update(&facade)?;`
- **Evidence:** `delivery.rs:2053-2060` `for reference in facade.find(REFERENCE)?.eq("definitionId", Value::string(&next)).list()?.iter() { queue.push(field(reference, "referencedId").to_owned()); }`; `definitions.rs:234` `if matches!(key.as_str(), "definitionId" | "panelRef" | "datasetId" | "dashboardRef") {`
- **Evidence:** `alias.rs:97-100` `if definition["type"] != "dataset" || body["serviceName"].is_string() { return true; }`; `alert.rs:1440` `let owner = text(definition, "ownerId", "");` and `:1452` `moqui_core::user::internal_login_user(account.get("username")...)`
- **Evidence:** `alert_delivery.rs:1012-1016` `definitions::engine_revise(ec, id, "Handed to a new owner", &actor, |body| { body["paused"] = json!(true); body["pausedReason"] = json!("transfer-pending"); Some(to.to_owned()) })?;`
- **Not confirmed:** how mr-boss's offboarding service picks the new owner and whether it shows what moves; the answer's `definitionIds` lists what moved, after the fact (`delivery.rs:2082`). Looked for and not found: a type test in the walk, a pause of a moved alert rule, a check at evaluation that the owner saved the rule (`evaluate_rule` reads `ownerId` only). The label rules still hold (`alert.rs:135-172`, `:174-269`: terms buckets of keyword, long and boolean fields, 128 bytes a value, 500 series).
- **Suggested fix:** Move only `dashboard`, `report`, `panel`, `dataset` and `calcgrid` definitions with a schedule; leave an `alert` (and its `contactPoint` references) to `transfer#AlertRuleOwner`, or pause it with `transfer-pending` and clear its state as that service does.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `delivery.rs:2027-2062`: every definition reached through `REFERENCE` rows that the leaving owner owns is re-owned, with no test of its type. Confirmed for the walk. Not read by the lead: `transfer#AlertRuleOwner`'s pause, `alert.rs:1440-1452`.

#### SEC-TLS-91. A dataset's `serviceName` and `rowFilterService` make each viewer call a service the dataset's author chose, with parameters a dashboard's author chose

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-report/src/lib.rs:234-243`, `:312-343`, `:877-899`, `:2644-2655`
- **Reached by:** the holder of SEC-TLS-82 (dataset save: `ADMIN` in the seed, the steward group in mr-boss), together with anyone who may save a dashboard over that dataset and share it with a group of their own (`definitions.rs:466-475`). The victim is a signed-in user who opens the shared dashboard (`render#Dashboard`) or is sent it, or an alert or schedule owner whose rule reads it.
- **What happens:** A dataset body may name any service as `serviceName`. A query over it calls that service in process as the viewer, with the query's parameters, and a panel's saved query carries `parameters` its author wrote. The engine asks only that the viewer hold the service for `view` by an own grant, then calls it through `sync_checked`. If the service returns no `rows` or `frame` the engine raises its own error after the call returned; `dashboard_frames_filtered` turns a panel's error into a notice and goes on, so the service's effect stands and the render succeeds. A viewer with wide own grants (an administrator whose group holds services for all actions) who opens a shared dashboard therefore runs any service they hold, with the author's parameters. `rowFilterService` is the same with fixed parameters (`userId`, `datasetId`, `rowFilterParameters`). The code's comment knows a dataset carries authority ("Dataset/service definitions can control authority; viewers need an explicit administration grant", `definitions.rs:435`).
- **Evidence:** `lib.rs:234-235` `if let Some(service) = body["serviceName"].as_str() { moqui_core::user::authorize_own("AT_SERVICE", service, "view").map_err(fail)?;`
- **Evidence:** `lib.rs:884-887` `let mut parameters = match moqui_lib::json::from_json(&options["parameters"]) { Value::Map(map) => map.into_iter().collect(), _ => Parameters::new(), };`; `:894-899` `let result = moqui_core::service::call::sync_checked(service, &parameters, &moqui_core::service::call::CallOptions::default()).map_err(fail)?;`
- **Evidence:** `lib.rs:908-910` `let rows = result["rows"].as_array().ok_or_else(|| fail("report source service must return rows or a frame"))?;`; `:2651-2654` `Err(error) => { tracing::error!(panel=%id,error=%error,"report panel query failed"); notices.push(json!({"level":"error","complete":false,"message":error.message()})); }`
- **Not confirmed:** whether a service's writes commit when the engine's later error is turned into a notice (the service facade's transaction handling was not traced); which users hold services by own grants wide enough to matter. Looked for and not found: a list of services a dataset may name, a test that the service is read only, a check of `serviceName` or `rowFilterService` at save (none in `definitions.rs` or `alias.rs`; `business::check_admitted` refuses `serviceName` only for a business dataset, `business.rs:107-111`). The `view` check and `sync_checked` hold: they stop a viewer without an own grant of the service.
- **Suggested fix:** Let a dataset name only a service registered as a report source (as scope policies are registered, `business.rs:200-236`), or one whose verb is `get`, `find` or `run` and that the dataset's steward could not otherwise call; check both names in `save#Definition`.

#### SEC-TLS-92. A dataset steward rewrites the fields and conditions of any DataDocument that is in a data feed

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-report/src/business.rs:529-595`, `:679-719`, `:838-977`, `:981-1099`
- **Reached by:** a holder of `org.moqui.report.dataset` (update) and of `org.moqui.report.dataset.<id>` (update) for some id: `ADMIN` in the seed (`REPORT_DATASETS_ADMIN`); in mr-boss the group `BOSS_INSIGHTS_DATA` ("Insights dataset steward"), which holds `org.moqui.report.dataset` and the pattern `org\.moqui\.report\.dataset\.insights-.*` (`BossSetupData.xml:2626-2629`, `:3385`). Routes: `Dashboards/Datasets.xml:24-31` `createDataset`, `DatasetFields.xml:17-31` `storeFields` and `storeConditions`, `DatasetDetail.xml:39-44` `rebuildIndex`, and the same transitions taken in by mr-boss `screen/Insights/ReportBuilder/Datasets.xml:29`, `DatasetFields.xml:25-26` and `DatasetDetail.xml:29`.
- **What happens:** `create#BusinessDataset` takes `dataDocumentId` from the caller and checks only that the document exists and is in some feed; it does not ask whether the caller may read the document or whether it belongs to the caller's app. `store#BusinessDatasetFields` then adds, changes and deletes `DataDocumentField` rows of that document, and `store#BusinessDatasetConditions` its `DataDocumentCondition` rows, with authorization off. A new field is any relationship path of up to six names whose last name is not a secret name and is not an encrypted field. `index#BusinessDataset` rebuilds the document's index with authorization off. The document is shared with other datasets, the app's own search screens and the feed, so a steward of `insights-x` can add fields of related entities to another app's document, which every reader of that document then gets from the index; delete or rewrite any condition that narrowed it, a Tools-made one included (the rows it left out come back at the next index build); and delete fields other consumers use. The only refusal is for a field a report definition names (`consumers_naming`).
- **Evidence:** `business.rs:549-565` `if search::telemetry::is_telemetry_index(document) {` ... `if metadata(ec, || { entity(ec)?.find(DOCUMENT)?.eq("dataDocumentId", Value::string(document)).one() })?.is_none()` ... `if feeds_of(ec, document)?.is_empty() {` (the only checks on the document)
- **Evidence:** `business.rs:846-848` `steward_of(id)?;` / `let definition = business_definition(ec, id)?;` / `let document = text(&definition["body"], "documentType", "").to_owned();`; `:733-741` `fn plain_path(path: &str) -> bool {` ... `parts.len() <= 6 && parts.iter().all(|part| plain_name(part)) && parts.last().is_some_and(|field| !moqui_core::context::secret_parameter(field))`
- **Evidence:** `business.rs:898` `row.set("dataDocumentId", Value::string(&document));` and `:962` `None => row.delete(&facade)?,` inside `metadata(ec, || {` (authorization off); `:1077` `row.set("postQuery", Value::string("N"));`, `:1085` `None => row.delete(&facade)?,`
- **Evidence:** `business.rs:696-705` `let _system = ec.disable_authz();` / `moqui_core::service::call::sync_checked(INDEX_SERVICE, ...`; `runtime/components/base/tools/template/ReportDatasets.js:250` `dataDocumentId: p.dataDocumentId,`
- **Not confirmed:** which documents with sensitive relationships are in a feed in a real installation; whether a six-name path reaches a confidential entity from a document a steward would pick; whether the BOSS screen's own actions or the BOSS root's denials stop `createDataset` for a document outside Insights (only the `transition-include` lines were read); how the steward reads the widened index (the read still needs `DataDocumentUserGroup` for the viewer, `lib.rs:286-288`). Looked for and not found: a check that the document is one the steward's grant names, a `DataDocumentUserGroup` check at create or at the field edit (one is made only at read, `lib.rs:272-285`), a rule that a document under a business dataset is that dataset's alone, a guard against removing a field or condition another consumer needs. Found: the encrypted-field and secret-name refusals, the function list, the report-definition consumers check.
- **Suggested fix:** Let a steward edit only a document registered as the dataset's own (a `BusinessDataset` flag set when the document was made for it, or an update artifact `org.moqui.report.document.<dataDocumentId>` asked beside the dataset's), ask the creator's `DataDocumentUserGroup` membership in `create#BusinessDataset`, and refuse to remove or weaken a condition the steward's dataset did not add.

#### SEC-TLS-93. A CalcGrid author's `clear`, `clearFormatting` or `style` edit over a huge area runs without limit, under locks every CalcGrid call waits on

Medium; reported (confirmed in code).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-calc/src/room.rs:503-526`, `:652-671`, `:767-773`, `:835-837`, `:1184-1206`; `crates/moqui-calc/src/service.rs:1185-1213`; IronCalc at the pinned revision, `base/src/user_model/common.rs:893-907`, `:1087-1136`, `:1853-1865`
- **Reached by:** a signed-in user who may edit a CalcGrid room: a holder of the Tools app, or of an access profile with `CALC_GRID_AUTHOR` (`service.rs:1179`). Through `Dashboards/CalcGrid/calcEdit` (`opsJson`) or the Tools data grid's calc transition (`Tools/Data/Grid.xml:324`), to `edit#CalcDocument`.
- **What happens:** An edit that writes cells is held to 100,000 cells before it is applied. An edit that does not write (`clear`, `clearFormatting`, `style`) takes its snapshot with `writes` false, where the only bound is on the part of the area inside the sheet's used area; on an empty sheet (after `addSheet`) or beyond the used area the snapshot returns at once, and the edit is applied with the request's `width` and `height`, each up to `i32::MAX`. The engine walks every cell of the area: `range_clear_contents` pushes one value per cell into a vector before it clears, `update_range_style` makes a style entry per cell, `range_clear_formatting` reads each. An area of 16,000 by 1,000,000 is 1.6e10 cells: the process runs out of memory or the worker never returns. The edit holds the room's mutex for its whole run (for a saved document also the head row's `FOR UPDATE` lock). Every `Rooms::get`, `join` and `open` first runs `sweep`, which takes each room's mutex while holding the map's, so the next CalcGrid call blocks on the busy room with the map locked and every later one blocks on the map, each holding a request worker.
- **Evidence:** `room.rs:513-514` `if writes {` / `if i64::from(width.max(1)) * i64::from(height.max(1)) > MAX_EDIT_CELLS {`; `:521-526` `let (max_row, max_column) = self.used(sheet);` ... `if last_row < row || last_column < column {` / `return Ok(edits);`
- **Evidence:** `room.rs:662` `self.snapshot(sheet, row, column, width, height, op["op"] == "restore")?` (false for `clear` and `clearFormatting`); `:669` `edits.extend(self.snapshot(sheet, row, column, width, height, false)?);` (`style`); `:771-772` `width: small("width", 1).max(1),` / `height: small("height", 1).max(1),`
- **Evidence:** `room.rs:835-837` `"clear" => self.model.range_clear_contents(&area()),` / `"clearFormatting" => self.model.range_clear_formatting(&area()),` / `"style" => self.model.update_range_style(&area(), &text("path"), &text("value")),`
- **Evidence:** IronCalc `common.rs:897-899` `for row in range.row..range.row + range.height {` / `let mut data_row = Vec::new();` / `for column in range.column..range.column + range.width {`, then `:906` `data_row.push(old_value);`; `:1853-1855` the same two loops around `self.update_single_cell_style(`
- **Evidence:** `service.rs:1193` `let mut held = moqui_lib::sync::lock(&room);`; `room.rs:1184-1189` `fn sweep(rooms: &mut RoomMap) {` / `rooms.retain(|_, room| {` / `let mut room = lock(room);`; `:1196-1197` `let mut rooms = lock(&self.rooms);` / `Self::sweep(&mut rooms);`
- **Not confirmed:** not run, so which of memory or time gives first is not measured; whether the release build wraps or panics on `range.row + range.height` past `i32::MAX` (an area below that needs neither). Looked for and not found: a bound on `width` times `height` in `apply` or `edit_calc_document` (only `MAX_EDIT_OPS`, 2,000 edits a call, `service.rs:1189`); a bound in the engine for the general case (`insert_rows` checks `LAST_ROW`; these three do not); the engine's history being off (`room.rs:67`) does not skip the `old_value` loop; a time limit on a request worker.
- **Suggested fix:** In `Room::apply` refuse any edit whose `width` times `height` is over `MAX_EDIT_CELLS` unless it is a whole-column or whole-row area the engine handles apart, and clamp an area to the sheet's limits. Take the rooms map's lock without taking each room's (keep the last-used time outside the room's mutex, or `try_lock` in `sweep`).

#### SEC-TLS-94. A CalcGrid room's rows are read again under the caller's scope of the moment, so a room made for one scope can come to hold another scope's rows

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-calc/src/service.rs:550-570` (`room_of`), `:849-873` (`refresh_calc_document`), `:1004-1011` (the save of a scratch room), `:1262-1277` (`get_calc_frame`); `crates/moqui-calc/src/room.rs:1211-1222`
- **Reached by:** two holders of the same access profile on one saved document; the reader of the room gains, not the user whose scope changes. In mr-boss the profile's grant carries `MANTLE_USER_ORG` and `MANTLE_ACTIVE_ORG` (`runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/data/BossSetupData.xml:148-149`), and the scope follows the user's own choice of active organization (mr-mantle `service/mantle/party/PartyServices.xml:625` `<set field="activeOrgId" from="ec.user.getPreference('ACTIVE_ORGANIZATION')"/>`, `:645` `<set field="filterOrgIds" from="activeOrgId ? [activeOrgId] : userOrgIds"/>`).
- **What happens:** A room's id is a hash of the document, the profile and the opener's scope fingerprint, computed once in `open_document_with`. After that a call finds the room by membership and profile id only. `refresh#CalcDocument` reads the data sheets as the caller, with the caller's entity filters as they are now, and replaces the room's rows for every member. `get#CalcFrame` (a dashboard panel, a report) finds any room of the document the caller is a member of and, when its data is over a minute old, does the same. So when A (organizations X and Y) opens a document with X active, shares the room with B (X only), then changes the active organization and refreshes, or a dashboard panel of A's reads a range, B's next `get#CalcCells` answers Y's rows. A stays a member for 20 minutes after the last use (`room.rs:45`). The save of a scratch room has the same gap the other way round: its rows were read at the open, its shared id is made from the saver's fingerprint at the save, and readers of that fingerprint then join it.
- **Evidence:** `service.rs:554-556` `let room = rooms` / `.get(&user_id, &id)` / `.filter(|room| moqui_lib::sync::lock(room).profile_id == access.profile_id());`
- **Evidence:** `service.rs:858` `let rows = match read_sheets(facade, &sheets, &access, true) {`, `:864` `match room.refresh(rows) {`; `:1262` `let room = match rooms.find_document(&user_id, &definition_id, profile_id.as_deref()) {`, `:1269` `let rows = match read_sheets(facade, &sheets, &access, true) {`, `:1273` `if let Err(err) = moqui_lib::sync::lock(&room).refresh(rows) {`
- **Evidence:** `room.rs:1218-1220` `room.is_member(user_id)` / `&& room.definition_id.as_deref() == Some(definition_id)` / `&& room.profile_id.as_deref() == profile_id`
- **Evidence:** `service.rs:1008-1011` `let scope = crate::scope::fingerprint(facade, &sheets, &access);` / `let shared_id = crate::room::document_room_id(&id, access.profile_id().as_deref(), &scope);` ... `Ok(seq) => rooms.make_shared(&scratch_id, &shared_id, seq),`
- **Not confirmed:** nothing was run. When a request sets `filterOrgIds` again after the preference changes (the app root screens call `setup#UserOrganizationInfo`; `profile.rs:298-305` calls it only when the key is missing); whether two users' fingerprints are equal in the mr-boss seed for a user with one organization and a user with that one active (both filter sets are in the condition). Other causes of a scope change within 20 minutes (a party relationship ended, a group membership, a time zone) take the same path but need an administrator's act. Looked for and not found: a second fingerprint in `room_of`, `refresh_calc_document`, `get_calc_frame` or `Room::refresh` (`scope::fingerprint` is called at `service.rs:748`, `:1008` and `:1336` only); a member dropped when their scope changes. The profile is checked on each call (`Access::of`), which does not cover the entity filters' values.
- **Suggested fix:** In `refresh_calc_document`, in the stale branch of `get_calc_frame` and before `make_shared`, compute the caller's fingerprint and compare `document_room_id` with the room's id; when it differs, take the caller out of the room and open the room of their scope (for a scratch save, read the rows again before sharing). Or do it in `room_of` for every call of a shared room.

#### SEC-TLS-95. A co-editing update with deeply nested elements overflows the stack when the server writes the page's markdown, which ends the process

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-collab/src/markdown.rs:572-622`, `:648-713`, `:800-811`; `crates/moqui-collab/src/lib.rs:512`, `:632-650`, `:675-677`; `crates/moqui-host/src/notws/collab.rs:204-228`
- **Reached by:** a signed-in user with update access to one wiki page, over `/notws`: `{collab: "join"}` then `{collab: "update", update: <base64 Yjs update>}`.
- **What happens:** `apply` takes any valid Yjs update from an editor, so the document's `default` fragment may hold elements nested to any depth (a browser editor would not make them; a raw update can). About 1.5 seconds later the room's tick calls `write_row`, which calls `markdown::render`. `render_blocks_in` calls `render_block`, which for a `blockquote`, a list item or an unknown tag calls `render_blocks` again with no depth count; `render_inlines` recurses the same way. The tick runs on a request worker with a 2 MiB stack in a release build, and a stack overflow in Rust is not a panic: the process aborts. An update may be up to 16 MiB (`notws.rs:264`), room for several hundred thousand nested elements. The state is not yet stored when the render runs, so the server comes back clean and the editor can do it again. `get#CollabDocument` renders the open room too (`service.rs:83`), so on another cluster member that took the update a viewer's read does the same.
- **Evidence:** `lib.rs:640-643` `let update = Update::decode_v1(update)...;` / `room.doc` / `.transact_mut()` / `.apply_update(update)`; `:512` `let snapshot = markdown::render(doc);`
- **Evidence:** `markdown.rs:655-657` `"blockquote" => {` / `let mut inner = String::new();` / `render_blocks(txn, node, "> ", &mut inner);`; `:703-705` `_ => {` / `let mut inner = String::new();` / `render_blocks(txn, node, "", &mut inner);`; `:806` `XmlOut::Element(element) => out.push_str(&render_inlines(txn, &element, false)),`
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-core/src/service/pool.rs:60` `const WORKER_STACK: usize = if cfg!(debug_assertions) { 8 } else { 2 } * 1024 * 1024;`
- **Not confirmed:** not run; the stack one level takes, so the depth that overflows, was not measured; whether `yrs` 0.28.1 itself refuses or recurses on such an update when it decodes or integrates it; the same question for `markdown::seed` over a page text with deep quotes (`write_blocks` recurses too, `markdown.rs:447`, but that text is what an editor may save anyway). Looked for and not found: a depth limit in `render_blocks_in`, `render_block`, `render_inlines` or `apply`; a check of the document's shape after an update (any element name and nesting is kept, `markdown.rs:702`); a `catch_unwind` around the tick (it would not catch an overflow).
- **Suggested fix:** Carry a depth in `render_blocks_in`, `render_block` and `render_inlines` and stop at a small number (32), writing the rest as text; refuse in `apply` an update that leaves the fragment deeper than that, or walk the tree with an explicit stack.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `moqui-collab/src/markdown.rs:572-590`: `render_blocks_in` recurses into children with no depth parameter. The lead's note names no other line read; the stack a level takes was not measured.

## LLM, A2A, MCP and Assist

#### SEC-AI-01. A room member approves a write that runs as another user

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `approval::decide_in` lets a room member who is not the requester approve only a write that member may make, checked as the decider (`decider_may_write` under `user::as_user`: a screen write's path, a service's own permission); another system's tool, an add-on tool or a change set only its requester approves. A member's rejection is unchanged; the write runs as the requester, and the approval keeps both users. Tests: `approval::tests::a_member_approves_only_a_write_they_may_make_themselves`, `members_tests::an_agent_member_replies_in_the_thread_and_a_room_member_approves_its_card`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-llm/src/approval/mod.rs:1129-1135`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/chat.rs:1085-1111`, `:1117-1170`
- **Reached by:** any signed-in user who may post in a chat room that has an agent member, through `decide#ChatCard` (the `chatAssistCard` transition) on an approval card another member's run posted.
- **What happens:** A run started from a room message runs as the sender. Its approval card is a room message. Any human member who may post can press Approve, and the write then runs with the sender's permissions. The earlier messages of the thread reach the model (wrapped as untrusted), so a member can also steer what gets proposed. A low-privilege member can thus approve a write an administrator's request produced, without the administrator seeing the card.
- **Evidence:** `approval/mod.rs:1131-1133` `// answered question 12: a chat run's card is decided by any human who may post in its room` / `None if decider.channel == Channel::Chat => { crate::chat::room_approval(facade, approval_id, &decider.user_id)?...`; `chat.rs:1106-1111` `let can_post = ...; Ok(can_post.then_some(view))`; `chat.rs:36-37` "an agent member, or the chat or run-as user of any agent, never decides. The write still runs as the requester."
- **Not confirmed:** how the room agent is added in practice (no web caller passes `agentRef`, see E); whether the card shows the full arguments to the deciding member; `docs/ai/Assist.md` states this as a decision (not compared).
- **Suggested fix:** let only the requester approve, or require that the decider also passes `user_permitted` for the write and record both; at least show the requester a notice before the write runs.

#### SEC-AI-02. An OpenUI Button runs a model-written write on one click, showing only the transition's name

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: a canvas Mutation click (`assist/canvas.rs` `mutation`) holds the write and answers its method, path, transition, parameters and hash, running nothing; it runs only when a second click, with a new nonce, confirms that hash, so a write the model or the values changed has another hash. The client (`WebrootAssist.js` `canvasAction`, `confirmCanvasWrite`) shows the transition, the path and every parameter before the user confirms; a Vue SFC canvas posts through the same path. Test: moqui-host it `assist::the_canvas_over_http_on_postgres`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-llm/src/assist/canvas.rs:316-371`; `crates/moqui-llm/src/ui/write.rs:157-240`; `js/WebrootAssistCanvas.js:740-750`
- **Reached by:** a signed-in `AgentUse` user who clicks a Button on a canvas the seed `assist` agent drew (OpenUI is on by default on the drawer). The program is the model's, so text the model read (a record, a mail, a page) can shape it.
- **What happens:** The model writes the Button's label and the Mutation behind it: a POST to any `/apps` transition with arguments that may be literals in the program. A click arms a nonce and the server runs the transition as the user, with no approval card. Beside the label the page shows "saves:" and the last path segment only; the parameters are not shown. A Button labelled "Show details" bound to a `delete...` or `update...` transition runs it.
- **Evidence:** `ui/write.rs:21-23` "A click on a Button the framework drew ... the click is the approval, so no card is asked for"; `canvas.rs:332-335` (only `canvas.data["mutations"]` membership), `:343-355` `ui::write::hold(...)` then `ui::write::execute(...)`; `WebrootAssistCanvas.js:349-352` `canvas.savesText = (path) => { ... return bare ? bare.slice(bare.lastIndexOf("/") + 1) : ""; }`, `:749` `"saves: " + saves.map(canvas.savesText).join(", ")`.
- **Not confirmed:** what the OpenUI validator (`ui/openui.rs`, not read) refuses in a Mutation; the user and role-group check `check_write` was read only as far as its call (`write.rs:238`).
- **Suggested fix:** on a Mutation click show the held write (path and parameters, as an approval card does) before it runs, or treat a Mutation whose arguments are not all bound to visible inputs as a card.

#### SEC-AI-81. A Vue SFC canvas can send what it read to another site by navigating its own frame

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-llm/src/ui/vue_sfc.rs:1295-1301`, `:560`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootAssistSandbox.js:24-25`, `:219-225`; `js/WebrootAssistCanvas.js:1160-1164`, `:1318-1320`; `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:885`
- **Reached by:** text an attacker planted where a run reads it (a record, a mail, a page, a file), on an agent whose `canvasDialects` name `vue-sfc` (an administrator's choice; the seed `assist` agent does not), for any `AgentUse` user of that agent on the drawer. No click is needed, and the "Other sites" preference is off.
- **What happens:** The model writes the component and its `<reads>` list. In the frame the component reads those `/apps` lists as the user through `this.$bridge.get` (up to 500 rows a read), then sets the frame's own location to `https://attacker/…?d=<data>`. `sandbox="allow-scripts"` does not stop a frame navigating itself, the frame's policy has no directive that governs navigation, and the page that holds the frame sends no `frame-src`, so the browser makes the request. The parent closes the frame when it sees the next `load` or the `leaving` message, which is after the request left. The server's name scan does not stop it: `this.$el.getRootNode().location.href = url` has no banned free name, because `location` after a dot is a property, which the scan skips. SEC-AI-05 records the image route, which needs "Other sites" on and has reads off; this route needs neither. A second route is likely: the runtime deletes `RTCPeerConnection` from the frame's window, but a new `<iframe>` with no `src` has its own window with the constructor.
- **Evidence:** `WebrootAssistSandbox.js:24-25` "The component's own code cannot be stopped from navigating the frame in every way; when it does, "leaving" goes out and the parent's watchdog closes the frame on its next load and records it."
- **Evidence:** `vue_sfc.rs:1296-1300` `"sandbox allow-scripts; default-src 'none'; ... connect-src 'none'; media-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; frame-src 'none'; child-src 'none'; worker-src 'none'; manifest-src 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'self' {origin}; base-uri 'none'"`
- **Evidence:** `MoquiDefaultConf.xml:885` `<response-header type="screen-render" name="Content-Security-Policy" value="frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self';"/>`; `docs/ai/Constitution.md:110` "The app's screen pages send no `frame-src`"
- **Evidence:** `WebrootAssistCanvas.js:1162-1163` `if (this.dog.loaded(Date.now()) === "navigation") { this.kill("navigation", "the canvas navigated its frame away, so it was closed");`; `:1318` `kids.push(h("iframe", { key: this.frameKey, ref: "frame", sandbox: "allow-scripts", referrerpolicy: "no-referrer", allow: "", title: "Canvas",`
- **Evidence:** `vue_sfc.rs:550` `let property = before == Some('.');`; `:560` `} else if !template && !property && !key && BANNED_GLOBALS.contains(&name.as_str()) {`
- **Evidence:** `WebrootAssistSandbox.js:221-223` `["RTCPeerConnection", "webkitRTCPeerConnection", ...].forEach((name) => { try { delete window[name]; } ...`
- **Evidence:** `docs/ai/Assist.md:1062-1063` "(their combination is the only way an image of another site could carry a read's data out)": true of images; the frame's navigation is another way.
- **Not confirmed:** not run in a browser. No `navigate-to` or other navigation limit was found in the frame policy, no `frame-src` or `child-src` on the screen pages, no `csp` attribute on the iframe, and no server check that refuses `getRootNode`, `location` as a property or a computed name. That a browser applies the parent's `frame-src` to a navigation the child frame starts, which the suggested fix rests on, is from the CSP specification, not from a test. The `about:blank` WebRTC route and a `dns-prefetch` link were not tested.
- **Suggested fix:** send `frame-src 'self'` (or the list of frames the application uses) on screen renders, at least on the pages that hold the drawer, so the canvas frame cannot leave the site; keep the watchdog. Remove WebRTC by a `Permissions-Policy` or CSP `webrtc 'block'` where supported, not only by deleting the names. Until then say in the agent's `canvasDialects` help that a Vue SFC canvas can send out what it reads.
- **Re-check:** Read `ui/vue_sfc.rs:1294-1301` (the frame's CSP has `connect-src 'none'`, `form-action 'none'`, and no `navigate-to`), `WebrootAssistSandbox.js:18-26` (its own comment: the component's code "cannot be stopped from navigating the frame in every way") and `WebrootAssistCanvas.js:1156-1164` (the watchdog closes the frame on its next load, after the request has gone). A known limit of the design, wider than SEC-AI-05. Not run.

#### SEC-AI-82. On a dev instance a `screen-xml` canvas runs model-written script in the application's origin

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-llm/src/ui/screen_xml.rs:48-66`, `:102-153`, `:169-208`; `js/WebrootAssistCanvas.js:1361-1373`, `:1389-1390`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:790-796`
- **Reached by:** text planted where a run reads it, on an instance with `instance_purpose=dev`, for a user with a dev-aide permission (`ADMIN` holds `DEV_AIDE` by its seed) talking to an agent whose policy pins a dev tool and whose `canvasDialects` name `screen-xml`. The path is `write_ui` with `kind: "screen-xml"`; the canvas opens by itself, with no approval and no click.
- **What happens:** Two things, either of which is enough. First, the program's first line names the page that shows it, `path="…"`. The server takes that attribute as it is (it checks only that `at` starts with `component://`), and the client uses it as the `src` of an iframe that has no `sandbox` attribute. `path="javascript:…//"` therefore runs in an `about:blank` frame of the application's origin (the screen policy has no `script-src`), with the page's session token within reach through `parent`. `path="https://other.site/…"` shows another site's page inside the drawer under "Preview of … · not saved". Second, with an honest `/qapps/…` path the frame loads that page with `moquiPreview=canvas:<id>`, and the server renders the model's XML standing at the named location. Nothing replaces the screen's client code or its server actions: the overlay only swaps the file's text, and the render runs under the entity fence. So a `<render-mode><text type="html">` with a `<script>`, or a `.qvt` template, runs in the application's origin, and the screen's `<actions>` run on the server with `ec` (reads with authorization off are possible; writes, outbound calls and processes are refused by the fence). The design says otherwise (`plans/design-dev-aide.md:1313-1314`, `:1402`). Script in the origin can post any transition as the user with the session token, so the approval that every other agent write waits for is passed by.
- **Evidence:** `screen_xml.rs:62-66` `let location = attribute("at"); (location.starts_with("component://")).then(|| Header { location, path: attribute("path"), })`; `:121` `let Some(header) = header(program) else {`; `:147-152` `Ok(Prepared { program: program.to_string(), statements: vec![header.location], ...` (the path is never looked at)
- **Evidence:** `WebrootAssistCanvas.js:1367` `canvas.previewUrl = (path, token) => path ? path + (path.indexOf("?") >= 0 ? "&" : "?") + "moquiPreview=" + encodeURIComponent(token) : "";`; `:1373` `src() { return this.header.path ? canvas.previewUrl(this.header.path, "canvas:" + this.artifactId) + "&v=" + (this.version || 0) : ""; }`; `:1389` `if (this.src) kids.push(h("iframe", { src: this.src, title: "Screen preview", class: "m-assist-screen-xml-frame",`
- **Evidence:** `render.rs:790-796` `if let Some(overlay) = super::overlay::for_token(factory, &token) { web::set_header("Content-Security-Policy", Some("frame-ancestors 'self'; form-action 'self';")); let _fence = crate::entity::fence::enter("a preview of proposed screen text"); return super::overlay::with(overlay, run); }`
- **Evidence:** `screen_xml.rs:205-206` `let header = header(&canvas.program)?; overlay.insert_location(factory, &header.location, &canvas.program);`
- **Evidence:** `plans/design-dev-aide.md:1313-1314` "`render-mode text` of client code and any script as placeholders"; `:1402` "the dev dialects' previews run under the read fence and draw no client code"
- **Not confirmed:** not run in a browser. No check of `path` was found on the server (`prepare`) or the client (`screenXmlHeader`, `src`), where every other canvas path goes through `safePath` or `plain_app_path`; no `sandbox` on the preview iframe; no render mode that replaces script and client text while an overlay is held (`overlay::active()` is read only by the caches, the URL builder and the screen-extend lookup); `at` may name any `component://` location of a loaded component. `moqui_devkit::check::check_text` was not read; it may refuse some screen content, but the `javascript:` path does not depend on the screen's content. Whether the dev agent's seed names `screen-xml` in `canvasDialects` is from `plans/design-dev-aide.md:1230`, not the seed file.
- **Suggested fix:** take `path` only when it passes the path check the other dialects use (an `/apps` or `/qapps` screen path, `screen::check_screen_path`), on the server in `prepare` and again in the client before it is a `src`. Give the preview iframe `sandbox` (no `allow-same-origin`) or serve the preview with a policy that has no script. Render a preview with client text and scripts as placeholders, as the design says, or make showing a `screen-xml` canvas an approval.
- **Re-check:** Confirmed for the lines. Read `ui/screen_xml.rs:47-66` (`header_named` checks only that `at` starts with `component://`; `path` is taken as written) and `WebrootAssistCanvas.js:1362-1373`, `:1389` (`src` is `path` plus the preview token, set on an `iframe` with no `sandbox`). Not read: `render.rs:790-796` (what the preview token renders), that the dialect is dev-only.

#### SEC-AI-83. Evals › Runs shows every user's prompts and replies to an `AgentAdmin` without `AgentAudit`, and logs nothing

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/System/Llm/Evals/Runs.xml:16-44`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/agent/AgentEval.js:26-31`, `:61-64`, `:34-40`
- **Reached by:** a holder of `AgentAdmin` (the seed `ADMIN` group, which does not hold `AgentAudit`), with `GET /apps/system/Llm/Evals/Runs` (optionally `runId=` or `agentId=`). With `MONITOR_EVAL` also the `saveRun` and `scoreRun` transitions.
- **What happens:** The screen's actions read `AgentMessage` and `AgentMessagePart` with authorization off for the 50 newest runs (or one run by id) of any user and put the text in the list: the first 300 characters of the user message and 500 of the reply. `save#RunAsDatasetItem` copies a run's whole user message, and with `useOutput=Y` its whole reply, into a dataset item that Dataset detail and Experiment detail show. `score#Run` with a judge scorer sends both to the model and stores the model's reasoning in `AgentScore.reason`. The content path the map describes (`get#LlmCallContent`, `get#AgentAuditView`) needs `AgentAudit` and writes an `AgentAuditLog` row; none of these does either.
- **Evidence:** `Runs.xml:19` `const find = (n, c) => ec.entity.find(n).condition(c).disableAuthz()`; `:37` `const msg = Array.from(find('moqui.agent.AgentMessage', {runId: r.runId, role: 'user'}).orderBy('messageSeq').limit(1).list())[0]`; `:39` `const input = msg ? Array.from(find('moqui.agent.AgentMessagePart', {messageId: msg.messageId})...map(p => p.textValue).join('\n') : ''`; `:42` `... cost: r.cost, input: clip(input, 300), output: clip(output, 500),`
- **Evidence:** `AgentEval.js:31` `return addItem({datasetId:p.datasetId,inputText:runInput(run.runId),groundTruth:p.groundTruth||(p.useOutput==='Y'?runOutput(run):null),...`
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-llm/src/services.rs:909-910` "`get#LlmCallContent`: ... for its own user, or for a user with AgentAudit, whose read is logged in `AgentAuditLog` (D12): AgentAdmin alone does not show another user's content."
- **Not confirmed:** `Monitor/AgentTraces`, which the screen links to (`Runs.xml:15`), was not read. The only permission check found in the screen's actions is `canEdit = ec.user.hasPermission('MONITOR_EVAL')` at `Runs.xml:17`, which gates the two forms, not the list; no `AgentAudit`, audit write or filter on the run's `userId` was found under `Evals/` or in `AgentEval.js`.
- **Suggested fix:** show the input and output columns, and allow `saveRun` and `scoreRun` on another user's run, only with `AgentAudit`, and write an `AgentAuditLog` row for each such read; or keep only the runs an experiment started (`startedByUserId`) readable without it.
- **Re-check:** Confirmed for the screen. Read `System/Llm/Evals/Runs.xml:16-44`: the actions find `AgentRun`, `AgentMessage` and `AgentMessagePart` with `disableAuthz()` for any run (the 50 latest, or a `runId` from the request) and put the input and output text in the rows; the only permission read is `MONITOR_EVAL` for `canEdit`. Not read: which group the Evals screens' authorization needs, and that nothing logs the view.

## Git hosting and pipelines

#### SEC-GIT-03. A gzip request body is inflated without limit, and SSH has no request limit

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `serve_http` caps a gzip body's decoder at `max-request` (upload-pack) or `max-push-size` (receive-pack) (`server::limited::Capped`, an error past it), `serve_ssh` holds the whole conversation's input to the same limits, and upload-pack counts its request lines (`upload::LineBudget`: `fetch-max-lines`, 1,000,000, in `fetch-max-request-bytes`, 64 MiB), else `GitFetchRequestTooLarge`. Tests: `server::limited::tests` `a_gzip_body_and_ssh_input_are_held_to_max_request`, `an_upload_pack_request_is_held_to_fetch_max_lines`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-git/src/server/mod.rs:771-777`; `crates/moqui-host/src/git_http.rs:97-130`, `:221-224`; `crates/moqui-git/src/server/upload.rs:120-128`, `:538-560`, `:650-660`; `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/git.rs:77-92`
- **Reached by:** upload-pack: anyone who may read a repository (anonymous where a repository is public, any account with view), `POST /git/<repo>/git-upload-pack` with `Content-Encoding: gzip`, or over SSH. Receive-pack: an account with a push right, over HTTP with gzip or over SSH.
- **What happens:** `max-request` (64 MiB) and `max-push-size` (2 GiB) count the body as sent. The server then wraps it in `GzDecoder` with no cap. Upload-pack keeps every `want`, `have`, `shallow` and unknown argument line in memory with no count, so a compressed body expands to many times the limit in memory. A push from an account with a push right writes the inflated pack to the quarantine directory with no total cap. Over SSH there is no byte limit on either service.
- **Evidence:**  - `server/mod.rs:773` `gunzip = flate2::read::GzDecoder::new(body);` - `git_http.rs:113` `total += data.len() as u64;` (frames of the raw body) - `upload.rs:123` `self.wants.push(parse_id(parts.next().unwrap_or_default())?);`; `:127` `self.deepen.client_shallow.push(parse_id(rest)?);`; `:552` `arguments.push(line);`; `:654` `haves.push(parse_id(rest)?);`; `:659` `request.caps.insert(argument.clone());` - `server/mod.rs:1161` `let received = pack_in::receive(store, &mut input, &dir, max_object_size(), pack_threads());` (no `take` unless the account has no push right) - `ssh/git.rs:82` `match reader.read(&mut buf).await {`
- **Not confirmed:** guards looked for and not found: a `take` on the decoder, a line count in `upload.rs`, a counter in `ssh/git.rs`. The job token's fetch is bounded (`job_fetch.rs:212`). A push's commands are bounded (`receive.rs:117-118`).
- **Suggested fix:** wrap the gunzipped reader in a `take` of the same limit and apply `max-request` and `max-push-size` in `serve_ssh`. Bound the count and bytes of upload-pack request lines as `RequestLimits` does for a push.

#### SEC-GIT-04. A viewer reads a whole blob or LFS object into server memory through REST

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `get#GitBlob` reads the file's size first (`GitProvider::file_at`, an LFS file's content counted) and answers a file over `git-server` `blob-max-bytes` (16 MiB) with its size and `tooLarge` only; `commit_diffs` diffs the first `commit-max-diff-files` (300) and lists the rest as too large; `get#GitLog` with a path compares at most `log-max-commits` (10,000) commits a page. Test: `service::read_limits_tests` `a_viewers_reads_are_held_to_their_limits`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-git/src/service/mod.rs:605`; `crates/moqui-git/src/browse.rs:290`; `crates/moqui-git/src/lfs_files.rs:134-141`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/resource/source.rs:246`; `crates/moqui-git/src/service/rest.rs:571-576`
- **Reached by:** any signed-in user who may view a repository, `GET /rest/s1/moqui/git/{repositoryId}/blob?path=...`; also `.../commits/{commitId}/diff` and `.../commits?path=...`.
- **What happens:** `get#GitBlob` reads the file whole, an LFS file as its content, and the REST base64-encodes it into the JSON answer. What can be stored is limited by `max-object-size` (512 MiB) and `lfs-max-object-size` (5 GiB), so one request holds up to that, plus the base64 copy, in memory. `get#GitCommitDiff` and `get#GitCommit` diff every file a commit changed with no file cap (compare has one, 300), and `get#GitLog` with `path` walks history until 500 matches with no commit or time budget.
- **Evidence:**  - `service/mod.rs:605` `match provider.blob_at(&id, &revision, &path_segments(parameters, "path")) {` - `browse.rs:290` `Some((mode, blob)) if !mode.is_tree() => self.file_content(&repo, &blob).map(Some),` - `lfs_files.rs:136` `Some(info) => Ok(moqui_core::resource::source::read_all(` - `rest.rs:575` `Value::string(base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(bytes)),` - `browse.rs:268` `for change in diff::commit_changes(store, &ops::read_commit(store, &id)?)? {` against `crates/moqui-git/src/service/hosting.rs:39` `MAX_COMPARE_FILES: usize = 300;`
- **Not confirmed:** whether the REST layer caps a response, or the request deadline ends the work before memory is taken. No size parameter was found on `get#GitBlob`.
- **Suggested fix:** refuse a blob over a `git-server` limit in `get#GitBlob` (answer its size, serve large content by a streamed download), cap the files of a commit's diff, and give `log` with a path a commit budget.

#### SEC-GIT-05. A small pack of large or deeply chained objects costs far more than its bytes

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `pack_in::read_entries` reads each entry's header once, keeping kinds and depths by offset (the quadratic work of `check_closure` and `store_entries` is gone), and refuses a chain deeper than `max-delta-depth` (4095) as `GitDeltaTooDeep`; an account with no push right is held to `review-max-object-size` (32 MiB) and `review-max-delta-depth` (250), and its push counts the larger of its pack and inflated bytes toward `review-max-push-bytes` and `review-max-account-bytes`. `pack-threads` defaults to 2. Tests: `server::pack_in::tests` `a_deep_delta_chain_is_refused_and_inflated_bytes_are_counted`, `a_push_for_review_counts_its_inflated_bytes`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-git/src/server/pack_in.rs:158-171`, `:249`, `:327-344`; `crates/moqui-git/src/db.rs:822-833`; `crates/moqui-git/src/server/mod.rs:1155-1158`
- **Reached by:** any account that may view a repository, through a push to `refs/for/<branch>` (`review-push` is `true` by default); and any account with a push right.
- **What happens:** the caps on an account without a push right count pack bytes (`review-max-push-bytes` 50 MiB, `review-max-account-bytes` 200 MiB a day). Three costs are not bound by them. Memory: each object may inflate to `max-object-size` (512 MiB), and the indexer inflates on every core (`pack-threads` empty). CPU: there is no delta chain depth limit, and `check_closure` asks `kind_in_pack` for every object, which walks its chain to the base, so one chain of N small deltas costs about N²/2 entry reads on a git worker, with no time limit. Storage: in a `db` repository each object over 8 MiB is stored inflated as a content blob, so stored bytes can exceed pack bytes by the deflate ratio.
- **Evidence:**  - `pack_in.rs:169` `alloc_limit_bytes: max_object,` and `:166` `thread_limit: threads,` - `server/mod.rs:1155` `let cap: u64 = crate::number_setting("review-max-push-bytes", 50 * 1024 * 1024);` - `pack_in.rs:339-342` `bundle.pack.decode_header(entry, &mut cache.0, &|base| resolve_in(bundle, base))` - gix-pack 0.77.0 `data/file/decode/header.rs`: `OfsDelta { base_distance } => { num_deltas += 1;` (no depth bound found) - `db.rs:824-825` `let mut decoder = flate2::read::ZlibDecoder::new(&data[..]); std::io::copy(&mut decoder, &mut writer)`
- **Not confirmed:** the real cost per chain step; whether gix's index writer refuses very deep chains; whether the database compresses blob chunks. `review-max-refused` (30 an hour) counts refused pushes only.
- **Suggested fix:** refuse a pack whose delta chain is deeper than a configured limit, or cache the kind per offset in `check_closure`. Give `pack-threads` a small default. Count inflated bytes toward the review caps, and hold an account without a push right to a lower object size.

#### SEC-GIT-06. An SSH push mirror's server is read without size or total-time limits

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `push::push` reads an SSH mirror server's refs within `MAX_ADVERTISEMENT` (32 MiB) and its report within `MAX_REPORT` (1 MiB), as `smart_http` does (which now also refuses a report cut at its cap), and the host's `RusshExec` gives the whole command a 30-minute deadline and keeps at most 64 KiB of stderr. Test: `push_tests::a_servers_refs_and_report_are_read_within_their_caps`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-git/src/mirror.rs:396-411`; `crates/moqui-git/src/server/push.rs:115-149`, `:310-334`; `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/client.rs:321-348`
- **Reached by:** a user with the delete right on a repository (on a hosting server, the owner of any repository): `store#GitRepositoryMirror` with an `ssh://` URL and the fingerprint of a server they run; the push starts after every change and from the queue.
- **What happens:** the refs advertisement is read into a map until a flush packet arrives, stderr is appended to a buffer, and the report's lines are pushed to vectors, all with no byte cap. Each read has 120 seconds but the whole conversation has no limit. A server that streams refs without end grows the Moqui process's memory. One that sends a packet every minute holds a service-pool thread, and when run from the queue it holds the `run#GitRepositoryMirrors` pass, which pushes up to 500 due mirrors one after another.
- **Evidence:**  - `push.rs:144` `remote.refs.insert(name.to_string(), parse_id(id)?);` inside `loop { match pktline::read(input)…` - `client.rs:340` `Received::Stderr(data) => self.stderr.extend_from_slice(&data),`; `:327` `tokio::time::timeout(TIMEOUT, channel.wait())` (per read) - `mirror.rs:400` `let pushed = push::push(repo.store.as_ref(), refs, covered, &mut &io, &mut output);`; the HTTPS path caps the same reads at `smart_http.rs:195` and `:267`
- **Not confirmed:** the size of the service pool and whether a service-call timeout cuts an async `run#GitRepositoryMirrors` off. The mirror's lease is renewed while held, so it does not end the push.
- **Suggested fix:** wrap the SSH channel's reader in the same limits as HTTPS (advertisement, report, stderr) and give the conversation the 30-minute deadline.

#### SEC-GIT-07. Git services that trust their caller

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `export#GitRepository`, `fetch#GitRepository` and `push#GitMirror` need the delete right (`hosting::allowed`), and with authorization on export's `targetPath` is a relative path of safe segments under `runtime/git-export`; `init#GitRepository` with `contentRepository` asks the update right; `queue#GitWebhookPush`, `run#GitRepositoryMirrors`, `clean#GitUpdates`, `check#MergeRequest`, `sync#MergeRequest` and `refresh#MergeRequests` refuse a caller unless authorization is disabled, as `queue#PushRuns` does. Test: `service::trust_tests::services_that_trusted_their_caller_check_it`. Follow-up (2026-11-16, commit 283fdf2ec): `FileServices.export#Drive` of a git drive checks the view right on the drive itself (`get#DriveAccess` level 1; a records or folder drive still needs manage) and calls `export#GitRepository` with authorization disabled and `underExportDir`, which still holds `targetPath` under `runtime/git-export`, so a viewer of a read-only git drive exports it and one who may not view it is refused; a direct caller of `export#GitRepository` still needs the delete right. Test: `example/test/Files.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-git/src/service/mod.rs:168-181` (`fetch#GitRepository`), `:185-198` (`push#GitMirror`), `:230-250` (`init#GitRepository`), `:296-316` (`export#GitRepository`); `crates/moqui-git/src/service/hosting.rs:1453-1544` (`queue#GitWebhookPush`), `:1737-1783` (`send#GitWebhookDelivery`); `crates/moqui-git/src/service/mirror.rs:357-378` (`run#GitRepositoryMirrors`); `GitReviewServices.xml:879-997` (`check#MergeRequest`, `sync#MergeRequest`, `refresh#MergeRequests`); `GitServices.xml:468`
- **Reached by:** any caller that passes service authorization for them; the checks found are in screens only (the tools `System/Resource` screens, mr-boss `Settings.xml` with `ec.user.isInGroup('ADMIN')` in the transition script). `FileServices.export#Drive` forwards a drive manager's `targetPath` to `export#GitRepository`.
- **What happens:** `export#GitRepository` writes a bare repository at any path the server process can write, and into a repository already there: its branch ref is set to the exported head, past that repository's branch rules. `init#GitRepository` with `contentRepository` commits `.gitattributes` without the update right `setup#GitLfs` asks. `queue#GitWebhookPush` takes `userId`, `source` and `refUpdates` from its parameters and queues events signed with each webhook's secret, where `queue#GitWebhookEvent` beside it needs the update right. `run#GitRepositoryMirrors` is `anonymous-all` and with a `mirrorId` pushes that mirror at once, past its back-off. `clean#GitUpdates` is `authenticate="false"` and deletes the push log older than the caller's `daysToKeep`. The three merge request services take any `mergeRequestId`, a hidden one's too, call the inner services with authorization disabled, move its head and status, dismiss approvals and queue a `synchronize` event; `check#MergeRequest` answers `mergeableEnumId`.
