- **Evidence:**  - `lib.rs:1435` `Some(target) if Path::new(target).is_absolute() => PathBuf::from(target),`; `:1442` `let target = fs::FsStore::init(&path, &definition.branch)?;`; `:1456` `target.compare_and_set_ref(&name, current, head.id)` - `service/mod.rs:244-245` `if flag(parameters, "contentRepository") { if let Err(err) = provider.setup_lfs(&id, &crate::attributes::DEFAULT_LFS_PATTERNS) {` against `:261` `if let Err(err) = moqui_core::user::authorize_own(GIT_ARTIFACT_TYPE, &id, "update") {` - `service/hosting.rs:1454` `let Some((provider, id)) = provider_and_id(parameters, true) else {`; `:1476` `let user_id = text(parameters, "userId");`; `:1551` `if allowed(parameters, "update").is_none() { return none(); }` (the sibling) - `service/mirror.rs:361` `let only = text(parameters, "mirrorId");` - `GitReviewServices.xml:892` `target: mr.targetRef, checkOnly: true, includeHidden: true}).disableAuthz().call()`; `:943` `.disableAuthz().updateAll({statusId: 'GmrrRequested'})` - `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/resource/FileServices.xml:695-696` `.name('org.moqui.git.GitServices.export#GitRepository').parameters({repositoryId: drive.repositoryId, targetPath: targetPath || null})`
- **Not confirmed:** which routes let a request name a service. No JSON-RPC route was found, and no resource of `moqui.rest.xml` names these services. The System "service run" screen, the load runner and the agent tool exposure were not traced; they decide who reaches these, including the three that need no service authorization.
- **Suggested fix:** put the right in each service: the delete right on the repository for export, fetch and the older mirror; `targetPath` only with authorization disabled or under a fixed directory; refuse `queue#GitWebhookPush`, `run#GitRepositoryMirrors`, `clean#GitUpdates` and the three merge request services without authorization disabled, as `queue#PushRuns` is refused.

#### SEC-GIT-82. A fetch's `shallow` lines make the server send the ancestors of a commit the reader may not read, and say whether the store holds it

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-git/src/server/plan.rs:128-165`; `crates/moqui-git/src/server/upload.rs:127-128`, `:157-195`, `:295-309`, `:354`, `:490-496`, `:725`, `:752`; `crates/moqui-git/src/server/job_fetch.rs:55-81`
- **Reached by:** anyone who may fetch the repository: a signed-in user with view, a client with no credentials on a repository anyone may view, over smart HTTP (`POST /git/<path>/git-upload-pack`, v0 and v2) and SSH; and a CI job holding a job token (`server/job_fetch.rs`), which includes the untrusted job of a fork's merge request. The caller must know the id of the commit.
- **What happens:** a fetch request may carry `shallow <id>` lines, which are kept as `client_shallow` with no check. The wants are checked against the readable refs (`Request::check`), and only after that `Send::shallow` calls `plan::shallow`, which (with `deepen 2147483647`, or with `deepen-relative` and `deepen N`) treats each `client_shallow` id the store holds as a commit to unshallow: it answers `unshallow <id>` and pushes that commit's parents onto `wants`. `plan::plan` then walks those parents as wants. The store is the fork network's (one set of rows for a `db` network, alternates for a `local` one) and holds hidden merge requests and commits their refs have left, so a reader who knows the id of a commit they may not read (another fork's, a hidden merge request's, a force-pushed-away one) receives its parents and everything they reach, minus the haves. The commit itself is not sent (the client claimed to have it). With `deepen-relative` the answer's `shallow <id>` lines also name the commits N levels down. Separately, `unshallow <id>` is answered if and only if the store holds the id as a commit (an id it holds as another kind ends the fetch with an error, an id it lacks is passed over), which tells which ids a store holds. For the job token, `check_request` bounds `deepen` by `max-fetch-depth` but lets `shallow` and `deepen-relative` through, and the `only` closure that restricts wants to the job's commit runs before the wants are extended; the boundary ids of each answer let the next request go deeper. The reviewer rates it High if a known commit id counts as no barrier.
- **Evidence:**  - `server/upload.rs:127-128` `} else if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("shallow ") {` / `self.deepen.client_shallow.push(parse_id(rest)?);` - `server/upload.rs:490` `request.check(advertised, wanted)?;` then `:496` `let (info, grafts) = sender.shallow(&mut request)?;` (v2: `:725` and `:752`, the same order) - `server/upload.rs:296-297` `let info = if request.deepen.deepens() {` / `plan::shallow(self.store, &mut request.wants, &request.deepen)?` - `server/plan.rs:129-134` `let client: Vec<ObjectId> = deepen.client_shallow.iter().copied().filter(|id| store.object_size(id).ok().flatten().is_some()).collect();` - `server/plan.rs:135-137` `let (boundary, inside) = if deepen.depth == Some(INFINITE_DEPTH) {` / `(HashSet::new(), client.iter().copied().collect())` - `server/plan.rs:139-140` `if deepen.relative {` / `by_depth(store, &client, depth.saturating_add(1))?` - `server/plan.rs:154-160` `for id in &client { if inside.contains(id) { info.unshallow.push(*id); for parent in ops::read_commit(store, id)?.parents { if store.object_size(&parent)?.is_some() && !wants.contains(&parent) { wants.push(parent); } } } }` - `server/upload.rs:354` `let planned = plan::plan(self.store, &request.wants, haves, &info.unshallow, grafts, &tags)?;` and `:501-502` `for id in &info.unshallow { pktline::text(out, &format!("unshallow {id}"))` - `server/job_fetch.rs:61-73`: the only lines `check_request` looks at are `want `, `want-ref `, `deepen-since `, `deepen-not ` and `deepen `; `:195-196` `Ok(ids.iter().filter(|id| **id == commit).copied().collect())` is the `wanted` closure, used by `Request::check` and `Negotiation::have` only.
- **Not confirmed:** not run. How a reader comes by the id is outside the code: a former member of a private fork, a commit id in a log, a status or a message, the author of a hidden merge request. `Walk::limited` was not read whole (that a hidden graft commit does not hide its parents rests on `revwalk.rs:191` and the module comment of `plan.rs:18-19`). Looked for and not found: a readability check of `client_shallow` in `Request::argument`, `Request::check`, `Send::shallow`, `plan::shallow`, `plan::plan`; a second check of `wants` after `plan::shallow`; a restriction in `serve_http`, `serve_ssh` or `Session::wanted`; a `shallow` or `deepen-relative` refusal in `job_fetch::check_request`. A test that fetches with `shallow <hidden commit>` and `deepen 2147483647` from a fork of the same network would settle it, and the job-token variant with `deepen-relative`.
- **Suggested fix:** in `Request::check`, drop (or refuse) every `client_shallow` id that `wanted` does not answer as reachable, before `plan::shallow` sees it; an id that is not kept is neither unshallowed nor answered. In `plan::shallow` add a parent to `wants` only when it is reachable too. In `job_fetch::check_request` refuse `deepen-relative`, and a `shallow` line that is not the job's commit or an ancestor within the depth.
- **Re-check:** Confirmed for the order. Read `server/upload.rs:486-496` (`request.check(advertised, wanted)` runs, then `sender.shallow(&mut request)`), `:127-128` (a `shallow` line's id is taken as sent) and `server/plan.rs:128-165` (a client shallow id is kept if the store holds the object, with no reachability test, and with `deepen` its parents are pushed onto `wants`). Not read by the lead: which commits a store holds that a given reader may not read (hidden refs, a job token's ref scope `job_fetch.rs:55-81`, a shared store). The reviewer asks for a test to confirm.

#### SEC-GIT-83. One v0 upload-pack request can keep a git worker walking history for hours

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-git/src/server/upload.rs:246-282` (`ok_to_give_up`), `:567-576`, `:122-126`, `:409-447`
- **Reached by:** anyone who may fetch a repository (a client with no credentials on a repository anyone may view, within `anonymous-max-concurrent`), with protocol v0 over HTTP or SSH.
- **What happens:** in a v0 negotiation with `multi_ack`, every `have` that is not common calls `ok_to_give_up`, which, for each want not yet known to reach a common commit, walks the want's history back to the date of the oldest common have, with a fresh `seen` set each time and nothing kept between calls when the walk does not reach a have. Wants are a `Vec` with no deduplication. A request with one common have that is old and is no ancestor of the want (the tip of an old unmerged branch; or, where push to review is on, the caller's own merge request head with an author date of 1970), W copies of one want and H made-up haves costs about W x H walks of the whole history. The line budget allows a million lines in 64 MiB a round. The route is exempt from the request timeout, and `idle-timeout` watches only the body and a client that stops reading. A few such requests hold every worker of `max-concurrent` (4), which git, LFS and the runner REST share.
- **Evidence:**  - `server/upload.rs:567-569` `} else {` / `got_other = true;` / `if multi_ack != MultiAck::None && negotiation.ok_to_give_up()? {` - `server/upload.rs:250-275` `for want in self.wants.clone() { if self.satisfied.contains(&want) { continue; } ... let mut pending = vec![commit]; let mut seen = HashSet::new(); ... while let Some(id) = pending.pop() { ... let info = ops::read_commit(self.store, &id)?; if info.seconds < oldest { continue; } pending.extend(info.parents); } if !reached { return Ok(false); }` - `server/upload.rs:124` `self.wants.push(parse_id(parts.next().unwrap_or_default())?);` - `server/upload.rs:424-425` `most_lines: number("fetch-max-lines", 1_000_000),` / `most_bytes: number("fetch-max-request-bytes", 64 * 1024 * 1024),`
- **Not confirmed:** the loop was read; the cost was not measured. The real cost per commit read, and whether `DbStore` or gix caches make a walk cheap enough to matter less, were not read. v2 calls `ok_to_give_up` once a request (`:741`) and is not affected. Looked for and not found in `upload.rs`, `limited.rs`, `server/mod.rs` or `git_http.rs`: a cap on the calls of `ok_to_give_up`, a memo of a want that failed, a deduplication of wants, a deadline on a negotiation.
- **Suggested fix:** deduplicate wants; remember per want the answer for the current set of haves and recompute only when a common have was added; cap the commits walked in a negotiation and answer `ready` (send the pack) past it.

#### SEC-GIT-98. A registered CI runner works in `/tmp/moqui-ci` by default, so another local user can own that directory and swap a job's checkout before its steps run

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-host/src/ci_run.rs:271`, `:286-288`, `:65-67`, `:108`; `crates/moqui-git/src/ci/runner/mod.rs:607`, `:613`, `:648-651`, `:126-130`, `:455`, `:662`. Report: round 3 `B17.md`.
- **Reached by:** a local account on the machine of a registered runner started with `moqui ci-run poll` and no `--work` (a Linux host, or a macOS root daemon with no `TMPDIR`, where `std::env::temp_dir()` is `/tmp`), which makes `/tmp/moqui-ci` before the runner first does: after a boot clears `/tmp`, or before the runner's first job. Without owning it, any local account still reads each job's files while it runs.
- **What happens:** Each claimed job's directory is `/tmp/moqui-ci/<jobRunId>`. The runner removes it, makes `<job>/home` for a step user and `<job>/src`, checks the commit out there, unpacks caches, hands `src` and `home` to the step user with `chown -R` (as root with `--step-user`), and runs each step with that directory as its working directory and the job's variables and secrets in its environment. Nothing checks the owner or mode of `/tmp/moqui-ci`, and every directory is made with the umask (0755 under 022). The owner of `/tmp/moqui-ci` can rename `<job>` within its own directory and put its own tree or a symbolic link there after the checkout: the steps then run that user's files with the job's secrets, the root runner writes its cache archive (`Scratch::new`, `mod.rs:571-573`) and runs `chown -R` through the swapped path, and the artifacts uploaded are that user's. Where the runner made the directory itself, any local user still reads a private repository's checkout, its caches and the steps' `home` during the job, and runners of two pools on one machine share the directory.
- **Evidence:** `ci_run.rs:271` `let work = option(args, "--work").map_or_else(|| std::env::temp_dir().join("moqui-ci"), PathBuf::from);`
- **Evidence:** `ci_run.rs:286-288` `if let (Some(job), Some(token)) = (answer["jobRunId"].as_str(), answer["token"].as_str()) {` / `let dir = work.join(job);` / `let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);`
- **Evidence:** `ci_run.rs:66-67` `let home = workspace.parent().unwrap_or(workspace).join("home");` / `match std::fs::create_dir_all(&home) {`
- **Evidence:** `runner/mod.rs:607` `std::fs::create_dir_all(&options.workspace).map_err(|err| {`; `:648-651` `if let Some(user) = &options.step_user {` ... `if let Err(err) = hand_over(&path, user) {`; `:127-128` `let status = Command::new("chown")` / `.arg("-R")`
- **Evidence:** `runner/mod.rs:455` `.current_dir(&options.workspace)`; `:662` `env.extend(pairs(&job["secrets"]));`
- **Not confirmed:** the race was not run, and how `transport.checkout` (`runner/http.rs`) treats a path swapped under it was not read. Also not traced: the claim answer's `jobRunId` is joined to the work directory unchecked (`ci_run.rs:287`), so an answer of `../x` makes the runner remove and use a directory outside it; only whoever answers as the CI server reaches that. Looked for and not found: an owner and mode check of the work directory such as `embedded::socket_dir_private` (SEC-OUT-06), `private_fs::create_dir_all`, a fresh random directory per runner, `O_NOFOLLOW`; a word on `--work`'s default in `moqui help ci-run` (`usage.rs:593-594` lists `[--work DIR]` only) or `docs/interfaces/Pipelines.md` (330-345).
- **Suggested fix:** default `--work` to a directory of the runner's own (its home or state directory, or one `mkdtemp` per start), make it with `private_fs::create_dir_all` and refuse one that is not a directory of this user with mode 0700, as `socket_dir_private` does; refuse a `jobRunId` that is not an id; with `--step-user` keep `<job>` root's and 0711.
- **Re-check:** Read `moqui-host/src/ci_run.rs:271` (`--work` defaults to `std::env::temp_dir().join("moqui-ci")`), `:286-288` (each job's directory is `work.join(job)`, removed then made again), `ci/runner/mod.rs:607` (`create_dir_all` of the workspace, with the umask) and `:661-664` (the job's secrets go into the steps' environment). Not read by the lead: whether the runner checks the owner or mode of `work` (the reviewer says it does not); not run.

## Mail

#### SEC-MAIL-02. A mail account's server settings make the server connect to internal addresses

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `inbound::connect_tcp` looks the host up once, checks every address and connects to one of them: none internal when the server is denied the private network (on production, every account-owned server, which is never given `allowPrivateNetwork` Y), and never a link-local or metadata address; SMTP takes the same rule. `test#EmailServer` answers a fixed kind of failure (`implementation::test_failure_kind`) and logs the server's text. Tests: moqui-mail `implementation::tests::a_server_not_allowed_the_private_network_is_refused_on_production_and_credentials_need_tls`, `client_tests::test_email_account_against_local_servers`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-mail/src/inbound.rs:1018-1031` (`connect_tcp`), `crates/moqui-mail/src/sync.rs:462-530` (`open_imap`), `crates/moqui-mail/src/implementation.rs` `smtp_transport` (1888-1934), `require_host` (979-998)
- **Reached by:** any signed-in member of `BASIC_COMMS_USERS`: the MailAccounts wizard's save and test (`MailSettingsServices` to `create#EmailAccount`, `test#EmailServer`), then "Sync now" and the sync job every minute.
- **What happens:** The user sets `storeHost`, `storePort`, `smtpHost` and `smtpPort` to any name or IPv4 literal (the wizard's `hostOk` pattern takes `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, `10.0.0.5`). `allowed-hosts` is empty by default, the IMAP and POP connection has no private-address check at all, and the SMTP one checks only servers with `allowPrivateNetwork` N, which account servers never get. The test's step message tells open from closed ports and can carry the first line the service answered, so a user can map the instance's network and loopback services and read a banner; the IMAP client also writes `s1 CAPABILITY` and a sign-in to that port.
- **Evidence:** `inbound.rs:1023-1027` `to_socket_addrs(&(host, port))` ... `TcpStream::connect_timeout(&address, Duration::from_secs(30))`; `implementation.rs:995-998` `if allowed.trim().is_empty() { return true; }`; `implementation.rs` `let connect_host = if field_text(server, "allowPrivateNetwork") == "N" { public_address(&host, port)? } else { host.clone() };`; `sync.rs:449-455` `return Err(SyncFailure::Connect(format!("{command}: {rest}")));`; `implementation.rs` `smtp_step` `Err(err) => TestStep::failed("smtp", format!("SMTP {place}: {err}"))`; `MailSettingsServices.xml` `const hostOk = (value) => /^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?)*$/.test(value)`
- **Not confirmed:** how much of a failure's text `MailSettingsServices.test#MailAccount` passes to the page (it joins `step.message` and a hint in the one place read); the POP client (`open_pop`) was not read but is documented to go through `connect_tcp`; whether an instance fence (`fence::check_effect`) is set on production.
- **Suggested fix:** Give every `EmailServer` a mail account owns `allowPrivateNetwork` N on a production instance and apply the one-lookup public-address rule in `connect_tcp` as `smtp_transport` does; answer a test with a fixed list of failure kinds.

#### SEC-MAIL-03. An anonymous GET changes the status of any mailbox or Outbox message

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-15 with SEC-RTE-01: the tracking image needs the message's keyed view key and moves only an `ES_SENT` message.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1289-1340` (`view_email_message`, `mark_email_message_viewed`); `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/BasicEntities.xml:670-675`
- **Reached by:** anonymous, `GET /email/{emailMessageId}` with a guessed sequenced id.
- **What happens:** The handler marks any `EmailMessage` `ES_VIEWED` with authorization disabled when the status flow allows it, and the flow allows it from `ES_READY`, `ES_SENT` and `ES_RECEIVED`. Those are also the statuses of the mail client's rows: a message waiting in a user's Outbox or being sent (`ES_READY`) leaves the Outbox queries and the job that retries a stopped send, so it is never sent and no longer listed; an Inbox message (`ES_RECEIVED`) leaves the unread count query and the out-of-office reply, and its `receivedDate` is overwritten. Ids are sequenced, so a loop over a range hits every message.
- **Evidence:** `web/mod.rs:1320-1324` `.find("moqui.basic.email.EmailMessage")?.eq("emailMessageId", Value::string(&id)).disable_authz().one()?`; `web/mod.rs:1332-1336` `("statusId".to_string(), Value::string("ES_VIEWED")), ("receivedDate".to_string(), crate::user::now_effective_value()),`; `BasicEntities.xml:670` `statusId="ES_READY" toStatusId="ES_VIEWED" transitionName="View"`; `EmailClientServices.xml:3854` `ec.entity.find(MESSAGE).condition('statusId', 'ES_READY').condition('sendError', 'equals', null)`; `EmailClientServices.xml:667` `item.outboxCount = messages().condition('statusId', 'in', ['ES_SCHEDULED', 'ES_READY']).count()`; `EmailClientServices.xml:4009` `.condition('emailFolderId', 'in', inboxIds).condition('statusId', 'ES_RECEIVED')`
- **Not confirmed:** a rate limit or tarpit on `/email/`; how the Inbox list (`find#Messages`) and the Sent folder treat `ES_VIEWED` (not read); what `record#SendResult` does when the status changed while a send was in flight (line 3751 tests `ES_READY`).
- **Suggested fix:** Mark only a message with no `emailAccountId` that is `ES_SENT`, and put a random token in the pixel URL (a field of the message) instead of the bare id.

#### SEC-MAIL-04. The tools REST resource returns mailbox passwords and OAuth refresh tokens

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: entity REST and Service REST entity resources answer no encrypted field, only `<field>IsSet` (SEC-DAT-03), and the tools `email/servers` GETs are services (`EmailServices.find#RelayServers`, `get#RelayServer`) that neither list nor answer a server a mail account owns (`EmailAccount.ownsEmailServer`). `PATCH email/servers/{id}` is still the entity update, `ADMIN` only, and does not refuse an account-owned server as the Admin screens do. Tests: `rest_tests.rs` `entity_resources_answer_encrypted_fields_as_set_and_read_a_page_at_most`, `framework/test/RelayServers.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/tools/service/moqui.rest.xml:122-131`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/rest.rs:659-695`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/sensitive.rs:23-29, 62`
- **Reached by:** a member of `ADMIN` (or a bearer of an ADMIN's API key), `GET /rest/s1/moqui/email/servers` and `/servers/{emailServerId}`.
- **What happens:** The other slice's report is confirmed. `EmailServer` is in the secret-config list, which only gates writes; reads go to `find_entity`, and the answer is stripped only of `UserAccount` password fields. Each row carries `mailPassword`, `smtpPassword` and `oauthRefreshToken` as the entity facade returns them, for relays and for every user's own mail account server, so one request gives an administrator each user's mailbox password and a Google or Microsoft refresh token. The entity's own description says of the token "never answered by a service". `GET /rest/s1/moqui/email/messages` likewise answers the messages of every account.
- **Evidence:** `rest.rs:647-648` `// As Java's RestApi, identity-admin entities are never readable here; secret-config reads are allowed on a declared resource and writes need ADMIN.`; `rest.rs:667` `"list" | "one" => find_entity(&facade, name, master_name, operation, params),`; `find_entity` `row.fields().iter().filter(|(_, value)| !value.is_null()).map(|(name, value)| (name.to_string(), value.clone()))`; `BasicEntities.xml:722,726,737-739` `<field name="mailPassword" ... encrypt="true"`, `<field name="oauthRefreshToken" type="text-long" encrypt="true"><description>The provider's refresh token, encrypted; never answered by a service.`
- **Not confirmed:** that the row's values are decrypted at this point was not traced in the entity facade; it is inferred from `implementation.rs:1849-1855`, where `field_text(server, "smtpPassword")` of a found row is used as the SMTP password. Whether `EmailServerAccessToken` or `EmailOAuthProvider` (client secret) is reachable through another declared resource or the generic entity tools was not checked (`is_restricted_generic_entity` covers `emailserver` and `systemmessageremote` only by name).
- **Suggested fix:** Leave every `encrypt="true"` field out of generic entity REST answers (answer whether one is set), and remove the `servers` entity resource or limit it to servers no account owns.

#### SEC-MAIL-62. A GET mails a screen's full export to an address the request names

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-15 with SEC-MAIL-61 (the same transitions).
- **Where:** runtime/components/base/webroot/template/screen/BasicTransitions.xml:20-28 (and 29-37); runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/mail/EmailServices.xml:242-264
- **Reached by:** an outsider who gets a signed-in user of one of the five mr-marble accounting screens to open a link (`.../FindTransactionEntry/emailScreenAsync?toAddresses=attacker@x&screenRenderMode=csv`); or that user directly.
- **What happens:** The transition is `read-only="true"` with a service call, so a GET with URL parameters runs it and no session token is checked. It renders the active screen with `pageNoLimit=true` as xlsx or csv and mails it through the `SYSTEM` server to `toAddresses`, `ccAddresses` and `bccAddresses` from the request, with `emailTemplateId` also the request's. One click sends every general ledger entry or the whole asset list to an outside address; `emailScreenUserAsync` fixes only `toAddresses` and leaves cc and bcc to the request.
- **Evidence:** `<transition name="emailScreenAsync" read-only="true">`; `<parameter name="toAddresses" required="true">`, `<parameter name="emailTemplateId" default-value="SCREEN_RENDER"/>`; `<if condition="!bodyParameters.pageNoLimit"><set field="bodyParameters.pageNoLimit" value="true"/></if>`; `in-map="Object.assign({}, context, {..., toAddresses: ec.user.userAccount?.emailAddress})"` (the user variant)
- **Not confirmed:** whether a mail server is set for `SYSTEM` on a given instance (with none the send is captured, not sent); the user the async render runs as and its authorization; a rate limit.
- **Suggested fix:** Make both transitions POST-only with the session token, pass named parameters only (no `emailTemplateId`, cc or bcc from the request), and send only to the user's own address unless the user holds a permission for outside recipients.

#### SEC-MAIL-64. mr-marble sendEmailMessage transitions send any stored message again

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `send#EmailMessage` (`moqui_mail` `send_message`, `may_send`) answers a message the caller may not send as one that does not exist: with authorization on, system mail only when Ready or Bounced (any for an `ADMIN`), and an account's message only when Ready or Bounced and the caller has send level on that account (`get#EmailAccountAccess` `canSend`); internal work is unchanged. mr-marble's `sendEmailMessage` transitions of Party, Order, Shipment and Invoice find the message by the record shown and pass only its id. Tests: moqui-mail `implementation::tests::a_user_sends_again_only_a_message_they_may_send`, `mr-marble/test/PartyEmailBody.spec.js`.
- **Where:** runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Party/PartyEmails.xml:20; Order/OrderDetail.xml:73; Shipment/ShipmentDetail.xml:63; Accounting/Invoice/EditInvoice.xml:70; crates/moqui-mail/src/implementation.rs:253-312
- **Reached by:** any signed-in user authorized for one of those four screens, POST `.../sendEmailMessage` with `emailMessageId=N`.
- **What happens:** The transition passes the request's parameters to `send#EmailMessage`, which checks no owner and refuses only `ES_DRAFT` and `ES_CANCELLED`. Any other message is sent through its own server or its mail account's server: another user's Sent mail goes to its recipients a second time from that user's account and credentials, a scheduled message goes now, a received message is sent out again from the account under the original sender's From, and old system mail (a reset, an invoice) is mailed again.
- **Evidence:** `<transition name="sendEmailMessage"><service-call name="org.moqui.mail.EmailServices.send#EmailMessage"/>`; `if status == "ES_DRAFT" { return fail(...) } if status == "ES_CANCELLED" { return fail(...) }` then `let account_id = field_text(&message, "emailAccountId"); let client = !account_id.is_empty();` and the server lookup, with no user or access check in between.
- **Not confirmed:** the service's artifact authorization for a marble user (it is called below the screen's inherited grant). `send_message` was read to its end (253-417): there is no later status or owner check; a server's `allowedToDomains` drops recipients outside it, and only a Ready or Bounced message's status changes after the send.
- **Suggested fix:** Check in `send#EmailMessage` that the message is system mail the caller may send (no `emailAccountId`, status Ready or Bounced) or is of an account the caller has send level on; have the marble transitions check the message belongs to the order, invoice, shipment or party shown.

#### SEC-MAIL-81. A message whose From names two domains, or none, is not held to DMARC

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-mail/src/auth.rs:643-665` (`verify_with`); `crates/moqui-host/src/smtp.rs:1946-1956`; `crates/moqui-mail/src/store.rs:128-137`
- **Reached by:** anonymous, an SMTP client of the listener in internet mode (port 25 of an edge or all-in-one node).
- **What happens:** `verify_with` takes the action from `dmarc.result()` and `dmarc.policy()`. `mail-auth` 0.15.1 returns an empty output (result `None`, policy `None`) when the `From` addresses are of more than one domain or there is none, so `failed` is false and the action is `Accept`. The listener refuses only `DmarcAction::Reject` and sends only `Quarantine` to Junk. The message is stored with the first mailbox of the (last) `From` field as its sender. So `From: ceo@bank.example, x@evil.example` (one field, two mailboxes), or two `From` fields, is delivered to the Inbox with `fromAddress` `ceo@bank.example` although `bank.example` publishes `p=reject`, and the listener's own `Authentication-Results` says `dmarc=none`, with `spf=pass` and `dkim=pass` for the sender's own domain. The out-of-office reply, which is held back only from a sender the kept `Authentication-Results` say failed DMARC, then answers the forged address.
- **Evidence:** `auth.rs:660` `let failed = matches!(dmarc.result(), DmarcResult::Fail(_));`; `auth.rs:661-665` `let mut action = match (failed, dmarc.policy()) { (true, Policy::Reject) => DmarcAction::Reject, (true, Policy::Quarantine) => DmarcAction::Quarantine, _ => DmarcAction::Accept, };`; `mail-auth-0.15.1/src/dmarc/verify.rs:46-48` "Messages without a From domain, or whose From header lists several different domains, are exempt and yield an empty output (result `None`)."; `mail-auth-0.15.1/src/dmarc/verify.rs:136-138` `} else if rfc5322_from_domain != domain { return DmarcOutput::default(); }` and `141-143` `if rfc5322_from_domain.is_empty() { return DmarcOutput::default(); }`; `mail-auth-0.15.1/src/dmarc/output.rs:132-137` `None if self.record.is_some() => DmarcResult::Fail(Error::NotAligned), None => DmarcResult::None,`; `mail-auth-0.15.1/src/message.rs:363-370` (`parse_from` extends `self.from` with every mailbox of every `From` field); `smtp.rs:1950` `if verdict.dmarc == moqui_mail::auth::DmarcAction::Reject {`; `store.rs:128-130` `let (from_address, from_name) = message.from().and_then(|list| list.first())`; `mail-parser-1.0.1/src/view/headers.rs:240-245` (`last_known` reads the last field of the name). Looked for and not found: a test of the number of `From` fields or mailboxes in `smtp.rs` `check` (1924-2008), in `auth.rs` `verify` and `verify_with`, and at the head of `delivery.rs` `deliver` (692-697).
- **Not confirmed:** not run; the library's lines were read by the reviewer only. What Rspamd (when `mail_rspamd_url` is set) does with such a message; how the mail client shows a message whose verdict is `dmarc=none`.
- **Suggested fix:** In `verify_with`, before the DMARC check, refuse (550 5.7.1, RFC 7489 section 6.6.1) a message with more than one `From` field, with no `From` address, or whose `From` addresses (`message.from_addresses()`) are not all of one domain; or evaluate DMARC for each domain and take the strictest action. Add a `Verdict` action for it and a test with both forms.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `auth.rs:643-664`: the action is Reject or Quarantine only for `DmarcResult::Fail`; any other result (None included) is Accept, and the results line and the shown sender use `first_from_address()`. That `mail-auth` answers None for such a From is the reviewer's reading of the crate, not the lead's.

#### SEC-MAIL-82. A mail user gets the node's DKIM signature on mail From any address of a hosted domain

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-mail/src/implementation.rs:1663-1677` (`lettre_send`), `1545-1563` (`deliver`); `crates/moqui-mail/src/auth.rs:408-417` (`signing_domain`), `427-458` (`is_hosted_domain`), `493-512`; `crates/moqui-mail/src/edge_mail.rs:629-634`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/mail/EmailClientServices.xml:5107-5122` (`create#EmailIdentity`)
- **Reached by:** any mail client user (`BASIC_COMMS_USERS`). First path: the MailAccounts wizard, an account of another server with an outgoing host the user names, then `create#EmailIdentity` on it (the user owns it, level 3) with an address of a hosted domain, then a send. Second path: the owner of a hosted account whose server is `edge:` adds an identity `ceo@` the same domain and sends.
- **What happens:** Every send by SMTP goes through `lettre_send`, whatever the server. When `mail_dkim_key` is set it signs the message for its `From` domain if that domain is a hosted or shared account's (or in `mail_dkim_domains`); it does not ask whether the sending account is hosted, has that address, or whose server the message goes to. An identity's address is any text with an `@` (SEC-MAIL-68, Info, open). So the user's own SMTP server receives a message `From: ceo@hosted.example` with a valid `d=hosted.example` signature of the node's key, which passes DKIM and DMARC anywhere once replayed. On the second path the edge node signs it (the domain is the paired node's) and delivers it to the MX itself. SEC-MAIL-67's fix limits which domains are signed for; it does not limit who may send as them, and SEC-MAIL-68 records the identity as stopped only by the account's own SMTP server, which here is the attacker's.
- **Evidence:** `implementation.rs:1666-1670` `if super::auth::key_configured() || own_listener(server) { let formatted = message.formatted(); let signed = super::auth::sign_for(formatted.clone(), |domain| { super::auth::is_hosted_domain(facade, domain) });`; `auth.rs:409-416` `let domain = from_domain(&raw)?; let configured = configured_domains(); let signs = if configured.is_empty() { hosted(&domain) } else { configured.contains(&domain) };`; `edge_mail.rs:631` `super::auth::sign_for(raw, |domain| paired.contains(domain))`; `EmailClientServices.xml:5113` ``if (!String(emailAddress).includes('@')) { ec.message.addError(`Not an email address: ${emailAddress}`); return }`` (the only test of the address in 5107-5122); `EmailClientServices.xml:273-276` (an account's own server is made from the `smtpHost` given). Looked for and not found: a test in `lettre_send`, `deliver` or `sign_for` of the sending account's kind, of the account's own address against `From`, or of `EmailAccount.ownsEmailServer`; a test in `create#EmailIdentity` of the address against the account's domain or the hosted domains.
- **Not confirmed:** that `save#Draft` and `send#Draft` put the identity's address in the letter's `From` unchanged (the map says so: "The draft's `fromAddress` is the identity's address"); `update#EmailIdentity`; whether an application narrows the wizard (the other report of this round found none that replaces it).
- **Suggested fix:** Sign only when the sender is entitled to the address: in `deliver`, pass the sending account and sign only for a hosted or shared account whose own address or a mail administrator's identity is the `From` address, and for system mail only through a relay no account owns. Independently, let `create#EmailIdentity` and `update#EmailIdentity` take an address of a hosted domain (or of `mail_dkim_domains`) only from a holder of `EMAIL_ACCOUNT_HOSTED`, which also closes SEC-MAIL-68 for those domains.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `implementation.rs:1658-1672`: `sign_for` is asked only whether the From domain is hosted, not whether the sender owns the address. Not read by the lead: what limits an identity's From address (the callers of `:1666`).

#### SEC-MAIL-83. A recipient domain's mail server holds an MX delivery session without end and makes the node buffer an unbounded line

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-mail/src/mx.rs:1161-1288` (`session`), `1149-1155` (`Target::step`), `442-491` (the flight slots); `lettre-0.11.23/src/transport/smtp/client/connection.rs:285-296`
- **Reached by:** whoever makes the edge node send one message to a domain they run. A mail user of a paired node does. An anonymous sender does when a hosted account has an automatic reply on (the reply goes to the `From` address of mail that did not fail DMARC), when a rule forwards to an outside address, or when `dsn` is on (the "delivered" notice to a sender with `spf=pass`).
- **What happens:** `session` sets the socket timeout before each command (`within!`) and tests the try's deadline only there. Lettre reads a reply with `BufReader::read_line` and tests its size limits only after a line ends. The read timeout is of one `read` call. A server that answers the greeting, `EHLO` or any command with bytes and no line feed, one byte each 59 seconds, never times out and never returns to `session`, so `SESSION_DEADLINE` and the row's lease do not end it: the thread, its row and its message stay in `Flight` for the life of the process. Eight such sessions (or the pairing's share) stop all MX delivery of that pairing, on a single pairing of the node. Sent fast instead, the same line grows a `String` without limit until the process has no memory.
- **Evidence:** `lettre-0.11.23/.../connection.rs:289-295` `while self.stream.read_line(&mut buffer).map_err(error::network)? > 0 { if buffer.len() - pre > MAX_RESPONSE_LINE_BYTES { return Err(error::response("SMTP response line too long")); } if buffer.len() > MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES {` (the limits are tested after `read_line` returns); `connection.rs:41` `stream: BufReader<NetworkStream>,`; `connection.rs:199` `self.stream.get_mut().set_read_timeout(duration)?;`; `mx.rs:1150-1154` `let left = self.deadline.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now()); if left.is_zero() { return Err("the try's time ran out".to_string()); } Ok(left.min(SMTP_TIMEOUT))`; `mx.rs:1171` `SmtpConnection::connect(address, Some(step), &client, None, None)` (the greeting and `EHLO` are read inside it); `mx.rs:448-454` `if flight.rows.contains(&id) || flight.items.contains(&item) || flight.rows.len() >= MAX_IN_FLIGHT || flight.nodes.get(&node).copied().unwrap_or(0) >= share { held_back = true; continue; }` and `478-481`, where a row leaves `Flight` only when its thread ends. Looked for and not found in `mx.rs`: a watchdog that ends a session thread at the deadline or the lease; a wrapper of the socket that limits the bytes or the time of one reply; a release of the `Flight` entry when the lease runs out.
- **Not confirmed:** not run against a server. The same client (`lettre::SmtpTransport`) is used for an account's own outgoing server (`implementation.rs` `smtp_send_retry`) and for the Postfix relay (`edge_mail.rs:540-544`); whether a user's own SMTP host can hold a request or job worker the same way was not traced.
- **Suggested fix:** Give each session a hard end outside lettre: connect the `TcpStream` in `mx.rs`, keep a clone, and have a timer (or the delivery thread, from the row's start time) call `shutdown(Both)` on it at the deadline, so a blocked read fails. Limit one reply by time, not by read: wrap the stream in a reader that fails after a total of 64 KiB or 60 seconds per reply. Free a row's `Flight` entry when its lease has passed.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `mx.rs:1150-1160`: `step()` tests the deadline when a command starts and gives each a minute. Not read by the lead: the reply reader and its line bound (lettre).

#### SEC-MAIL-84. A manager of a mail account points its server at another host and receives the stored password or OAuth token

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/mail/EmailClientServices.xml:467-471` (`update#EmailAccount`); `crates/moqui-mail/src/sync.rs:530-548` (`sign_in`); `crates/moqui-mail/src/oauth.rs:1736-1746` (`store_on_account`)
- **Reached by:** a member of a mail account with `EapManage` (level 3) who is not its owner, or a user who became owner through `transfer#EmailAccountOwner`. Path: `BasicComms/Settings/MailAccounts/Account.xml:49` and `:56` (`MailSettingsServices.save#AccountConnection`) to `update#EmailAccount`, then the account's test (`test#EmailAccount`, level 3), "Sync now", or the sync job a minute later.
- **What happens:** `update#EmailAccount` writes each server field given and skips an empty password, so the stored one stays. Nothing clears `mailPassword`, `smtpPassword` or `oauthRefreshToken` when `storeHost`, `smtpHost`, a port, `storeProtocol`, `storeAllowPlain` or `mailUsername` changes. The wizard service does not ask for the password when it changes an existing account's servers. The next connection signs in to the new host with the stored secret: `LOGIN` with the password, or XOAUTH2 with an access token made from the stored refresh token (for Google, the `https://mail.google.com/` scope). A member who was given the mail of a shared mailbox gets the mailbox's password, or a bearer token good for the whole mailbox at the provider, which the account's access level never gave. The relay path already has this rule (`update#RelayServer`: a changed host, port, TLS setting, user or sign-in kind clears each stored password not given again); the account path lacks it. `oauthProviderEnumId` can be changed the same way, which sends one provider's refresh token to another provider's token endpoint.
- **Evidence:** `EmailClientServices.xml:360` ``if (access.level < 3) { ec.message.addError(`You may not change email account ${emailAccountId}`); return }``; `EmailClientServices.xml:467-471` `for (const name of serverGiven) {` / `if (name === 'mailPassword' && !mailPassword) continue` / `if (name === 'smtpPassword' && !smtpPassword) continue` / `server.set(name, blank(serverFields[name]))`; `EmailClientServices.xml:318` "An empty mailPassword or smtpPassword keeps the one stored"; `MailSettingsServices.xml` (`save#AccountConnection`, existing account) `const changes = Object.assign({emailAccountId: emailAccountId, emailAddress: address}, servers || {})` then `if (password) { changes.mailPassword = password; changes.authTypeEnumId = 'EatPassword' }` (the check `if (!oauth && !password) { invalid('mailPassword', ...` is only in the branch that makes a new account); `sync.rs:467` `let tcp = connect_tcp(&server.host, server.port, server.deny_internal)`; `sync.rs:545-546` `client` / `.login(&server.user, &server.password)`; `sync.rs:538-540` `let authenticator = super::oauth::XOauth2 {` / `response: super::oauth::xoauth2(&server.user, &token.0),`; `oauth.rs:1736-1743` `let empty_hosts = current` ... `if empty_hosts {` / `for (name, value) in provider_server_fields(provider, pending.pop) {` (a sign-in keeps hosts already on the server row, whatever they are); `EmailClientServices.xml:743-744` (`transfer#EmailAccountOwner`) "The server sign-in stays the account's." Looked for and not found: a clear of the secrets on an endpoint change in `update#EmailAccount` (only a change of `authTypeEnumId` away from `EatOAuth2` revokes, lines 461-466); a check that an `EatOAuth2` server's host is the provider's `storeHost` or under its `hostSuffixes` (none in `sync.rs`, `inbound.rs` or `oauth.rs`); an owner-only rule for server fields (level 3 is the only test).
- **Not confirmed:** the SMTP side was read by grep only (`implementation.rs:1916`, `1986`); the same rule is expected there. The post-service SECA at `EdgeClaims.secas.xml:24` was not read further. Not run.
- **Suggested fix:** In `update#EmailAccount`, when the host, port, TLS setting, protocol, user or sign-in kind of either side changes and the matching password is not given again, clear `mailPassword`, `smtpPassword` and the refresh token (revoking it) and mark the account `EasAuthFailed`, as `update#RelayServer` does. For an `EatOAuth2` server refuse a `storeHost` or `smtpHost` that is not the provider row's own or under its `hostSuffixes`, in the update and again where the token is used.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `EmailClientServices.xml:448-476`: the loop over `serverGiven` skips `mailPassword` and `smtpPassword` when not given and sets the other fields, so a new `storeHost` keeps the stored password; nothing between `:448` and `server.update()` clears a credential on a host, port or user change, and `oauthRefreshToken` is cleared only when the type leaves `EatOAuth2`. `sync.rs:530-548` signs in to `server.host` with the stored password or the access token. Not read by the lead: who counts as a manager (`:95-130`), the SMTP side.

#### SEC-MAIL-85. A mail server a user names can exhaust the server's memory: no read from IMAP or POP3 is bounded

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-mail/src/inbound.rs:1122-1137` (`read_line_bytes`), `900-915` (`pop_read_dot`), `833` (greeting), `921-922` (CAPA); `crates/moqui-mail/src/sync.rs:444-460` (`raw_command`), `2462` (body fetch); `imap-3.0.0-alpha.15/src/client.rs:1544-1556`, `1639-1641`
- **Reached by:** any mail client user (`BASIC_COMMS_USERS`), who adds an account with a host of their own (`save#AccountConnection` to `create#EmailAccount`), then the test (`test#EmailAccount`), "Sync now" (`sync#EmailAccount`), the job every minute and an IDLE worker. Also a real provider's server, or anyone in the path of a `storeAllowPlain` connection.
- **What happens:** The POP3 client reads a line with `read_until(b'\n')` into a `Vec` with no length limit, starting with the greeting before any sign-in, and reads a multi-line answer (`CAPA`, `UIDL`, `LIST`, `RETR`) to the dot line with no total limit. The IMAP client crate reads each line the same way and appends lines until the tagged answer, so one response, a literal included, is held whole whatever its size. `FETCH_BYTES` only groups messages by the sizes the server itself reported (`RFC822.SIZE`); a message larger than 8 MiB is "fetched alone", with no upper limit, and a server can report a small size and send any amount. `raw_command` (plain IMAP before STARTTLS) limits a line to 16 KiB but collects lines without limit until the tag. The only limits are the 60 second read and write timeouts on the socket (`inbound.rs:1070-1071`), which a server that keeps sending never reaches. A server that sends without end grows the process's memory until allocation fails; `catch_unwind` (`sync.rs:1082`, `idle.rs:288`) does not catch that.
- **Evidence:** `inbound.rs:1124-1126` `let read = reader` / `.read_until(b'\n', &mut line)`; `inbound.rs:902-913` `loop {` / `let line = read_line_bytes(reader)?;` / `if line == b"." {` ... `raw.extend_from_slice(line);`; `inbound.rs:833` `let greeting = read_line(&mut reader)?;`; `sync.rs:81-84` "the IMAP client holds a whole response, so a batch is bounded by size as well as by count, and a larger message is fetched alone" / `const FETCH_BYTES: u64 = 8 * 1024 * 1024;`; `sync.rs:2617` `let size = size.and_then(|size| u64::try_from(size).ok()).unwrap_or(UNKNOWN_SIZE);`; `sync.rs:2462` `let bodies = conn.session.uid_fetch(uid_set(&uids), "(UID BODY.PEEK[])")?;`; `sync.rs:450-459` `loop {` / `let line = raw_line(stream)?;` ... `lines.push(line);`; `imap client.rs:1641` `let read = self.stream.read_until(LF, into)?;`; `imap client.rs:1553-1556` `let start_new = data.len();` / `self.readline(data)?;`. Looked for and not found: a cap on a line, a literal or a response in `inbound.rs`, `sync.rs`, `remote.rs`, `idle.rs` (only `raw_line`'s 16,384 bytes, `sync.rs:424`); a `Read::take` or a counting reader around `Net`; a maximum message size setting in `email-client` (`sync-batch` and `FETCH_CHUNK` limit counts only). The internal-address guard and `allowed-hosts` limit which host, not what it sends.
- **Not confirmed:** how the `imap` crate reads a literal after the line that announces it (only `readline` and the top of `read_response_onto` were read); not run against a server.
- **Suggested fix:** Wrap `Net` in a reader that counts bytes per response and fails past a limit: a few KiB for a status line, a configured maximum message size (for example 50 MiB) plus a margin for a fetch, and a bound on the lines of one answer. Skip a message whose `RFC822.SIZE` or POP3 `LIST` size is over the maximum and record it as skipped. Give a session an overall deadline.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `inbound.rs:1122-1137` (`read_until(b'\n')` into a `Vec` with no cap) and `:900-915` (`pop_read_dot` appends until a lone dot, no cap). The POP3 path stands. The IMAP path rests on the `imap` crate, not read by the lead.

## Files, DAV, SSH and the terminal

#### SEC-FIL-01. `/resources` runs elFinder write commands on a GET with no session token

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-RTE-05 (the same finding).
- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot.xml:257`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2670`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/elfinder.rs:671,760`
- **Reached by:** a member of `ADMIN` with a cookie session who follows a link on another site to `GET /resources?resourceRoot=...&cmd=rm&targets[]=<hash>` (or `cmd=put&target=<hash>&content=...`, `mkdir`, `mkfile`, `rename`).
- **What happens:** The transition is `read-only`, so URL parameters are accepted, and the session token is checked only for POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE. The session cookie is `SameSite=Lax`, which a top-level cross-site GET carries. A target hash is the volume id and the Base64 of a path, so it is computed, not guessed. `rm` of a folder deletes everything under it; in production this covers `dbresource://` (the drives live under `dbresource://drive`) and `git://` roots, and on a non-production instance also `file:`, `runtime://` and `component://`, where `put` writes code.
- **Evidence:** `webroot.xml:257` `<transition name="resources" read-only="true">`; `render.rs:2667-2672` `if !matches!(job.method.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str(), "post" | "put" | "patch" | "delete") { return Ok(false); }` (in `csrf_rejected`); `render.rs:2076` `if transition.read_only || !runs_actions(transition) { return None; }` (in `insecure_transition`); `crates/moqui-core/src/user/session.rs:3002` `"; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax; Path=/"`; `elfinder.rs:671` `"rm" => 'rm: {` and `:760` `"put" => 'put: {`.
- **Not confirmed:** Not tried against a server. No `Sec-Fetch-Site` check was found by `grep -i 'sec-fetch'` in `crates/moqui-core/src` and `crates/moqui-host/src`; the web protection layer in front of the screen dispatch was not read.
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse the write commands (`mkdir`, `mkfile`, `rm`, `rename`, `upload`, `put`) unless the request is a POST that carried the session token, or split them into a transition that is not `read-only`.

#### SEC-FIL-02. An app password opens the admin shell and the SFTP root over SSH, past the second factor

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `auth_password` records a login by app password (`LoginBy::AppPassword`), which gets the drives backend over SFTP (`sftp-drives`) and nothing else: the admin shell, `sftp-root` and git over SSH are not granted, and `apps` and `agent-apps` answer that an app password opens the drives over SFTP only. Test: moqui-host it `ssh::sftp_and_the_admin_shell_run_on_the_built_in_server`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:905-925`, `:746-763`
- **Reached by:** anyone holding an app password of a user with `SSH_SHELL` (seeded on `ADMIN_ADV`) or `SSH_SFTP` (seeded on `ADMIN`), over the SSH listener.
- **What happens:** `auth_password` tries the secret as an app password and, on success, reads the user's SSH permissions and accepts the login exactly as for the account password. An app password is described as a device credential for CalDAV, CardDAV, WebDAV and SFTP to the drives, has no scope field, and is not subject to the second factor. So a secret stored in a sync client opens `moqui>` (`service`, `entity`, `js`) and the `sftp-root` for an account whose own password SSH refuses because it needs a second factor.
- **Evidence:** `server.rs:910-913` `let by_app_password = moqui_core::user::looks_like_app_password(&secret) && moqui_core::user::with_client_ip(&client_ip, || { moqui_core::user::login_app_password(&username, &secret) }).is_ok();`; `:921` `ssh_permissions()`; `:925` `Ok(permissions) => Ok(self.accept(user, permissions, true)),`; `:760` `self.can_shell = shell;`; `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/SecurityEntities.xml:449-451` `A password for one device or app of a user: the CalDAV, CardDAV and WebDAV servers and the SFTP drives take it ... so an account with a second factor can sync`.
- **Not confirmed:** `command/shell.rs` was not read, so a second check inside the shell (beyond the Tools-app authorization the map names) is not ruled out. Whether `login_app_password` applies the account's `ipAllowed` and lockout was not traced.
- **Suggested fix:** Record on the connection that the login was by app password and give such a login the drives SFTP backend only (no admin shell, no `sftp-root`, no `agent-apps`); or give `UserAppPassword` a scope and check it per channel.

#### SEC-FIL-03. DAV sends a user's HTML or SVG file inline from the application's origin

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: the WebDAV file GET (`dav/files.rs` `get`) and the CalDAV and CardDAV object GET (`dav/pim.rs` `object`) add `web::user_file_guard_headers`, the rule a screen download of a user's file follows: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` (not for a PDF shown inline) and `Content-Disposition: attachment` for a type that can run script (HTML, SVG, XML, JavaScript). Test: moqui-dav `dav::tests::a_users_html_file_is_an_attachment_and_every_file_is_sandboxed`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/files.rs:415-490`
- **Reached by:** a user whose browser holds Basic credentials for `/dav` (the folder listing page exists "for a browser that opens a DAV address") and who opens a file another member put in a shared drive, at `/dav/files/<user>/<drive>/<name>.html`.
- **What happens:** The file is sent with the content type of its name and no `Content-Disposition`, `X-Content-Type-Options` or `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`. The screen download path applies all three to a user's file (`file_headers`), and sends active types as attachments; the DAV path does not share that rule. Script in the file runs in the application's origin, where the same browser's session cookie applies.
- **Evidence:** `files.rs:460-465` `headers: vec![("Content-Type".into(), content_type), ("ETag".into(), etag), ("Accept-Ranges".into(), "bytes".into())],`; compare `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1533-1536` `headers.push(("X-Content-Type-Options".to_string(), "nosniff".to_string())); if inline && content_type != "application/pdf" { headers.push(("Content-Security-Policy".to_string(), "sandbox".to_string())); }`; `grep -i 'nosniff|content-security-policy'` over `dav_http.rs` and `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/*.rs` finds nothing.
- **Not confirmed:** Whether a header layer in `http.rs` adds headers to host routes (the grep there found none). CalDAV and CardDAV GET were not read.
- **Suggested fix:** Send DAV file content with `Content-Disposition: attachment` for active types, and `nosniff` plus `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` on every file, by reusing `user_file_inline` and `file_headers`.

#### SEC-FIL-04. DAV buffers up to 256 MiB per request before it checks credentials

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: what the head answers is answered before the body is read (`moqui_dav::dav::answer_before_body`: a path outside the server, OPTIONS, `enabled="false"`, no credentials, credentials over plain HTTP), and a PUT's credentials are checked first without a login (`credentials_hold`). A PUT's body is spooled as an upload's file part up to `max-file` and handed to the drive without a copy (`BodySpool`, `DavRequest::upload`); any other method's body stops at 1 MiB (`MAX_XML_BODY`). Tests: moqui-dav `dav::tests::the_head_is_answered_before_the_body_and_a_put_takes_its_spooled_file`, moqui-host it `collab::dav_routes_and_coediting_over_the_socket`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-host/src/dav_http.rs:47-59,79`; `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:1286`
- **Reached by:** anyone who can reach the HTTP listener, with any method on `/dav/...` or `/.well-known/caldav` and a large body.
- **What happens:** The handler reads the body whole into memory up to `max-file` (256 MiB), copies it into the `DavRequest` (`body.to_vec()`), and only then calls `serve`, which checks `enabled` and the login. A PUT clones it again into the uploaded file. A few concurrent anonymous requests hold gigabytes; `enabled="false"` does not stop the read.
- **Evidence:** `dav_http.rs:56` `let body = match read_body(&state, body, limit).await {`; `:79` `body: body.to_vec(),`; `http.rs:1286` `let read = axum::body::to_bytes(body, limit.saturating_add(1));`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/files.rs:561` `UploadedFile::in_memory("file", &name, &content_type, context.request.body.clone())`.
- **Not confirmed:** The web protection's per-address connection and rate limits, and the admission queue, were not read; they may bound the number of such requests.
- **Suggested fix:** Check `enabled` and the credentials from the headers before reading the body, cap the body of methods other than PUT at a small size, and spool a PUT to disk.

#### SEC-FIL-08. `/resources` lets `ADMIN` read any file the server's user can read, in production too

Medium; reported (to check); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: on a production instance the elFinder connector refuses every command, reads (`get`, `file`, `open`) included, on a root other than `dbresource://`, `content://` and `git://` (`Connector::root_refused`, before the command runs); a dev or test instance is unchanged. Test: moqui-dav `elfinder::tests::a_file_root_is_not_served_in_production_and_a_data_root_is`.
- **Where:** `webroot.xml:257-272`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/elfinder.rs:756-759`
- **Reached by:** a member of `ADMIN`, `GET /resources?resourceRoot=file:/<dir>&cmd=get&target=<hash>` or `cmd=file`.
- **What happens:** `resourceRoot` is a request parameter and the production rule restricts write commands only. With a `file:` or `runtime://` root, `get` and `file` return file content, which includes `runtime/.env` (the host key, cluster and encryption keys, database passwords). The map treats conf editing as needing the sealed `CONF_EDIT` because the conf is that sensitive; this route gives `ADMIN` the secrets without it.
- **Evidence:** `webroot.xml:258` `<parameter name="resourceRoot"/>`; `elfinder.rs:756-759` `"get" => { let entry = self.entry(&location)?; response.insert("content".to_string(), Value::string(self.read_text(&entry)?)); }` with no `write_refused` call; `elfinder.rs:781` `writes_restricted` is the only production rule.
- **Not confirmed:** `context/locate.rs` `file_path` was not read, so whether a `file:` location may be absolute or leave the runtime directory is not confirmed. The map already says the route "reads and writes the `resourceRoot` it is given (`component://`, `file:` included)".
- **Suggested fix:** In production accept only `dbresource://` and `git://` roots for every command, or require a sealed permission for `file:`, `runtime://` and `component://` roots.

#### SEC-FIL-61. SFTP holds up to `sftp-max-file` in memory per channel, and the handler does not count channels

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: the bytes open SFTP files hold (writes in memory, read windows) are counted per user over all of the user's channels and connections (`ssh/sftp_fs.rs` `Held`), so one user holds at most `sftp-max-file`, and one connection holds at most 8 session channels waiting for a request or running `sftp` (`MAX_SESSION_CHANNELS`; one more is refused). A write is still held in memory, not spooled to a file. Tests: `ssh::sftp_fs::tests::the_bytes_held_are_capped_per_user_over_every_channel`, moqui-host it `ssh::sftp_and_the_admin_shell_run_on_the_built_in_server`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:1010 ; crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/sftp_fs.rs:216
- **Reached by:** any account that can log in over SSH (key, password or app password) while the SSH listener is on; `dav-server` `sftp-drives` is true by default, so no permission is needed.
- **What happens:** A file opened for writing is kept in memory until it is closed, capped at `sftp-max-file` (256 MiB) per channel. `channel_open_session` accepts every session channel of a logged-in connection and each may start the `sftp` subsystem, so one connection can hold many times 256 MiB, and a user has 4 connections. The server's memory is the limit.
- **Evidence:** `server.rs:1016-1021` `if !self.let_in() { reply.reject(...) } self.channels.insert(channel.id(), channel); reply.accept().await;` (no count); `sftp_fs.rs:216-218` `fn room(&self) -> usize { self.max_file.saturating_sub(self.held) }`; `sftp_fs.rs:50-53` `Write { location: String, bytes: Vec<u8> }`
- **Not confirmed:** Whether russh limits channels per connection by itself (the crate was not read); `grep` for `channels.len()` and `MAX_CHANNELS` in `server.rs` found nothing. The write path (lines 462-535, 557-578) was read only by its messages.
- **Suggested fix:** Cap session channels per connection (a handful) and count write buffers per user or per process instead of per channel, or spool a write to a temporary file.

#### SEC-FIL-81. An SSH client sets a terminal size of up to 10 million cells, and each `apps` session draws frames that large

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/server.rs:765-772`, `:1119`, `:1141`; `crates/moqui-host/src/term/apps.rs:343-344`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/encode.rs:84-98`
- **Reached by:** a network peer with no credentials, while the SSH listener is on and `login-dialog` is true (the default): keyboard-interactive is accepted with no questions, then a session channel, `pty-req` and `shell`. Also any account logged in by key or password (`apps`).
- **What happens:** `window` takes the columns and rows of `pty-req` and `window-change` up to 65535 each. `serve` applies only a minimum. The session's `Screen` allocates `Buffer::new(width, height)` for every frame and keeps the last one, and a first frame collects a `Change` for every cell. revue panics above 16384 a side or 10,000,000 cells (caught, so a larger size only ends the session); a size just under it, such as 3160 by 3160, is drawn. That is two buffers of 10 million cells, a vector of 10 million changes and the encoded frame, for each session, redrawn on each key or resize. Eight connections with no user (all of `max-sessions`) do it at once. The browser socket and `agent-apps` clamp to 500 by 500; SSH does not.
- **Evidence:** `server.rs:766-770` `let fit = |value: u32, default: u16| match u16::try_from(value) { Ok(0) => default, Ok(value) => value, Err(_) => u16::MAX, };`; `apps.rs:343-344` `columns: columns.max(20),` `rows: rows.max(10),`; `encode.rs:84` `let mut buffer = Buffer::new(self.width, self.height);`; `encode.rs:94-97` `let all: Vec<Change> = (0..self.height).flat_map(|y| (0..self.width).map(move |x| (x, y)))`; revue 3.15.0 `buffer.rs:47,49` `const MAX_BUFFER_DIMENSION: u16 = 16_384;` `const MAX_BUFFER_SIZE: usize = 10_000_000;`; `crates/moqui-host/src/termws.rs:282` `Some(number.clamp(least, 500))`; `ssh/agent.rs:117` `.filter(|(columns, rows): &(u16, u16)| (40..=500).contains(columns) && (10..=500).contains(rows))`. No maximum was found in `window`, `term::serve`, `moqui_tui::run_session` or its `resize` (`session_loop.rs:251-257` refuses only 0).
- **Not confirmed:** the size of a `Cell` in memory (the reviewer takes it as 24 to 32 bytes, so about 250 MB a buffer) and the cost of laying a screen out at that width; nothing was run. Whether the workspace builds with `panic = "abort"` anywhere (a grep of the root `Cargo.toml` found no `panic` setting).
- **Suggested fix:** Clamp in `window` (or in `term::serve`) to the 500 by 500 the browser socket uses.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `ssh/server.rs:764-772` (`window` clamps each of columns and rows to `u16::MAX` only) and `term/apps.rs:338-345` (the session takes them with a lower bound only). Not read by the lead: where the reviewer's 10 million cell bound comes from, the cost of a frame.

#### SEC-FIL-82. A calendar file of nested `BEGIN` lines overflows the stack when its parse tree is dropped, which ends the server process

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/ics.rs:31-36`, `:54-98`, `:516-584`
- **Reached by:** (a) any signed-in user with a party, through the mr-boss `Commons/Settings/Calendars.xml:64` transition to `mantle.work.CalendarServices.subscribe#Calendar` with an `https` URL they serve, then the `fetch#CalendarSubscriptions` job (`MantleSetupData.xml:832`, every 15 minutes) or `fetchNow`; and whoever serves a feed a user subscribed to. (b) Any DAV user with a calendar: `PUT /dav/calendars/<user>/<calendar>/x.ics` (1 MiB). (c) `import#Calendar` with a file. (d) Anyone who can send mail to a user: a `.ics` part of up to 512 KiB is parsed when the message is shown (`basic-comms/.../InboxServices.xml:465-468`).
- **What happens:** `components` pushes one `Component` for each `BEGIN` line and never limits the depth. At the end of the text, or at an `END`, each open component is pushed into its parent's `children`, so N `BEGIN` lines with no `END` make a chain N deep. `Component` has no `Drop` of its own; when `parse` returns, the compiler's drop of `Vec<Component>` recurses once for each level. A line is 7 or 8 bytes (`BEGIN:` with an empty name is accepted), so 1 MiB is about 131,000 to 150,000 levels, 512 KiB about 65,000, and a 16 MiB feed about 2,000,000. Service and job workers have a 2 MiB stack in a release build. A stack overflow is not a panic: the host function guard (`catch_unwind`) does not catch it and the process aborts. The subscription's `lastFetchDate` is not written when the process dies, so the job fetches the same feed again after a restart. Availability only: no data is read or written.
- **Evidence:** `ics.rs:62` `"BEGIN" => stack.push(Component {`; `ics.rs:91-96` `while let Some(component) = stack.pop() { match stack.last_mut() { Some(parent) => parent.children.push(component),`; `ics.rs:32-36` `struct Component { name: String, properties: Vec<Property>, children: Vec<Component>, }`; `ics.rs:517` `let top = components(text);`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/pool.rs:60` `const WORKER_STACK: usize = if cfg!(debug_assertions) { 8 } else { 2 } * 1024 * 1024;`; `crates/moqui-lib/src/http.rs:46` `const MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES: u64 = 16 * 1024 * 1024;`; `CalendarServices.xml:922` `response = ec.service.rest().method('GET').uri(uri).contentType('text/calendar').timeout(60).denyInternal().call()`; `CalendarServices.xml:963` `const parsed = ec.factory.getTool('ICalendar').parse(String(feedText))`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/oxide/engine.rs:1310` `let result = std::panic::catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(|| body(&mut *js)));`. No depth or component count limit was found in `ics.rs` or `line.rs` (`MAX_OBJECT` in `dav/pim.rs:43` bounds bytes only), no manual `Drop` for `Component`, and no size limit on the feed below the HTTP client's 16 MiB.
- **Not confirmed:** not run. The bytes of stack one level of the drop takes, so whether the 512 KiB mail part or the 1 MiB PUT overflows the thread it runs on (the reviewer holds that the feed's 2,000,000 levels overflow any stack here); which thread and stack size a DAV request and a screen request run on (`script/run.rs:101` speaks of an 8 MiB request worker, `pool.rs:60` of 2 MiB).
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse nesting past a small depth in `components` (iCalendar needs four levels: VCALENDAR, VEVENT or VTIMEZONE, VALARM or STANDARD), and cap the number of components and properties of one file. Give the feed fetch its own size limit.
- **Re-check:** The reviewer rated this High; the lead set it to Medium, as the register's other availability findings by a signed-in user are (SEC-GIT-03, SEC-GIT-05). The lead read `pim/ics.rs:31-36` (`Component` owns `children: Vec<Component>`, derived drop, so the drop recurses a level for each nesting) and `:54-98` (`components` nests with no depth limit, building on a heap stack). Not measured: the stack one level takes; whether a thread's overflow ends the process here (in Rust it aborts).

#### SEC-FIL-83. Any user a drive is listed for locks any path of it over WebDAV, the whole drive included, and holds every other writer

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/files.rs:319-337`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/locks.rs:99-158`; `crates/moqui-core/src/resource/locks.rs:139-185`
- **Reached by:** a signed-in user (app password) who is a member of a shared drive at view level (`RdpView`), with `LOCK /dav/files/<user>/<drive>/` and a `lockinfo` body.
- **What happens:** the LOCK branch checks only that no other user holds a lock, then writes one. It does not ask whether the user may write the path, nor whether the path exists or is one the user may see (a records drive). With no `Depth: 0` the lock is depth infinity, so a lock on the drive's root location covers every file. The resource facade then refuses every write, delete and move in the drive by any other user, the owner and the jobs (no user) included, on the Files screens, SFTP and the services. The lock lasts up to an hour and its holder refreshes or retakes it without end. Only the holder's own UNLOCK with the token removes it; no screen or service removes another user's lock (a grep for `ResourceLock` and `locks::remove` finds only `locks.rs` and the DAV handler). Rows are not counted, so a user also writes one row for each path they name.
- **Evidence:** `files.rs:320-321` `let location = drive.location(&path); if let Err(refused) = locks::check_write(&location, &context.user_id, false) {`; `files.rs:325` `let mut answer = locks::lock(` (no `can_write` test before it; compare `files.rs:178` `resource.privileges(entry.can_write)`); `locks.rs:150` `depth_infinity: !request.header("depth").is_some_and(|depth| depth.trim() == "0"),`; `locks.rs:27` `const MAX_SECONDS: u64 = 3600;`; `crates/moqui-core/src/resource/locks.rs:182-183` `.find(|(path, lock)| user_id != Some(lock.user_id.as_str()) && applies(path, lock, &key, tree));`; `FileServices.xml:627` `if (!access.drive || access.level < 1) continue` (a view member's drive is listed). No access check was found in `locks::lock`, in the LOCK branch or in `moqui_core::resource::locks::put`, and no limit on locks per user or per drive.
- **Not confirmed:** whether the `ownerXml` column (`text-long`, `ResourceEntities.xml:511`) takes the up to 1 MiB of `D:owner` text a LOCK body may carry (`locks.rs:136-145`); if it does, that text is written into the `lockdiscovery` of every entry under the lock in every PROPFIND (`files.rs:173-177`, `locks.rs:61-70`).
- **Suggested fix:** Take a lock only where `get#DriveAccess` gives `canWrite` at the path (for a path that is not there, at its parent); bound the `D:owner` text and the locks one user holds; give the drive's owner and managers a way to release a lock.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `dav/files.rs:319-337`: LOCK calls `locks::check_write(&location, user, false)` and then `locks::lock`; no drive write-level check is in these lines. Not read by the lead: `locks.rs:99-158` (what `check_write` tests).

#### SEC-FIL-84. An SFTP read handle holds a window as large as the client asks, past the per-user bound of SEC-FIL-61

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-host/src/ssh/sftp_fs.rs:351-407`
- **Reached by:** any SSH user, an app password login on the drives backend included (`sftp-drives` defaults to true), with `open` for reading and one `read` whose length is large.
- **What happens:** the recorded fix of SEC-FIL-61 does not hold for reads. `read_window` reads `max(READ_WINDOW, len)` bytes, up to the rest of the file, into the handle's window. `len` is the client's 32-bit value; russh-sftp passes it unchanged. The window is added to the user's `Held` count, but no room is checked, unlike `open` for writing and `write`. A file may be as large as `sftp-max-file` (256 MiB) to be opened. With 64 handles a channel and 8 channels a connection, one connection holds 64 x 8 windows of the file's size, and each answer copies the window again (`window[..stop].to_vec()`).
- **Evidence:** `sftp_fs.rs:374-376` `let want = usize::try_from(size - offset).unwrap_or(usize::MAX).min(READ_WINDOW.max(len as usize));`; `sftp_fs.rs:394-401` `let mine = self.held.mine.saturating_sub(window.len()).saturating_add(fetched.len()); self.held.set(mine); *window_at = offset; *window = fetched;`; `sftp_fs.rs:651-654` (the write path, for contrast) `let grown = end.saturating_sub(bytes.len()); if grown > room { return Err(Self::too_much_held()); }`; `russh-sftp-3.0.1/src/server/mod.rs:57` `Packet::Read(read) => into_wrap!(id, handler, read; id, handle, offset, len),`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/resource/source.rs:128` `let want = usize::try_from((self.len - offset).min(max as u64)).unwrap_or(max);`. No clamp on `len` was found in the handler or in russh-sftp's server (its `max_client_packet_len` bounds what the client sends, not this field).
- **Not confirmed:** the largest packet russh will send back; the window is held in the handle whatever is sent.
- **Suggested fix:** Cap the window at `READ_WINDOW` (answer short reads), and refuse a window the user's room does not hold.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `ssh/sftp_fs.rs:374-401`: `want` is the smaller of the bytes left and `max(READ_WINDOW, len)`, `len` the client's `u32`, and the per-user count `held` is set after the read.

#### SEC-FIL-85. One period of a recurrence rule is built whole before any limit applies: a yearly rule makes about 31.5 million date-times

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-dav/src/pim/rrule.rs:353-379`, `:450-475`, `:641-643`
- **Reached by:** any user who can store an event's rule: a DAV PUT to a calendar they edit (`CalendarDavServices.xml:535`, stored at `CalendarServices.xml:1061` with no check), an import, a subscribed feed. The cost is then paid on every read of a range of that calendar by anyone who sees it (`EventServices.xml:519`), the anonymous published link included. A DAV PUT also expands a rule taken straight from the body, once for each `RANGE=THISANDFUTURE` override in the object (`CalendarServices.xml:1098`).
- **What happens:** `Plan::new` makes `times` as every BYHOUR x BYMINUTE x BYSECOND, up to 86,400. For `FREQ=YEARLY` with all seven weekdays in BYDAY, `period` pushes those times for each of the year's 365 days: 31,536,000 `DateTime` values of 12 bytes, about 380 MB (more while the vector grows), then sorts them. `MAX_INSTANCES`, `MAX_PERIODS` and the open-ended limit are tested only after `period` returns. The rule is about 470 characters and fits the `text-long` `rrule` field. A few requests at once, or a few such events in one range, take gigabytes.
- **Evidence:** `rrule.rs:369-373` `for hour in &hours { for minute in &minutes { for second in &seconds { if let Ok(time) = Time::new(*hour, *minute, *second, start.subsec_nanosecond()) { times.push(time);`; `rrule.rs:454-457` `if self.day_matches(date) { for time in &self.times { out.push(DateTime::from_parts(date, *time));`; `rrule.rs:471-472` `for day in 0..i64::from(first_of_month.days_in_month()) { add_day(add_days(first_of_month, day)?, &mut out);`; `rrule.rs:641-642` `for _ in 0..MAX_PERIODS { let Some(candidates) = plan.period(index) else { break };`; `CalendarServices.xml:1061` `const fields = {rrule: event.rrule ?? null, exdates: listText(event.exdates), ...`; `mr-mantle/entity/WorkEffortEntities.xml:1005` `<field name="rrule" type="text-long"/>`. No bound on a period's size or on the BYxxx list lengths was found in `Rule::parse` (values are range checked, list lengths are not; only BYSECOND is deduplicated, `rrule.rs:251-252`), and no validation of the rule before it is stored.
- **Not confirmed:** the real peak memory and time; the sizes are computed, nothing was run.
- **Suggested fix:** Bound the candidates of one period (stop at the instance limit while generating, or refuse a rule whose times of day times matching days pass a few thousand); parse a rule before storing it.
- **Re-check:** Confirmed by the lead for the cost. The lead read `rrule.rs:353-379` (`times` is every BYHOUR x BYMINUTE x BYSECOND, up to 86,400) and `:450-475` (a yearly period pushes every time of every matching day into one `Vec` before any limit). Not read by the lead: where a stored rule is expanded and for whom.

#### SEC-FIL-86. A PROPFIND that asks for many properties is answered once for each child, whole in memory

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/mod.rs:646-711`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/files.rs:371-386`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/pim.rs:353-366`
- **Reached by:** any DAV user, on a folder or collection with many entries (their own drive will do).
- **What happens:** a 1 MiB body may name about 170,000 properties. `prop_response` writes each asked property the resource lacks into a 404 propstat, for every resource; `multistatus` keeps every response as a `String` and then joins them. A folder of 10,000 files gives about 10,000 x 170,000 x 4 bytes, several gigabytes, from one request. Neither the children (`list#Folder` answers them all) nor the asked properties are counted.
- **Evidence:** `dav/mod.rs:661-665` `for (ns, name) in list { match resource.props.iter().find(|prop| prop.ns == ns && prop.name == name) { Some(prop) => ..., None => missing.push_str(&xml::empty_element(ns, name)),`; `dav/mod.rs:694-698` `let responses: Vec<String> = resources.iter().map(|resource| prop_response(resource, wanted)).collect();`; `dav/files.rs:381` `Ok(list) => resources.extend(list.iter().map(|child| entry_resource(context, drive, child))),`; `dav/mod.rs:213` `pub const MAX_XML_BODY: usize = 1024 * 1024;`. No limit on `PropRequest::Some` entries or on the children of one answer was found, and the multistatus is not streamed.
- **Not confirmed:** whether the host bounds a response body (`dav_http.rs` not read). The sizes are computed, nothing was run.
- **Suggested fix:** Cap the asked properties (a few hundred), and write the multistatus as a stream or cap its size.

#### SEC-FIL-99. A bracketed paste is kept in memory with no cap and no deadline, over SSH before a login

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-tui/src/decode.rs:72-76`, `:91-104`, `:471-473`; `crates/moqui-host/src/term/apps.rs:304`, `:378`, `:389-393`. Report: round 3 `B15.md`.
- **Reached by:** any SSH peer when `ssh-server@port` is set and `login-dialog` is on (its default): a keyboard-interactive connection gets an `apps` session with no user (`Who::Login`, `apps.rs:327-331`) for `LOGIN_WAIT` (120 seconds). Any signed-in user through `/termws` (an `apps` session is open to every signed-in user) or a signed-in SSH `apps` session, with no time limit but the 10-minute idle.
- **What happens:** After `ESC [ 200 ~` every byte of input is appended to `Decoder::paste` until `ESC [ 201 ~`, and the deadline is off inside a paste, so nothing ends it. Each read (4096 bytes) scans the whole buffer again for the end marker, so the session thread's work grows with the square of the paste.
