The host's pump sends each read to an unbounded `std::sync::mpsc` channel without waiting for the session thread, so the SSH window keeps refilling and the bytes pile up in the queue and the buffer. The login timer only queues a `Close` behind the backlog. Eight such connections from one address are allowed (`max-sessions`, `max-sessions-per-source`).
- **Evidence:** `decode.rs:72-73` `` /// When [`Self::flush`] should run: ... None inside a paste, which `` / `/// waits for its end however long it takes.`; `:93` `paste.extend_from_slice(bytes);`; `:101` `let Some(at) = find_sub(buf, paste_end) else {`; `:472` `hay.windows(needle.len()).position(|window| window == needle)`
- **Evidence:** `apps.rs:304` `let (events, inbox) = mpsc::channel::<SessionEvent>();`; `apps.rs:392` `let _ = events.send(SessionEvent::Input(buffer[..read].to_vec()));`
- **Not confirmed:** not run, and the rate reached was not measured. The SSH server's channel and russh's flow control were not read; that bytes arrive at network rate is reasoned from the unbounded send. Looked for and not found: a cap on `paste` or `pending` (the OSC and CSI scans have one, `decode.rs:182-188`, `:270-272`; a paste has none); a bounded channel or a wait on the session thread in `apps.rs`; a byte limit before login in the SSH layer (the pre-auth limits end at the accepted keyboard-interactive login).
- **Suggested fix:** Cap a paste (for example 1 MiB, the prompt's line limit): drop or deliver what came and leave paste mode past it; search only the new bytes plus the last five for the end marker; make the pump's channel bounded (or count queued bytes) so input waits on the session.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `moqui-tui/src/decode.rs:91-104` (inside a paste every read is appended to `paste` and the whole buffer is searched again for the end marker) and `:72-76` (no deadline applies inside a paste). Not read by the lead: the input channel's bound (`term/apps.rs:304`, `:392`).

#### SEC-FIL-100. The browser terminal's 500 by 500 limit does not hold: the session takes a window size from its input bytes, which brings SEC-FIL-81 to every signed-in user

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-tui/src/decode.rs:300-305`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/session_loop.rs:240-241`, `:251-257`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/session/mod.rs:560-575`; `crates/moqui-host/src/termws.rs:274-284`. Report: round 3 `B15.md`.
- **Reached by:** any signed-in user through `/qapps/Terminal` and `/termws` (an `apps` session), and any SSH `apps` user, who already has SEC-FIL-81.
- **What happens:** `/termws` clamps the JSON resize message to 500 by 500, but the binary input it forwards to the same session goes through the decoder, which turns `ESC [ 8 ; rows ; cols t` into `Decoded::Resize` with the numbers as parsed (`u16`, so up to 65,535); the loop resizes the session and the screen with only a zero check. Frames then reach SEC-FIL-81's size: up to Revue's 10 million cells (beyond 16,384 a side or 10 million cells `Buffer::new` panics and the session ends, revue `buffer.rs:113-133`). A component that draws per row and column multiplies it: the calc grid builds a line for each of `rows - 12` rows with a span per column that fits (`crates/moqui-tui/src/components/calc.rs:1152-1157`, `:1167-1168`), on every frame and every 1.5-second poll.
- **Evidence:** `decode.rs:22` "report is accepted so a test can feed a size without a pty."; `decode.rs:305` `return Take::One(Decoded::Resize { columns, rows }, len);`
- **Evidence:** `session_loop.rs:240-241` `Decoded::Resize { columns, rows } => { resize(view, screen, columns, rows);`; `session_loop.rs:252` `if columns == 0 || rows == 0 || screen.size() == (columns, rows) {`; `termws.rs:282` `Some(number.clamp(least, 500))`
- **Not confirmed:** not run, and the memory of a frame at the largest allowed size was not measured; the frame cost is SEC-FIL-81's, likely. Looked for and not found: a maximum in `resize` (`session_loop.rs:251-257`), `Session::resize` (`session/mod.rs:560-575`) and `Screen::resize` (`encode.rs:71-75`); a filter of `CSI t` reports in `termws.rs` `run_ephemeral` or `term/apps.rs`.
- **Suggested fix:** Drop `Decoded::Resize` outside tests (or clamp it to the transport's limit), and put the 500 by 500 limit (or a cell count) in `session_loop::resize`, so every transport has it; fixing SEC-FIL-81 there covers both.
- **Re-check:** The lead read `moqui-tui/src/decode.rs:300-305` (`ESC[8;rows;cols t` becomes `Decoded::Resize` with the numbers as parsed, `u16`, so up to 65,535) and `ui/session_loop.rs:240-257` (the session and the screen are resized with no clamp). The frame cost is as SEC-FIL-81 (likely).

## Calls, chat and messaging

#### SEC-COM-01. Any Comms user lists every account and can message or add it; only calls ask the apps who may be reached

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: a new provider hook, `chat-target` (`ProviderInterfaces.chat#Target`), is asked by the internal `ChatServices.get#RefusedUsers`, which `CommsServices.search#Users`, `create#Room`, `add#Member` and a public room's mention of a non-member use; a refused user is answered as one that does not exist, and a provider that fails refuses them all. `search#Users` finds no `EXTERNAL_USER` account, and an external caller reaches only the people of their own rooms. The Calls pickers ask `call-target`. mr-boss registers `get#CallTarget` as its `chat-target`; its own `ChatterServices.search#Users` was not read. Test: `basic-comms/test/ChatReach.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/service/org/moqui/comms/CommsServices.xml:17-45`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/chat/ChatServices.xml:1334-1340` (`create#Room`), `:1625-1629` (`add#Member`)
- **Reached by:** any signed-in user who may open the Comms screens (`BASIC_COMMS_USERS`, or an app's root that mounts them), `GET .../Chat/searchUsers?term=`, `.../Calls/searchUsers`, `.../Calls/History/searchUsers`; then `.../Chat/createDirect` or `.../Chat/addMembers` with any `userId`.
- **What happens:** `search#Users` reads `UserAccount` with authorization disabled and answers the id and name of every enabled account (an empty term matches all, 50 a call, so a few letters walk the directory), with no organization, provider or `EXTERNAL_USER` check. `create#Room` and `add#Member` then take any enabled account as a member without that account's consent, which also puts the two in each other's `/ChatKeys` reach and presence pushes. Calls do ask the apps (`call-target`, IronStone: the user's organizations) and answer a refused callee "as a user that does not exist", but the Calls picker lists that same user, and chat has no such hook, so in a deployment with several organizations a user of one finds and messages the users of another. mr-boss overrides `searchUsers` only on `Inbox/Mail.xml`.
- **Evidence:** `CommsServices.xml:31` `<entity-find entity-name="moqui.security.UserAccount" list="accountList" limit="${pageLimit}" disable-authz="true">`
- **Evidence:** `ChatServices.xml:1625-1628` `const found = new Set(Array.from(ec.entity.find('moqui.security.UserAccount').condition('userId', 'in', targets)...disableAuthz().list())...)` `if (missing.length) { ec.message.addError(`User ${missing.join(', ')} not found`); return }`
- **Evidence:** `Calls.js:738-739` `// an app's call-target providers may keep the people called to the user's own (the same answer as for no such user)` `if (refusedTargets(invitees).length) return refuse('noCallee', 'Choose someone to call')`; `grep -rn "call-target\|chat-target"` over `chat/` finds nothing.
- **Evidence:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen-extend/basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Inbox/Mail.xml:22-24` is the only `searchUsers` override found in mr-boss.
- **Not confirmed:** whether IronStone keeps users of different organizations out of each other's reach some other way (an entity filter on `UserAccount` does not apply: authorization is disabled); whether an external user can be a `BASIC_COMMS_USERS` member in any app; the XMPP side says an external user "learns only the usernames of the people of their own rooms", which this search does not follow.
- **Suggested fix:** Ask a provider hook (the `call-target` one, or a `chat-target` beside it) in `search#Users`, `create#Room`, `add#Member` and the mention lists, and leave external users out of the search for anyone, and out of its answers for an external caller.

#### SEC-COM-02. A chat message may carry an external image, so a sender learns each reader's address and when they read

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: chat bodies are cleaned with `moqui_lib::safe_html::clean_chat` (`StringUtilities.cleanChatHtml`) in `send#Message`, `edit#Message` and a forward's copy, so an image keeps its `src` only when it is `/richTextImage/<id>`; the client drops any other image source too (`moqui.chat.clean` for an encrypted room, `moqui.chat.ownImages` for a stored body), so a message stored before shows none. No `img-src` policy was added. Tests: `safe_html::tests::a_chat_message_keeps_only_this_servers_images`, `chat_tests::a_message_keeps_only_this_servers_images`, `WebrootChat.page.js`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-lib/src/safe_html.rs:30-36`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/chat/ChatServices.xml:2182,2296`; `runtime/components/base/basic-comms/screen/BasicComms/Chat.qvue:380-381`
- **Reached by:** any member of a room, `POST .../Chat/send` with `body=<p><img src="https://host.example/x.png?u=1"></p>`; every other member's browser loads it when the room shows the message.
- **What happens:** The body is cleaned with ammonia's defaults plus three `span` attributes, and the room renders it with `v-html`. `send#Message` treats a body with only an `<img>` as not empty, so images in bodies are expected, and nothing limits `src` to this server. The default `Content-Security-Policy` has no `img-src`. The link preview code avoids exactly this ("no picture is read, so no reader's address reaches a third party"), and read receipts are a user's choice (`CHAT_READ_RECEIPTS`), which a remote image gets around. The same holds for an encrypted room: the client's DOMPurify list allows `img` with `src`.
- **Evidence:** `safe_html.rs:33-34` `let mut builder = ammonia::Builder::default();` `builder.add_tag_attributes("span", &["data-mention-id", "data-record-entity", "data-record-id"]);`
- **Evidence:** `ChatServices.xml:2296` `const emptyBody = !bodyText && !/<img\b/i.test(html)`
- **Evidence:** `Chat.qvue:381` `v-html="item.bodyHtml" @click="room.bodyClick($event)"></div>`
- **Evidence:** `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootChat.js:665-670` `ALLOWED_TAGS: [..., "i", "img", "ins", ...]`, `ALLOWED_ATTR: [..., "alt", "src", ...]` with the comment "what the server lets into a plain room's message (allow-html="safe", ammonia's defaults and the mention and record spans) and no more"
- **Evidence:** `runtime/components/base/framework/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:827` `<response-header type="screen-render" name="Content-Security-Policy" value="frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self';"/>`
- **Evidence:** ammonia 4.2.5 `src/lib.rs:383` (default tags) `"header", "hgroup", "hr", "i", "img",`; `:413-414` `"img" => hashset![ "align", "alt", "height", "src", "width"`; `:455` `"https",` (default URL schemes), read in the cargo registry copy.
- **Not confirmed:** whether `Chat.qvue` rewrites `img` sources before showing a body (`decorateBody` at line 287 only rewrites mention spans); the referrer policy.
- **Suggested fix:** In chat bodies keep only images whose `src` is this server's own image route (`richTextImage`), or drop `img` from chat's cleaner and the client's list; or send an `img-src 'self' data: blob:` policy on the app pages.

#### SEC-COM-11. `ipAllowed` is not checked for XMPP sign-in or its service calls

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `Backend::call` carries the XMPP session's peer, and the host runs every call (the user's, and the internal sign-in lookups, PLAIN's app password login among them) inside `with_client_ip(peer)`, so its login checks `ipAllowed`; SCRAM and FAST ask `Backend::sign_in` before success, which applies the key login's gate and `ipAllowed` and writes the login history (`login_verified_app_password`). Tests: moqui-xmpp `a_verified_sign_in_is_checked_and_recorded_for_the_sessions_peer`, `user/login_tests.rs` `a_verified_app_password_sign_in_checks_ip_allowed_and_is_recorded`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-host/src/xmpp/mod.rs:596-603`; `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1451-1456`
- **Reached by:** a user whose account or group has `ipAllowed` set, or anyone holding that user's app password, from any address, over `xmpp_port`, `xmpp_tls_port` or `/xmpp-websocket`.
- **What happens:** The session's sign-in (SCRAM, FAST) never calls a login function, and each service call logs the user in with `internal_login_user(username)` on a worker that has no request and no `with_client_ip`. `check_ip_allowed` returns Ok for an empty address. The session reads and writes the user's chat from an address the account is not allowed to sign in from. DAV, SSH, the LLM gateway, A2A and the terminal pass the client address; XMPP does not.
- **Evidence:** `mod.rs:600` `if let Err(err) = moqui_core::user::internal_login_user(username) {`; `login.rs:1454-1456` `if client_ip.is_empty() { return Ok(()); }`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/dav/mod.rs:285` `let logged_in = moqui_core::user::with_client_ip(&request.client_ip, || {` (the pattern XMPP lacks). `Caller::User(String)` carries the username only (`crates/moqui-xmpp/src/backend.rs:33`).
- **Not confirmed:** whether `crate::web::current_client_ip()` can be non-empty on a pool worker from an earlier job (it is the request's; not traced). Not run.
- **Suggested fix:** Carry the session's peer address in `Caller::User` and wrap `run_service` (and the sign-in lookups) in `with_client_ip`; refuse the SASL exchange when `ipAllowed` does not match.

#### SEC-COM-12. A connected XMPP app keeps a locked account locked

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-WEB-03: an XMPP session's service calls log in internally, which counts no failure, and an attempt during a lock window does not restart it, so the lock ends after `disable-minutes` while the app stays connected.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1303-1310`, `1736-1746`; `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/session/mod.rs:505-517`; `XmppServices.xml:87`
- **Reached by:** anyone who can make three failed web sign-ins for a username (the default `max-failures`), while that user has an XMPP app connected.
- **What happens:** Three bad passwords lock the account for 5 minutes (`disable-minutes`). `check#Credential` passes a locked account (its `disabledDateTime` is in the past), so the XMPP session stays. Every service call of the session (the presence heartbeat each 60 seconds, each stanza, each push that looks a room up) runs `internal_login_user`, whose gate counts a failed login for an account that is still disabled, and a count over the maximum writes `disabledDateTime` to now. The lock starts again each minute and does not end while the app stays connected; the web sign-in stays refused.
- **Evidence:** `login.rs:1305-1309` `if still_disabled(account, now) { if !user_id.is_empty() { increment_failed(facade, &user_id)?; } return Err(Error::new(INVALID_LOGIN)); }`; `login.rs:1736-1743` `let disable = next > policy::max_failures(); ... let when = if disable { now_value() }`; `session/mod.rs:512-516` `.call(services::HEARTBEAT, json!({"idleSeconds": ...}))`; `MoquiDefaultConf.xml:912` `<login max-failures="3" disable-minutes="5" history-store="true"/>`.
- **Not confirmed:** `internal_login` reads the account through the entity cache (`find_username_cached`, `login.rs:1642-1647`) and `increment_failed` writes with raw SQL; whether the cached row shows the lock on the node that runs the XMPP calls was not traced. Not run. The same gate serves DAV app passwords and queued work, which belongs to the identity slice.
- **Suggested fix:** Do not count a failure in `gate_account` for an internal login (no credential was tried); end an XMPP session, or skip its service calls, while the account is locked.

#### SEC-COM-13. TURN: usernames from unauthenticated requests are kept without bound

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `admit_message` gives the TURN servers a username only when the request's MESSAGE-INTEGRITY holds for that name's password (`Message::validate_integrity`), which only a client that knows the password sends; any other request gets the server's 401, as an unknown name does. Names that proved their password still stay for the life of the process (turn-server-proto has no removal), bounded by the credentials the services issue. Test: moqui-rtc `turn::tests::a_username_is_kept_only_for_a_request_that_proves_its_password`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-rtc/src/turn/mod.rs:473-492`
- **Reached by:** anyone who can send a datagram to `turn_port` or open one of the TURN TCP or TLS connections (TURN is off by default; UDP sources can be forged).
- **What happens:** `admit_message` runs before the server checks MESSAGE-INTEGRITY. For any request whose USERNAME has the form `<future expiry>:<text up to 192 bytes>`, it computes the password and calls `add_user` on every listener's `TurnServer`, and inserts the name in `self.users`. Nothing removes a name from `users` or from the servers (grep of `users.remove`, `users.retain`, `remove_user` in the file: none), so each distinct forged username grows memory for the life of the process. Honest credentials also stay after they expire.
- **Evidence:** `mod.rs:476` `let good = leg_of(username, unix_now()).filter(|leg| !self.is_revoked(leg));` `:480-491` `if !self.users.contains(username) { let pass = good.map(|leg| self.password_of(username, leg)).unwrap_or_default(); for server in &mut self.servers { server.add_user(username.clone(), pass.clone()); } ... self.users.insert(username.clone()); }`; the comment at `:474-475` "one it has (it expired or its leg was revoked since) is dropped, since the server would still take it".
- **Not confirmed:** `turn-server-proto` was not read (whether `add_user` has a cap of its own). The UDP loop has no per-source rate limit (`crates/moqui-host/src/rtc/turn.rs:463-470`). Not run.
- **Suggested fix:** Add a user only after the server's 401 challenge was answered with a valid nonce, or cap and expire the table (drop a name at its expiry, bound the count, per-source rate on UDP).

#### SEC-COM-14. A file uploaded over XMPP outlives the deletion and retention of its message

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `clear#MessageContent` and `clean#PendingAttachments` remove each chat file through the internal `ChatServices.remove#AttachmentFile`: a file under the XMPP upload root goes when no other `ChatAttachment` has its location, with its `XmppUpload` rows, and the `XmppUpload` rows made to serve the chat file to XMPP clients always go. Test: `chat_tests::a_file_put_over_xmpp_goes_with_the_last_message_that_has_it`.
- **Where:** `ChatServices.xml:2807-2811`, `3235`; `XmppServices.xml:699`, `743`; `crates/moqui-host/src/xmpp/upload.rs:181-187`
- **Reached by:** any holder of the upload URL (the uploader, every XMPP reader of the room, anyone it was passed to), after the message was deleted (`redact#Message`) or removed by room retention.
- **What happens:** `attach#Upload` makes the chat file on the upload's own location, `dbresource://xmpp-upload/...`. `clear#MessageContent` deletes a stored file only when its location starts with the chat root (`dbresource://chat/`), so this file stays; the `XmppUpload` row stays too, since `clean#Uploads` removes only slots never put. The URL goes on serving the file with no expiry. A file uploaded in the web client is under the chat root and is deleted.
- **Evidence:** `ChatServices.xml:2807` `const chatRoot = (System.getProperty('chat_attachment_root') || 'dbresource://chat').replace(/\/+$/, '') + '/'` `:2809` `try { if (file.location && String(file.location).startsWith(chatRoot)) ec.resource.getLocationReference(file.location).delete() }`; `XmppServices.xml:699` `location: upload.location`; `XmppServices.xml:743` `.condition('completedDate', null).condition('createdDate', '<', hourAgo)`.
- **Not confirmed:** the rest of `redact#Message` and `clean#RoomRetention` (only `clear#MessageContent` was read; its description says both call it). Not run.
- **Suggested fix:** In `clear#MessageContent` and `clean#PendingAttachments` also delete a file under the XMPP upload root when no other `ChatAttachment` has the location, and delete the `XmppUpload` rows of the file (by `location` or `chatAttachmentId`).

#### SEC-COM-25. A forged-source digest failure blocks a trunk's or a phone's address

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: the digest nonce's MAC covers the address the challenge was sent to (`NonceBook::nonce`, `verify`), so an answer with a nonce not issued to its own source is challenged again and not counted, and only a source that received the challenge counts failures against itself; a failed answer from a trunk's target or listed inbound address is refused (403) but never counted (`authenticate`, `trunk_by_address`). An operator's block or one reloaded from `VoiceAuthFailure` still applies. Tests: moqui-sip `digest::tests::a_nonce_answers_only_from_the_source_it_was_issued_to`, `ua::phones::tests::failed_answers_from_a_trunks_address_never_block_it`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-sip/src/ua.rs:1871-1888` (failure counting), `ua.rs:469` (the block), `crates/moqui-lib/src/source_guard.rs:121-146`
- **Reached by:** anonymous, over UDP (`sip_port` set), from a network that lets it forge source addresses. Five REGISTER datagrams (registrar on) or INVITE datagrams (source not a trunk's) that carry any `Authorization` / `Proxy-Authorization` header, sent with the victim's address as source.
- **What happens:** A failed answer is counted against the datagram's source address with no proof the source sent it: the nonce need not be one this server issued, and a nonce is not bound to an address. After `authFailureLimit` (5) the address is blocked for `authBlockSeconds` (an hour), on every transport, since the block is by address. With the registrar on, the victim can be a provider trunk's signaling address (a REGISTER is authenticated whatever its source), which drops the trunk's inbound INVITEs and its responses to this server's REGISTER and INVITE, also over the trunk's TLS connection. A phone's NAT address or a PBX that signs in by digest can be blocked the same way with the registrar off (INVITE). The block is written to `VoiceAuthFailure` and reloaded after a restart.
- **Evidence:** `ua.rs:1873-1875` `let blocked = match source { Some(source) => { let failure = lock(&self.guard).failure(source.ip(), Instant::now());`; `ua.rs:1759-1762` `if method == Method::Register { if self.phones.is_some() { ... self.register_phone(tx, source).await;` (no trunk-address test before it); `ua.rs:1827` `None => Some((lock(&self.nonces).verify(auth, &method, None, now_secs()), None)),`; `digest.rs:396-398` `let Some(issued) = self.issued_at(&auth.nonce) else { return Verdict::Invalid; };` (a nonce this server never issued is a counted failure); `digest.rs:334-338` `mac.update(&time.to_be_bytes()); mac.update(salt); mac.update(self.realm.as_bytes());` (no address in the MAC); `ua.rs:469` `if lock(&self.guard).is_blocked(addr.ip(), now) { return None; }` (all transports, trunk addresses included); `source_guard.rs` has no exemption list (grep `exempt|trusted|trunk`: nothing).
- **Not confirmed:** not run. Whether rsipstack drops a datagram whose Via does not match its source before the transaction is made was not read. The map already says a UDP source can be forged (old line 965-966) but only for a trunk taken by address.
- **Suggested fix:** Count a failure only when the answer's nonce was issued by this server to that same source (put the source address in the nonce MAC), so a forged source cannot answer; and never block an address that is a trunk's target or listed inbound address.

#### SEC-COM-81. One signed-in user can stall every user's chat pushes, and make the server post to a host of their choice without limit

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/service/web_push.rs:190-274`, `:1105-1155`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_push.rs:323-325`, `:1087-1111`, `:1821-1879`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/chat_push_transport.rs:142-166`
- **Reached by:** any signed-in user, `POST /WebPush/store` (`webroot/screen/webroot/WebPush.xml:18-31`), then `POST /qapps/testClientPush` (`qapps.xml:145-158`) or any message to a room the user is in.
- **What happens:** `store#WebPushSubscription` keeps a row for every distinct endpoint and counts none, so a user stores hundreds of endpoints on one public https host they run (or a third party's). One thread a node sends all chat pushes, one after another, each a blocking POST with a 10 second timeout. An endpoint that holds the request and answers 201 just inside the timeout is "delivered" and never disabled; one that never answers is tried 12 times. `send#TestPush` queues a push to the endpoint the caller names every 10 seconds, each device on its own, so the user needs nobody else: 500 slow endpoints fill a round (`PUSHES_PER_ROUND`) for about 5,000 seconds, and the next call fills the next. While that goes on no other user's chat push leaves this node's worker. The same rows make the server send a 4 KiB POST to the named host for every message in the user's rooms, once per stored endpoint.
- **Evidence:** `web_push.rs:222-233` `let existing = facade.find(SUBSCRIPTION)?.eq("endpointHash", Value::string(&hash)).one()?; let mut row = match &existing { Some(row) => row.clone(), None => { let mut row = facade.make_value(SUBSCRIPTION)?; row.set_sequenced_id_primary(facade)?;` (no count of the user's rows before it)
- **Evidence:** `web_push.rs:71` `const TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10);`; `:1116-1119` `moqui_lib::http::RestClient::new().deny_internal_addresses(true).timeout(TIMEOUT).redirects(0, false)`
- **Evidence:** `chat_push.rs:323-325` `std::thread::Builder::new().name("moqui-chat-push".to_string()).spawn(move || {`; `:113` `const PUSHES_PER_ROUND: i64 = 500;`; `:1098-1099` `for row in rows.values() { match deliver_one(facade, transport, row, node, now) {`
- **Evidence:** `chat_push_transport.rs:159` `200..=299 => Sent::Delivered,`; `:144-150` `Err(error) => { ... Sent::Retry { after_millis: None, error, } }`
- **Evidence:** `chat_push.rs:1854-1855` `} else if let Some(endpoint) = param(parameters, "endpoint") { find = find.eq("endpointHash", Value::string(super::web_push::endpoint_hash(&endpoint)));`; `:1865` `if test_too_soon(facade, &subscriptions, now)? {` (per device, not per user)
- **Not confirmed:** not run. That `RestClient`'s timeout is a whole-request deadline (a slow answer may be cut sooner or later). Whether a call's ring (`send#WebPush`, a `special()` call in `Calls.js:361`, `:909`), which loops the callee's subscriptions the same way (`web_push.rs:899-920`), runs on the caller's request thread. The cluster case (each node has a worker, and a leased row is skipped by the others). No cap on a user's subscriptions was found in `store_subscription`, `register_device`, the service definitions (`NotificationServices.xml:497-509`) or the screen, no per-user or per-host limit in `deliver_due`, and no web-protection rule on `/WebPush/store`; `register#PushDevice` does hold a device key to one row (`web_push.rs:435-442`), so the native path is bounded by the user's device keys.
- **Suggested fix:** Cap a user's active Web Push subscriptions (ten or so; the oldest goes) and one test push a user per 10 seconds. Send pushes on a small pool with a per-host concurrency limit and a short connect timeout, and count a send that takes most of the timeout as a failure toward `MAX_FAILURES`.
- **Re-check:** Read `chat_push.rs:318-328` (one `moqui-chat-push` thread) and `:1092-1102` (rows delivered one after another by `deliver_one`). Not read: `web_push.rs:190-274` (the subscription count), the post timeout, the endpoint host rules.

#### SEC-COM-82. An XMPP client can send a stanza far over the 256 KiB limit, before it signs in, and the server keeps it in memory

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-xmpp/src/framing.rs:90-104`
- **Reached by:** a network peer of `xmpp_port` or `xmpp_tls_port` (or an edge node's XMPP tunnel ports), before STARTTLS and before SASL; and any signed-in XMPP user with no time limit. Not over `/xmpp-websocket`, where the frame size is held by the socket (`xmpp/ws.rs:57-58`).
- **What happens:** `feed` counts the bytes the parser took toward `max_stanza` unless the call is "between stanzas". It decides that from `tree.depth() == 1` and a `NeedMoreData` answer. minidom's builder pushes an element on its stack only at the end of its start tag, so the depth is still 1 through the whole start tag of a top-level stanza. A read that ends in the middle of an attribute is `NeedMoreData` at depth 1: its bytes were taken by the parser and are not counted. The call that completes the attribute counts only the bytes of that call. A client that sends `<iq a1='` and 8,000 bytes, then `' a2='` and 8,000 bytes, and so on, each in its own TCP segment, is counted a few bytes for every 8 KiB. rxml's raw parser does not refuse an element for its number of attributes, and each attribute (a token of up to 8,192 bytes) is kept in the builder's map until the tag ends, which the client never sends. A connection that has not signed in lasts 60 to 90 seconds, 20 from one address, so the growth is what the peer can send in that time on each; a signed-in session has no such end (only the 600 second idle timer, which every read resets).
- **Evidence:** `framing.rs:94-104` `let parsed = self.parser.parse(&mut data, false); // whitespace between stanzas is not counted (rxml bounds a token it holds back) let between = self.opened && self.tree.depth() == 1 && matches!(parsed, Ok(Some(RawEvent::Text(..))) | Err(EndOrError::NeedMoreData)); if !between { self.in_stanza += before - data.len(); if self.in_stanza > self.max_stanza { return Err(FramingError::TooLarge); } }`
- **Evidence:** `minidom-0.19.0/src/tree_builder.rs:59-61` `pub fn depth(&self) -> usize { self.stack.len() }`; `:123-137` `RawEvent::ElementHeadOpen(_, (prefix, name)) => { ... self.next_tag = Some((` (nothing pushed); `:151-153` `(None, name) => { attrs.insert(Namespace::NONE, name, value.as_str().to_owned()); }`; `:158` and `:186-187` `RawEvent::ElementHeadClose(_) => { ... let el = Element::new(...); self.stack.push(el);`
- **Evidence:** `rxml-0.14.0/src/parser/common.rs:103` `max_token_length: 8192,`; `parser/raw.rs:298-300` "the following cases are not detected and must be handled by consumer code: - Duplicate attributes"
- **Evidence:** `conn.rs:45` `pub const SIGN_IN_WITHIN: Duration = Duration::from_secs(60);`; `crates/moqui-host/src/xmpp/mod.rs:59` `const PER_SOURCE: usize = 20;`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. That a `parse` call that answers `NeedMoreData` has consumed the bytes it was given (read from the lexer's scratchpad design, `lexer/mod.rs:655-700`, not from a run). How reads fall on a TLS stream (a record is up to 16 KiB, the read buffer 16 KiB, so two attributes that land in one read are counted whole: the client paces its writes). No other byte or attribute count was found in `feed`, in `conn.rs` `read_frames` and `run`, in rxml's raw parser (`event_length` is only an overflow check, `raw.rs:440-448`) or in minidom's builder, and no memory limit per connection. The depth check (`framing.rs:126`) and the count inside nested elements (depth 2 and more) do hold.
- **Suggested fix:** Count every byte the parser takes while a stanza is open: treat a call as between stanzas only when the builder has no start tag under way (track `ElementHeadOpen` to `ElementHeadClose` in `StreamReader`), or count all bytes and reset the count only when whitespace at depth 1 ends. A test: a start tag sent in 8 KiB attributes, one segment each, ends the stream at the limit.
- **Re-check:** Read `framing.rs:89-104`: bytes are not counted when the stream is open, the tree's depth is 1 and the parser answers `NeedMoreData`; a start tag cut in the middle of an attribute is that case if minidom's depth stays 1 until the tag closes and rxml consumed the bytes. Both rest on the crates, which the reviewer read and the lead did not; not run. A test settles it.

## Data layer and stored secrets

#### SEC-DAT-01. A find by request parameters takes conditions and order on password-hash and encrypted fields

Medium; confirmed (second check); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `search_form_map` takes no condition or order from request input on a password field, and no order, pattern, range or period on an encrypted field; an `equals` on an encrypted field stays for a screen's or script's own find and is refused on a generic find (`EntityFind::generic_input`: entity REST, Service REST, the data grid, `searchFormMap`'s `genericInput`, an `entity-find` whose entity name is expanded). Tests: `search_form_tests` `request_input_takes_no_condition_or_order_on_a_secret_or_encrypted_field`, `actions_tests` `a_generic_finds_search_form_takes_no_condition_on_an_encrypted_field`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/search_form.rs:189-195`, `:86-92`; callers `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/query_rest.rs:561-563`, `crates/moqui-core/src/service/rest.rs:708-711`, `:752-755`, `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/entities.rs:193-197`
- **Reached by:** a signed-in user with view authorization on `moqui.security.UserAccount`, on a view that aliases its fields (`UserAccountAndGroup`), or on any entity with an encrypted field, through `GET /rest/e1/<entity>?currentPassword_op=begins&currentPassword=<prefix>`, a Service REST entity `list` or `count`, or a screen with `<search-form-inputs>` (Tools `System/Security/UserAccount/UserAccountList.xml:39-40`).
- **What happens:** The result omits the password fields, but the condition is applied, so the row count answers whether a user's stored hash starts with the given text. Repeating it reads the whole argon2 string, or a `resetPassword` or `passwordSalt`. `orderByField=currentPassword` sorts by the hash. On an encrypted field an `equals` condition is sealed with the constant IV, so it confirms a guessed value.
- **Evidence:** `search_form.rs:189-193` `for field in fields { let name = field.name.as_str(); if skip.contains(name) { continue; } ... if input.contains_key(name) || input.contains_key(&op_key) {`; `entities.rs:197` `return this.searchFormMap(inputs, null, null, second, third === true);` (no skip list); `query_rest.rs:228-231` strips the result only; `grid.rs:316-318` is the one caller that refuses (`hidden_value`).
- **Not confirmed:** which groups other than `ADMIN` hold view authorization on a `UserAccount` entity or view in a shipped component (the base seed gives entity REST none; mr-boss screens were not searched beyond a grep of `search-form-inputs`).
- **Suggested fix:** In `search_form_map`, skip `sensitive::secret_fields` of the entity and its encrypted fields for conditions and order, as `grid.rs` `hidden_value` does, unless the caller names the field explicitly.
- **Re-check:** The mechanism is confirmed: no field filter in `search_form.rs`, `sensitive.rs` or the REST callers, which pass an empty skip list; `grid.rs` `hidden_value` is the one caller that refuses. On an encrypted field `equals` confirms a guessed value; a `begins` pattern is sealed whole and is no prefix test. Who other than `ADMIN` holds view on a `UserAccount` entity or view was not traced.

#### SEC-DAT-02. An encrypted field in a data document is indexed in plaintext

Medium; likely (second check); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `document.rs` `document_info` leaves a `DataDocumentField` on an encrypted or `UserAccount` password field out of the document, its feed and the index, with a warning naming it, and Mantle's `MantleParty` no longer lists `identifications:idValue`. Documents indexed before keep the number until the `mantle` index is rebuilt. Tests: `document_tests.rs` `a_document_leaves_out_encrypted_fields_and_takes_only_the_listed_functions`, `mr-mantle/test/search/MantleSearch.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/data/MantleDocumentData.xml:55`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/query_rest.rs:1414`; `crates/moqui-db/src/model/view.rs:549`
- **Reached by:** anyone who can search the `mantle` index for parties, and anyone who reads the search store or a dump that includes it.
- **What happens:** `MantleParty` lists `identifications:idValue`, which is `PartyIdentification.idValue` (`encrypt="true"`, a party's identification number). The document's view alias inherits `encrypt`, the feed reads the view with authorization disabled, and each row is decrypted before the document is built. The `MantleSearch` feed pushes it to `index#DataDocuments` in real time. Nothing in `document.rs` or `sensitive.rs` leaves an encrypted or password field out of a document.
- **Evidence:** `MantleDocumentData.xml:55` `<fields fieldSeqId="18" fieldPath="identifications:idValue"/>`; `PartyEntities.xml:462` `<field name="idValue" type="text-medium" encrypt="true"/>`; `query_rest.rs:1414` `let row = field_rw::after_read(facade, &self.view, mapped)?;`; `document.rs:864` `let _authz = (crate::hooks::get().disable_authz)();`; `MantleDocumentData.xml:380-383`.
- **Not confirmed:** `crates/moqui-core/src/search/` was not read, so whether the index writer drops any field, and who may query the index, are not traced. `grep` finds no `encrypt` in `document.rs`, `search/feed.rs`, `search/mod.rs` or `search/query.rs`.
- **Suggested fix:** Leave encrypted fields and `sensitive::secret_fields` out of a document when it is built, or refuse a `DataDocumentField` on one; remove `identifications:idValue` from `MantleParty`.
- **Re-check:** `crates/moqui-report/src/business.rs:790-820` refuses an encrypted field for a report dataset only; nothing does so for a document or the feed. The index write in `crates/moqui-core/src/search/` and who may query the index were not read.

#### SEC-DAT-03. REST entity reads return stored credentials in plaintext

Medium; confirmed (lead); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: entity REST (`find_rest`, `RecordGuard::Answer`) and Service REST `find_entity` answer no encrypted field, only `<field>IsSet` (true or false), in nested records too. Tests: moqui-entity `generic_reads_leave_out_restricted_related_rows_and_answer_encrypted_fields_as_set`, `rest_tests.rs` `entity_resources_answer_encrypted_fields_as_set_and_read_a_page_at_most`.
- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/tools/service/moqui.rest.xml:123-126`, `:179-183`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/rest.rs:714-723`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/query_rest.rs:499-544`
- **Reached by:** a member of `ADMIN` (the `/moqui` REST path grant) with `GET /rest/s1/moqui/email/servers`, `GET /rest/s1/moqui/entity/syncs`; and any caller with `AT_ENTITY` view on an entity that has an encrypted field, with `GET /rest/e1/<entity>`.
- **What happens:** The reply holds `EmailServer.mailPassword`, `.smtpPassword`, `.oauthRefreshToken` and `EntitySync.targetPassword` decrypted. Entity REST refuses the restricted entities (`EmailServer`, `SystemMessageRemote`, `DatabaseConnection`, `UserAuthcFactor`) but has no check of `encrypt`, so the other encrypted fields (`LlmEndpoint.apiKey`, `GitRepository.accessToken`, `OauthFlow.secret`, `InstanceHost.password`, `AcmeCertificate.privateKeyPem`, `CreditCard.cardNumber`) come back in plaintext. The map says an administrator reads only whether a secret is set.
- **Evidence:** `service/rest.rs:660-666` only identity-admin and audit entities are refused for `list`, `one`, `count`, with the comment "secret-config reads are allowed on a declared resource"; `:717-721` `row.fields().iter().filter(|(_, value)| !value.is_null()).map(...)`; `query_rest.rs` has no occurrence of `encrypt`.
- **Not confirmed:** whether the `default` master of `EmailServer` or `EntitySync` narrows the fields (the `list` methods use no master).
- **Suggested fix:** Mask or drop `encrypt` fields in `render_record` and in `find_entity`, as the grid does, and answer a `<field>IsSet` flag.
- **Re-check:** Read `crates/moqui-core/src/service/rest.rs` (the entity `list` and `one` of a declared resource return every non-null field; only identity-admin and audit entities are refused) and `moqui.rest.xml` (`email/servers` is `EmailServer` `list`). Confirmed for Service REST, likely for Entity REST (which groups hold entity view on entities with encrypted fields was not traced). Read `run_entity`, `find_entity`, the `sensitive.rs` lists and `query_rest.rs` `rest`: neither REST path uses `field.encrypt`; only the five `UserAccount` password fields are stripped. `SecurityRest.spec.js:337-346` shows a non-ADMIN fixture with the `MOQUI_API` grant listing `/rest/s1/moqui/email/servers`. The public demo keeps `ADMIN` view on `/moqui`.

#### SEC-DAT-04. A view alias function from a database row may be any SQL function name

Medium; likely (second check); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: a `DbViewEntityAlias` or `DataDocumentField` `functionName` must be one of `derived.rs` `ROW_VIEW_FUNCTIONS` (min, max, sum, avg, count, count-distinct, upper, lower); another refuses the view or the document (`check_row_function`), and the report engine's `FUNCTIONS` is that list. Views defined in XML keep `check_word`. Tests: moqui-entity `a_db_view_alias_takes_only_the_listed_functions`, `document_tests.rs`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-db/src/sql/view.rs:1195-1209`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/derived.rs:295-296`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/document.rs:627`; `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Views/EditDbView.xml:61`, `:117`
- **Reached by:** a user who can write `DbViewEntityAlias` or `DataDocumentField` rows (Tools Data Views and Data Documents; `Dashboards/DatasetFields.xml:20` also passes `functionName`), then any find of that view.
- **What happens:** `functionName` is checked to be one word and is written as `<NAME>(<column>)`. On the embedded cluster the connection is the bootstrap superuser, so a function such as `pg_read_file` or `pg_ls_dir` runs on a column the same user can fill. The SQL runner has its own permission (`DATABASE_ADMIN`) and audit; this path has neither.
- **Evidence:** `view.rs:1204-1206` `fn check_word ... function.chars().all(|ch| ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || ch == '_')`; `:1200` `Ok(format!("{}(", function.to_ascii_uppercase()))`; `EditDbView.xml:117` `if (functionName) aliasValue.functionName = functionName`.
- **Not confirmed:** who is authorized for `EditDbView.xml`, the Dashboards dataset screen and `create#DataDocumentField` (only `ADMIN` was assumed); whether a later check rejects a non-aggregate function on a plain column.
- **Suggested fix:** For views built from rows (`derived.rs`), accept only a fixed list (`min`, `max`, `sum`, `avg`, `count`, `count-distinct`, `upper`, `lower`).
- **Re-check:** The Dashboards dataset path is refuted (`business.rs:889-892` accepts only its `FUNCTIONS` list). The Tools Data Views and Data Documents paths stand: no allow list in `derived.rs`, `document.rs` or `model/view.rs`. Who is authorized for those Tools screens, and whether Postgres runs a non-aggregate function there, were not tested.

#### SEC-DAT-81. A Marble user names any document type in a search and reads mail subjects, chat text and telemetry of every user

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Search.xml:19-23`, `QuickSearch.xml:41-46`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/GeneralServices.xml:238-286`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/template/party/PartyForms.xml:75-76`, `:110-111`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/template/product/ProductTransitions.xml:29-30`, `:80-82`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/search/SearchServices.xml:140-141`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/search.rs:138-153`. Report: round 3 `B10.md`.
- **Reached by:** a signed-in user who may open Marble's QuickSearch (`mr-marble/screen/marble.xml:34`; the seed gives Marble to `ADMIN` and `MarbleErpAdmin`, and defines `MarbleErpApp`, with `Search.xml`, "for all non-admin user groups", `mr-marble/data/ZmeMarbleErpSetupData.xml:26-41`), with `documentType=<any id>&queryString=<query>`; or one who may open a Marble screen that includes `searchPartyList` or `getProductList`, with `documentType=` and `term=`.
- **What happens:** The screen passes its whole context to `search#MantleFiltered`. That service adds an organization filter only when `documentType` is empty, `MantleParty` or `MantleProduct`; for any other type it adds none and calls `search#DataDocuments`, which drops `indexName` when a type is given and searches the type named, with no authorization check of the document's primary entity. `ec.search` refuses only browser replays. So `MoquiEmailMessage`, `MoquiChatEvent`, `MoquiDriveFile`, the `mantle_sales` and `mantle_inventory` documents, and the telemetry stores (`otel_spans`, `otel_logs` and the others, which resolve without a `DataDocument` row) are all searched, though none is in the `mantle` index. The result list shows each hit's id and its `documentTitle`: `${subject}` for mail, `${bodyText}` for a chat message. The query string takes `field:value`, prefixes and wildcards on any indexed field, so text that is not shown (a mail body, a log line, a span's `url.query`) is read by asking. The lookups `searchPartyList` and `getProductList` take `documentType` from the request first and answer ids and a label: a yes or no for each query. `MailSearchData.xml:20` says the mail index is searched "so a user never sees another user's mail"; that rule is in `EmailClientServices`, not on this path.
- **Evidence:** `Search.xml:22` `<service-call name="mantle.GeneralServices.search#MantleFiltered" in-map="context" out-map="context"/>`; `QuickSearch.xml:45` the same
- **Evidence:** `GeneralServices.xml:257-259` `if (!documentType) entityFilterSetId = filterSetFor('mantle.product.Product') || filterSetFor('mantle.party.Party'); else if (documentType === 'MantleParty') ...; else if (documentType === 'MantleProduct') ...`; `:283-284` `<service-call name="org.moqui.search.SearchServices.search#DataDocuments" ... in-map="Object.assign({}, context, {flattenDocument:false})"/>`
- **Evidence:** `SearchServices.xml:141` `const target = documentType ? {documentType: documentType} : {indexName: indexName};`
- **Evidence:** `host/search.rs:143` `if documents.iter().any(|id| id == search::telemetry::REPLAYS) {` (the only refusal); `search/mod.rs:696` `if let Some(mapping) = telemetry::telemetry_mapping(data_document_id) {`
- **Evidence:** `MailSearchData.xml:22-23` `dataDocumentId="MoquiEmailMessage" ... documentTitle="${subject}"`; `ChatSetupData.xml:33-34` `dataDocumentId="MoquiChatEvent" ... documentTitle="${bodyText}"`
- **Evidence:** `PartyForms.xml:76` `<set field="documentType" from="documentType || searchPartyDocType || 'MantleParty'"/>` (the request parameter first); `ProductTransitions.xml:30` the same for products; their answer is `{value: document._id, label: ...}`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. That a request parameter `documentType` reaches the service through `in-map="context"` in this runtime (it is the form's own field, `Search.xml:28-36`); how `SearchResults` renders a row whose type has no `DataDocument` row (telemetry), where `${dataDocument.documentName}` has nothing to read; which groups hold `MarbleErpApp` in a real install. The reviewer rates it High where Marble is given to ordinary staff. Looked for and not found: an authorization check of the document's primary entity in `search#DataDocuments` (`search#Universal` has one, `UniversalSearchServices.xml:156`); a check that `documentType` belongs to `indexName`; a list of allowed types in the Marble screens (the drop-down lists the `mantle` documents, the service takes any text); a refusal of the telemetry targets in `ec.search`.
- **Suggested fix:** In `search#DataDocuments`, refuse a `documentType` that is not a `DataDocument` of `indexName`, and in `ec.search` refuse the telemetry targets unless the caller is the telemetry reader. In `search#MantleFiltered`, `searchPartyList` and `getProductList`, take the type from a fixed list (the screen's own setting), check view authorization on its primary entity, and leave out a type that has no organization scope, as `search#Universal` does.
- **Re-check:** Read mr-marble `QuickSearch.xml:41-46` (it calls `search#MantleFiltered` with `in-map="context"`, so a request's `documentType` reaches it) and mr-mantle `GeneralServices.xml:238-260` (an organization filter set only for no type, `MantleParty` or `MantleProduct`). Not read by the lead: whether `ec.search` applies a document's entity authorization (`search.rs:143`).

#### SEC-DAT-82. Universal search counts the records it then hides, so a query tells what a hidden record says

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/search/UniversalSearchServices.xml:257-259`, `:273-323`, `:378-380`, `:384-390`. Report: round 3 `B10.md`.
- **Reached by:** any signed-in mr-boss user, through `Commons/Search` (`q`, `entityType`; `mr-boss/screen/Commons/Search.xml:21`, `:40-42`) and the palette; and `GET /rest/s1/mantle/search`.
- **What happens:** The trusted filter holds the organization and party limits. The rules that hide single records inside an organization run afterwards, one hit at a time, and skip a dropped hit with `continue`: a row the entity filters refuse, a confidential HR case, a hidden applicant, a protected People task, the task of a confidential Smithy issue, a maintenance work order or request. `total` and the `entityType` and `category` facet counts still come from the index, except in the three cases the code names (bound mailboxes, one-sided finance, payroll), where the total becomes the rows shown. A lexical query takes `field:value` and prefixes. So `q=name:acq*` with `entityType=MantleTask` answers a total of 1 with no results, and the text of a hidden record is read a prefix at a time.
- **Evidence:** `:258` `total = page.total`; `:273` `if (current == null) continue`; `:302-303` `...hiddenApplicants([current.partyId]).length) continue`; `:314-315` `check#TaskHidden ... ?.hidden !== false) continue`; `:318-319` `.workHidden(current.workEffortId, userPartyId)) continue`; `:322-323` `.requestHidden(current, userPartyId)) continue`
- **Evidence:** `:378-380` `const liveTypes = [...new Set((liveMailboxCounts ? ['MantleCommunicationEvent'] : []).concat(financeDropped || !bothSides ? sidedTypes : []).concat(payrollTypes))]` / `if (liveTypes.length) { total = results.length; totalRelation = 'gte' }`; `:387-388` the facet count over the `indexed` types from `ec.search.aggregate`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. That `Commons/Search` shows `total` and the facet counts to the user (`Search.xml:45-48` sets them; the widgets were not read); that a hidden task's or applicant's document passes the caller's organization limit (it does when its owner or a party is one of the caller's organizations, the case the per-hit rules exist for). Looked for and not found: a recount after the per-hit checks for these types (only the three named cases); the hide rules as clauses of the trusted filter (only the organization, private-contact, mailbox and wiki clauses are there, `:196-215`).
- **Suggested fix:** When any hit of a type was dropped, or for every type that has a per-hit rule, report the page's count for that type, as the code already does for `liveTypes`. Better, put each hide rule in the trusted filter (a field in the document that marks it), so the index never counts the record.
- **Re-check:** Read `UniversalSearchServices.xml:257-259` (`total = page.total`, from the index) and `:378-380` (only the live types reset the total to the rows shown).

#### SEC-DAT-83. A load's `disableEntityEca` and `disableAuditLog` switch entity ECA rules and the audit log off for every thread, and two overlapping loads can leave them off

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:1886-1889`, `:1919-1921`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/facade.rs:123`, `:515-525`, `:788-791`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/load_feed/eca.rs:65`; `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/field_rw.rs:327`. Report: round 3 `B11.md`.
- **Reached by:** a holder of `all` on `Tools/Data/Snapshots` who starts `importSnapshot`, whose three switches default to true (`runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Snapshots.xml:66-67`, `EntityServices.xml:300-303`, `:311-313`); then any user who writes while the import runs.
- **What happens:** `load_data` sets the flags on the entity facade, which is one object shared by all threads, runs the load (a snapshot import has a 3600 s timeout), and then sets them back to what it read first. While they are set, `match_rules_with` returns no rule for any write on any thread, so the rules of `Security.eecas.xml` do not run: the privileged-group check (`UserGroupMemberPrivileged`), the sealed-permission checks (`UserGroupMemberSealed`, `UserGroupPermissionSealed`) and the `DataDocumentFieldExpression` check, which nothing else enforces (`assertPrivilegedGroupMembership` is called only from the ECA file). No audited change of any user is written to `EntityAuditLog`, and no data feed row is made. And the restore is not nested: a load B that starts inside load A reads `before` as true and, ending after A, writes true back; the flags then stay set until the process restarts.
- **Evidence:** `tools.rs:1886` `let before = facade.flags();`, `:1887` `facade.set_disable_entity_eca(flag("disableEntityEca") || before.disable_entity_eca);`, `:1888` `facade.set_disable_audit_log(flag("disableAuditLog") || before.disable_audit_log);`
- **Evidence:** `tools.rs:1919` `facade.set_disable_entity_eca(before.disable_entity_eca);`
- **Evidence:** `facade.rs:789` `let mut flags = write(&self.inner.flags);`
- **Evidence:** `eca.rs:65` `if facade.flags().disable_entity_eca || (crate::hooks::get().entity_eca_disabled)() {` and `:66` `return Vec::new();`
- **Evidence:** `field_rw.rs:327` `if operation == "delete" || facade.flags().disable_audit_log || facade.sim_active() {`
- **Evidence:** `Security.eecas.xml:18` `<actions><script>UserSecurityChecks.assertPrivilegedGroupMembership(userGroupId)</script></actions>`
- **Not confirmed:** confirmed in code for the flags, likely for a use of the window. Which reachable service a user who is not in `ADMIN_ADV` would use to write `UserGroupMember` while a load runs was not traced (`AccessServices.store#Membership` and the entity-auto services are candidates). Looked for and not found: a per-thread or per-context form of the flags in `load_data` (the execution context has one for ECA, `entity_eca_disabled`, `entity_hooks.rs:36`, which `load_data` does not use); a second enforcement of the privileged-group and sealed-permission checks in a service (a grep of `assert_privileged_group_membership`, `assert_sealed_permission_grant`); a lock that keeps two loads apart.
- **Suggested fix:** Carry the three switches on the thread (a guard like `FkScope`, `loader.rs:78`) or on the execution context, not on the facade; never let a switch skip the `UserSecurityChecks` rules (`service/eca.rs:264` `is_security_check` already tells them apart).
- **Re-check:** Read `script/host/tools.rs:1886-1889` (the facade's ECA, audit and feed flags are set from the request) and `:1919-1921` (the values read before are restored), so of two loads that overlap the later restore can leave them off. Not read by the lead: that the facade's flags are process-wide.

## PASS frontend (mr-pass)

#### SEC-PSF-01. In local mode a login key in a header drives every PASS page, without the session token

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: in local mode mr-pass `Facade.js` `checkLoginKey` applies the authority's login-key rule: a request with an `api_key` or `login_key` header, a user signed in by a login key (`ec.user.loginKeyKind`) or a session a header login started (`moquiHeaderLogin`) is refused unless the key is a device's and the operation is one of `PassAuthority.js` `APP_KEY_OPERATIONS`; an access token is scope `git` and signs in to no PASS page (SEC-PFS-61). Test: `mr-pass/test/Forum/ForumAccess.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `service/pass/Facade.js:25-32`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/mod.rs:1123-1128`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:1702`; the rule that is skipped is `mr-mantle/script/PassAuthority.js:379-386`.
- **Reached by:** anyone holding an account's unscoped login key (a Smithy access token made for git, an installed app's device key, a session's key), with any POST to a PASS page transition on a site served in local mode, sending the key as `api_key` or `login_key`.
- **What happens:** The authority's rule is that a login key calls only the installed app's operations and only as a device key, and an access token calls none (`APP_KEY_OPERATIONS`). That rule is in `run()`, which a local-mode page never calls: the page's transition calls the Mantle service directly as the key's user. A header login also skips the transition's session-token check. So a git access token can post in the forum, change the account's settings, read its invoices, orders and private messages, and run every Smithy form that asks for no password; in remote mode the same token is refused.
- **Evidence:** `PassAuthority.js:379-386` `if ((p.headerKey || p.headerLogin) && (keyKind(p.headerKey) !== 'device' || !APP_KEY_OPERATIONS.includes(operation))) fail('Unauthorized', "accessDenied: A login key calls only an installed app's operations, and an access token none; sign in to the site")`
- **Evidence:** `Facade.js:26-32` `if (!backend) return ec.service.sync().throwOnError(true).name(def.service).parameters({ input }).call().result`
- **Evidence:** `screen/mod.rs:1123-1127` `match user::apply_header_keys(request.header("api_key"), request.header("login_key")) { ... Ok(true) => { user::note_header_login(); return Ok((None, true)); }`; `user/login.rs:613-616` `if !key_scope.is_empty() && Some(key_scope.as_str()) != scope { return Err(...) }` (a key with no scope passes); `render.rs:1702` `if !job.header_authenticated && !job.new_session && csrf_rejected(job, &transition)? {`
- **Evidence:** grep for `loginKeyKind`, `moquiHeaderLogin`, `headerLogin` in `mr-pass` (outside `test/`): no match; in `mr-mantle/service`: no match.
- **Not confirmed:** that the access tokens `mantle.smithy.SmithyServices.create#AccessToken` makes are `UserLoginKey` rows with an empty `scope` (the comment at `PassAuthority.js:306-309` says an access token is a key with a description; the service was not read). Whether any Mantle service checks the key kind itself (the grep above found none). `addSshKey` and `createAccessToken` take `password` and `code` fields (`SmithySchema.js:239-250`), so new credentials may need re-entry; not traced.
- **Suggested fix:** Apply the login-key rule where both modes pass: in `Facade.call` when `pass_backend_url` is empty (refuse when `ec.user.loginKeyKind` is set, or the session came by a header login, unless the operation is in `APP_KEY_OPERATIONS` and the key is a device key), or in a shared Mantle helper each operation's entry calls.

## PASS authority and public accounts

#### SEC-PSA-01. PASS sign-in has no rate limit on the authority

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `startSession` and `completeSession` of `public/Account.js` call `throttle('session', userName)`: 20 sign-ins a requester (a registered client's forwarded address, else the peer) and 10 a user name in ten minutes, refused with "Wait before signing in again" before any password is checked; the session and challenge paths are in the `login` rate limit. Test: `framework/test/PassSignInThrottle.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `FW/service/org/moqui/public/Account.js:536-537`; `FW/conf/MoquiDefaultConf.xml:869`; `runtime/conf/pass/MoquiAuthorityConf.xml:30-37`
- **Reached by:** anonymous, `POST /rest/s1/pass/v1/sites/{any}/account/session` (or `account/challenge`) with a business-host session and its `x-csrf-token`; or through a PASS frontend, whose calls carry no session token.
- **What happens:** Registration, verification and recovery are counted per requester and per address; sign-in is not. The web protection's `login` rule names `/Login`, `/rest/login` and `/SecondFactor` only, and the example authority conf exempts the PASS servers' addresses from web protection, so their visitors' failed sign-ins meet neither the rate limit nor the `auth-failure` ban. What is left is the account's own failed-login count, which does not stop one guess each across many accounts, staff accounts included (one `UserAccount` table).
- **Evidence:** `if (op === 'startSession' || op === 'completeSession') return startSession(p)` (no `throttle`), against `if (op === 'beginRegistration') { throttle('registration', p.emailAddress)`; `<rate-limit name="login" path="/Login/**,/rest/login,/rest/login/**,/SecondFactor/**" methods="POST" requests="10" seconds="60" burst="10"/>`; `<client-rule name="pass-servers" action="exempt" cidr="10.0.2.0/24"/>` with the comment "the PASS servers are exempt, or all their visitors would count as one client".
- **Not confirmed:** the PASS frontend's own limits (slice 14). The `all` rule (50 a second) and the `auth-failure` ban still count a direct caller when web protection is on (`webapp_web_protection` is empty by default; on with `webapp_trusted_proxies`, per the conf comment).
- **Suggested fix:** Call `throttle('session')` in `startSession` (per requester key, and per user name), as the other account actions do, and add the servlet's `account/session` and `account/challenge` paths to the `login` rule.

## PASS application services (Mantle)

#### SEC-PSS-01. A download product's file location is not restricted

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `ShopFulfillment.js` `servedDownload` applies `Content.js` `owned` (under the content root, in `product/<productId>/`, no `..`) to a download's file, so `get#Downloads`, `issue#Download` and `get#DownloadFile` answer NOT_AVAILABLE for any other location, and `create#ProductContent` and `Content.js` `update` refuse such a location for a download row. The entity-auto create (mr-marble `createContentWithLocation`) still stores any location, which nothing serves as a download. Test: `mr-mantle/test/product/StoreFulfillment.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `M/product/ShopFulfillment.js:118`, `:525`, `:612`; `M/product/ProductServices.xml:937`, `:958`
- **Reached by:** a staff user who may call `mantle.product.ProductServices.create#ProductContent` (or update the row), then any buyer of that product through `getShopDownload`.
- **What happens:** The file a granted download streams is `ProductContent.contentLocation` of a `PcntDownload` row. `create#ProductContent` takes `contentLocation` as an input and stores it as given. The download path applies no scheme or path rule, and the frontend opens the location with authorization off, so a row naming `file:///...` or a `component://` path would stream a server file to the buyer. The product image path has exactly this guard and the download path does not.
- **Evidence:** `M/product/ShopFulfillment.js:118` `rows("mantle.product.ProductContent", {productId, productContentTypeEnumId: "PcntDownload"})` then `.filter(row => lib.active(row) && row.contentLocation)`
- **Evidence:** `M/product/ShopFulfillment.js:612` `location: content.contentLocation,`
- **Evidence:** `M/product/ProductServices.xml:937` `<parameter name="contentLocation"/>`; `:958` `<service-call name="update#mantle.product.ProductContent" in-map="{productContentId:productContentId, contentLocation:contentLocation}"/>`
- **Evidence:** `M/product/ShopCatalog.js:341` `/^(?:dbresource|component):\/\//.test(String(row.contentLocation)) &&` (the image path's rule)
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-core/src/context/locate.rs:88` `/// A local path for a classpath://, component://, file:, or plain location.`
- **Not confirmed:** whether the reference a script opens refuses `file:` or absolute paths (the read path in `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs` was not traced); which permission `create#ProductContent` needs; whether the same staff user can already read such a location through a BOSS content download.
- **Suggested fix:** Apply the image rule to `PcntDownload` rows in `downloadContents` and `getDownloadFile` (a `dbresource://` location under the content root, no `..`), and refuse a caller-given `contentLocation` in `create#ProductContent`.

#### SEC-PSS-21. `createCustomerCase` leaves the staged upload on disk when the operation is a repeat

Medium; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `createCustomerCase` stages the file through a callback that `intakeCase` and `contactCase` call only after the form token or the asker is checked and after `lib.operation`'s repeat answer, and unstages it on any later error, so a repeat stages nothing and a caller with no credential stages nothing (SEC-PSS-22). Test: `mr-pass/test/Support/SupportAccess.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `request/SupportPortal.js:758-765`, `:775`, `:833`
- **Reached by:** anonymous, through a registered PASS frontend, with a queue's public form token (`CaseIntake`, the public NewCustomerCase link); also any signed-in customer account. Request: `createCustomerCase` (POST `support/cases`) with a `file`, repeating one `operationId` with the same name and email.
- **What happens:** the file is staged and scanned before the operation key is looked up. When the key was used before with the same request hash (the hash does not include the file), `intakeCase` and `contactCase` return the first result and nothing removes the staged file; the `catch` in `createCustomerCase` unstages only on an error. Each repeat leaves up to 10 MiB under `pass_media_root/support`, 30 times in ten minutes per requester address, with no further limit (the form's hourly and address limits are in `create#IntakeRequest`, which a repeat never reaches).
- **Evidence:** `:758` `const staged = media().stageGiven(p.file, "file", "support")`; `:759-765` `try { if (!blank(p.token)) return intakeCase(…); return contactCase(…) } catch (error) { media().unstage(staged); throw error }`; `:770-775` `const operation = lib.operation({operationKey: key}, "SupportCaseIntake", organizationPartyId, {token: …, name: p.requestName, email: p.emailAddress})` / `if (operation.old) return lib.previous(operation)`; `:833` `if (operation.old) return lib.previous(operation)`
- **Not confirmed:** a job that sweeps `pass_media_root/support` (none found by `grep -rn pass_media_root` in mr-mantle and `crates/`); the scanner must pass the file (a valid image passes the sniff).
- **Suggested fix:** look the operation up before staging, or unstage in a `finally` whenever the file was not kept.

#### SEC-PSS-41. An anonymous event reservation answers the directory name of the party that owns a typed email address

Medium; reported (confirmed in code; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `EventRegistration.attendeeName` keeps the name typed (the buyer's on the first place, the attendee's at completion and in the staff correction), and `MarketingPortal.js` `registrationView`, the ticket and the notice name an attendee by it, never by `partyName(attendeePartyId)`. A typed address no longer attaches the reservation to the directory party that has it; a signed-in account with that verified address claims it (SEC-PSS-61, fixed). Test: `mr-pass/test/Marketing/MarketingRegistration.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `service/mantle/marketing/MarketingPortal.js` reserveRegistration (starts 1058), registrationView (starts 961), subscriberOf (198-231); `service/mantle/work/EventRegistrationPlaces.js:76-81`, `:276-282`, `:394`, complete (starts 422)
- **Reached by:** anonymous, the PASS operation `reserveRegistration` on any site with a published, open event offer (`buyerEmail` is the input); also `completeRegistration` with an attendee's `email`.
- **What happens:** The buyer party is the first existing directory party with that current email address (not disabled, merged or private), else a new person. The first place's attendee is the buyer party, and the answer to the same request names each place's attendee with `partyName(attendeePartyId)`, the party's stored first and last name or organization name, not the name typed. A visitor learns whether an address is known to the organization's party directory and the name kept for it (a customer contact, a supplier contact, an employee). An attendee's address at completion is matched the same way.
- **Evidence:** `subscriberOf`: `if (party && party.disabled !== "Y" && !party.mergedToPartyId && !party.privateOwnerUserId) return {partyId: party.partyId, contactMechId: info.contactMechId}`
- **Evidence:** `EventRegistrationPlaces.js:394` `attendeePartyId: firstPlace ? p.buyer.partyId : null`
- **Evidence:** registrationView `attendeeName: row.attendeePartyId ? P.partyName(row.attendeePartyId) : ""`; reserveRegistration returns `registrationView(reservation, policy, "token", {buyerName: buyer.name})`
- **Evidence:** `EventRegistrationPlaces.js` complete `: personOf(person.name, person.address)` then `attendeePartyId: party.partyId`
- **Not confirmed:** the limits bound it (ten a client in ten minutes, five an address an hour, 500 a site an hour: `regLimits`), and it needs an open offer. The ticket and receipt mail text was not read for the same name.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the typed name on the registration (or the reservation) and show that, never the matched party's name; or do not match an unverified address to an existing party until the address is confirmed.

#### SEC-PSS-42. A site owner can publish any wiki page of an unrestricted space as the site's public page

Medium; reported (likely; a helper reader's item, not checked by the slice's reviewer); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `Forum.js` `publishSitePage` takes a page only from the site's `publishingWikiSpaceId`, or from a space the caller administers (`get#WikiPageAccess` `canAdmin`), with one refusal for a missing page, another space, no update access or another revision; `updateSite` refuses a publishing space the caller does not administer. Test: `mr-pass/test/Forum/ForumAccess.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `service/mantle/forum/Forum.js` publishSitePage (starts 3275), getSitePage (starts 1976); `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/resource/WikiServices.xml:1802-1808`
- **Reached by:** a signed-in public account that is an owner of a PASS site (an owner is named by an operator at create#Site and can make other members owners with set#SiteRole), the PASS operation `publishSitePage` (PATCH `pages/{pageRole}`) with a `wikiPageId` and `versionName`.
- **What happens:** The only checks beyond site ownership are the wiki's `canUpdate` for the page and that `versionName` equals the page's published version. `canUpdate` is true for any user account when neither the space nor the page has `restrictView` or `restrictUpdate` set, so the wiki's own rule does not separate a public account from staff; the page need not be in the site's publishing space or related to the site at all. The bound page's published text is then served to anonymous visitors by `getSitePage` (`guidelines` or `about`). The two refusals differ (`Wiki publication access required`, `Select the exact currently published wiki revision`), so the operation also tells an owner whether a page id exists and has that published version.
- **Evidence:** `Forum.js` publishSitePage `.name('org.moqui.resource.WikiServices.get#WikiPageAccess').parameters({ wikiPageId: p.wikiPageId })` and `if (!page || !p.versionName || page.publishedVersionName !== p.versionName) invalid(...)`; no check of the page's space
- **Evidence:** `WikiServices.xml:1802` `canView = adminGroup || ((space.restrictView !== 'Y' || ...) && (!page || page.restrictView !== 'Y' || ...))`; `:1807` `canUpdate = canView && !readOnly && (canAdmin || ((space.restrictUpdate !== 'Y' || spaceUser?.allowUpdate === 'Y') && (!page || page.restrictUpdate !== 'Y' || pageUser?.allowUpdate === 'Y')))`
- **Not confirmed:** how guessable `wikiPageId` and the published version name are (not traced); whether deployments make non-staff accounts site owners; whether internal wiki spaces are created restricted by default (the entity fields have no default: `ResourceEntities.xml:789-790`). `updateSite` has the related gap: an owner sets `publishingWikiSpaceId` to any existing space (`one('moqui.resource.wiki.WikiSpace', {wikiSpaceId: p.publishingWikiSpaceId})`, NotFound otherwise).
- **Suggested fix:** Require the page to be in a space bound to the site (the site's publishing space or a site page space) and require an internal party or an explicit wiki grant, not the wiki's default-open update rule.

#### SEC-PSS-61. An anonymous event reservation answers the stored name of the party that owns a typed email address

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-PSS-41: the answer names the attendee as typed, never the stored party's name. The other half is fixed too (the owner's decision of 2026-11-16): a typed address attaches nothing to the directory party that has it. `MarketingPortal.js` `reserveRegistration` names the buyer by `buyerOf`, a new person with the name and address typed, and `completeRegistration` passes `typedAddresses`, so each attendee's typed address is a new person too (`EventRegistrationPlaces.js` `personOf`); the reservation, its ticket order and its capability are the new person's. A signed-in account whose verified address (ExternalAccess `selfOf`) is the one typed claims it, live or not: `EventRegistrationClaims.js` `claim`, run by `MarketingPortal.js` `reach` before each registration page operation, merges the typed buyer or a live place's typed attendee into the account's party (`merge#Parties` as the system with `claimRegistration`, which lets `MarketingPartyMerge.js` move live registrations for a system merge only) and re-points the claimed buyer's `ExternalAccessCapability` rows, so the page's and the mailed links keep working; nothing is claimed for an unverified account, another address, a reservation with no live place or a person with anything besides the reservation. Staff entries under MARKETING_EVENT_MANAGE still name the directory party of an address, and PASS subscription requests still do (confirmed by their own mail). Commits mr-mantle 87102aed, 755b7116 (mr-pass 6b45a26 for the SEC-PSS-41 feature). Tests: `mr-mantle/test/work/EventRegistrations.spec.js` "a typed address attaches no reservation or attendee to the directory party that has it; a verified account's own does" and "a verified account claims a live reservation typed with its address, and the mailed links still work; others claim nothing".
- **Where:** runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/marketing/MarketingPortal.js:198 (subscriberOf), :1019, :1058; runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/work/EventRegistrationPlaces.js:76, :394
- **Reached by:** anonymous, the PASS operation `reserveRegistration` (a `TOKEN_WRITES` name) with `buyerEmail`, on any site with a published, open event offer.
- **What happens:** The buyer is the first existing directory party with that current email address, else a new person; the first place's attendee is that party, and the answer to the same request names the attendee with `partyName(attendeePartyId)`, the stored first and last name or organization name (or the party id), not the name typed. A visitor learns whether an address is known to the organization and the name kept for it, and the reservation, its order and its capability are attached to that existing party. This confirms the first report's unchecked `SITES-C1`.
- **Evidence:** `if (party && party.disabled !== "Y" && !party.mergedToPartyId && !party.privateOwnerUserId) return {partyId: party.partyId, contactMechId: info.contactMechId}`; `attendeePartyId: firstPlace ? p.buyer.partyId : null,`; `attendeeName: row.attendeePartyId ? P.partyName(row.attendeePartyId) : "",`; `if (person) return [person.firstName, person.lastName].filter(Boolean).join(" ") || partyId`
- **Not confirmed:** the guard found is the limits only (`regLimits`: 10 a client in ten minutes, 500 a site an hour, 5 an address an hour); the receipt and ticket mail were not read for the same name; `completeRegistration`'s attendee match was not read.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the typed name on the reservation and show that; do not attach an unverified address to an existing party until the address is confirmed.

#### SEC-PSS-81. A shopper applies any of the store's promo codes by its sequenced record id, codes customers are not to enter included, and the cart then shows the code's text

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/order/ShopCart.js:537-581`, `:384-395`, `:434-437`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/StorePromotions.js:668-671`, `:788-793`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/PromotionServices.xml:252-255`. Report: round 3 `C8.md`.
- **Reached by:** anonymous. Any visitor makes a cart with `addCartItem` and calls `applyPromoCode` (`POST shop/cart/codes`, `script/PassRoutes.js:236`, a `TOKEN_WRITES` operation, `script/PassAuthority.js:40`) with the cart capability; locally through `pass.Facade.call#Operation`.
- **What happens:** `storeCodeOf` looks the shopper's text up as a code's text, ignoring case, and also as its `promoCodeId`, and takes the first match whose promotion is of the shop's store. Codes made in Commerce get a sequenced `promoCodeId` (`store#PromoCode` and `generate#PromoCodes` create the row with no id), so a code whose text is 8 random characters (`drawCodes`) is applied by its neighbouring number. Nothing on this path reads the code's `userEntered` flag ("Customers enter it"), so a code staff meant to apply themselves is applied too. Once applied, the cart view lists the code by its text, so the guesser learns it, and a code with `useLimitPerCode` is used up for the customer it was sent to. What still limits it: 20 tries a requester in ten minutes (keyed by the address, so several addresses multiply it), `requireParty` codes (the party list), and the dates and use limits of `add#OrderPromoCode`. Mantle's own `add#OrderPromoCode` has the same id fallback, so Shop inherits it twice.
- **Evidence:** `ShopCart.js:545-549` `find("mantle.product.store.ProductStorePromoCode").condition(...makeCondition("promoCode", "equals", code).ignoreCase())` ... `.concat(Array.from(find("mantle.product.store.ProductStorePromoCode", {promoCodeId: code}).list()))`
