- **Evidence:** `ShopCart.js:539` `if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,40}$/.test(code))`; `:52` `codeTries: 20`; `:567` `externalAccess().throttle("cart-code", limits.codeTries, ...)`; `:573-576` `lib.call("mantle.product.PromotionServices.add#OrderPromoCode", {orderId: cart.row.orderId, promoCode: storeCode.promoCode})`
- **Evidence:** `ShopCart.js:434-435` `promoCodes: codeRows.map(code => ({code: code.promoCode || code.promoCodeId,`
- **Evidence:** `StorePromotions.js:668-671` `call("create#" + CODE, Object.assign({userEntered: "Y", requireParty: "N"}, values, {storePromotionId: promotion.storePromotionId}))` (no `promoCodeId`; `:788-793` the same for each generated code); `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/sequence.rs:252-258` the next id of the sequence bank, unless the entity or datasource uses UUIDs (`:239-246`)
- **Evidence:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/entity/ProductStoreEntities.xml:372` `<field name="userEntered" type="text-indicator"/>`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Shared/PromoCodeDetail.xml:184` `<option key="Y" text="Customers enter it"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** the instance's sequence settings (prefix, UUID, bank) for `ProductStorePromoCode`; whether any store uses `userEntered` N codes today. Looked for and not found: a check of `userEntered` in `ShopCart.js` (`grep userEntered` finds only `StorePromotions.js` and the Commerce screens) or in `add#OrderPromoCode` (`PromotionServices.xml:240-370`); a per-cart or per-store limit of tries (only the requester's); a code id that is not a sequence.
- **Suggested fix:** In `storeCodeOf` match the code's text only (drop the `promoCodeId` lookup), and refuse a code whose `userEntered` is not `Y`; answer the code as the shopper typed it. Drop the id fallback from `add#OrderPromoCode` too, or give it a parameter that Shop sets.
- **Re-check:** Read `order/ShopCart.js:544-556` (`storeCodeOf`): a shopper's text is matched as `promoCode` (ignoring case) and also as `promoCodeId`, and the only filter is the code's store; no test of whether customers may enter it in these lines. `:430-435` answers the code's text. Not read by the lead: how ids are sequenced on a deployment.

#### SEC-PSS-82. A product's name or description content row can name any file or URL, and Shop reads it and publishes it to anonymous visitors (SEC-PSS-01's fix covers downloads only)

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/ShopCatalog.js:244-264`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/ProductServices.xml:944-950`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/product/Content.js:171`, `:198-201`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-marble/screen/SimpleScreens/Catalog/Product/Content/ContentList.xml:22`, `:74-77`. Report: round 3 `C8.md`.
- **Reached by:** a staff user who can create or change a `ProductContent` row: `mantle.product.ProductServices.create#ProductContent` (any type but `PcntDownload` keeps a given `contentLocation`), `update#ProductContent` (`Content.js` `update`), or the entity-auto create of mr-marble's `createContentWithLocation` transition (a form with a `contentLocation` text field). Then any anonymous visitor of a Shop site of a store that carries the product, through `getProduct` (`GET shop/products/{productId}`, `script/PassRoutes.js:225`), `getShopPage` (`:214`), `getShopSitemap` (`:228`), `getCart` and the store feeds (`service/mantle/product/StoreFeeds.js:260`).
- **What happens:** `contentTexts` picks the store's `PcntProductName`, `PcntDescriptionLong` or `PcntDescription` row of a product and reads its `contentLocation` as text through the resource facade, which serves `file:`, `runtime://`, `component://`, `classpath://`, `http` and `https` (`docs/security/Attack-Surface-Files.md:111`). The text becomes the product's name or its description (sanitized as rich text, so the text itself stays), on the product cards, the product page, the sitemap and the cart's offers. A row naming `runtime://.env`, `runtime://conf/...` or `file:///...` publishes that file on the product page and in the sitemap; an `http` row makes the authority fetch it (internal addresses are refused on a production instance only). SEC-PSS-01's fix applied its location rule to `PcntDownload` rows only, and the image rule (`ShopCatalog.js:341-343`) is not applied to text rows.
- **Evidence:** `ShopCatalog.js:251-252` `for (const row of rowsIn(CONTENT, "productId", productIds, {productContentTypeEnumId: typeEnumId})) {` `if (!activeAt(row, shop.at) || (row.productStoreId && row.productStoreId !== shop.productStoreId)) continue`
- **Evidence:** `ShopCatalog.js:257` `const value = row.contentLocation ? ec.resource.getLocationText(row.contentLocation, false) : row.description`; `:670` `description: descriptionHtml ? {$richText: ec.resource.theme.sanitizeRichText(String(descriptionHtml))} : null,`
- **Evidence:** `ProductServices.xml:945` `<if condition="contentLocation &amp;&amp; productContentTypeEnumId == 'PcntDownload'">` (the only location check of the create)
- **Evidence:** `Content.js:171` `const data = Object.assign(pick(p, [...metadata, c.type, "contentLocation"]), {[c.id]: row[c.id]}),` and `:200` `if (c.kind === "product" && merged[c.type] === "PcntDownload" && merged.contentLocation && !owned(merged, c))`
- **Not confirmed:** which permission or screen authorization `create#ProductContent`, `update#ProductContent` and the mr-marble Catalog screens need; whether that staff user can already read the same location through mr-marble's own `downloadContent` transition (`ContentList.xml:24-30`, `ec.web.sendResourceResponse(productContent.contentLocation)`), which would make the added harm the publication to anonymous visitors. Looked for and not found: a scheme or root rule in `contentTexts` (unlike `imagesOf`); a location rule on create and update for text types (`Content.js` `save`, `:348`, refuses a foreign location, but `update` and both creates do not).
- **Suggested fix:** Apply `Content.js` `owned` (or the image rule) in `contentTexts` and skip any other row, and refuse a caller-given `contentLocation` for every product content type in `create#ProductContent`, `Content.js` `update` and the mr-marble location form.
- **Re-check:** Read `product/ShopCatalog.js:250-260`: `ec.resource.getLocationText(row.contentLocation, false)` for the shop's name and description rows, whatever the location. Not read by the lead: who writes `ProductContent.contentLocation` (the store editor, per the reviewer), mr-marble's `downloadContent`.

#### SEC-PSS-83. A delegated integration administrator of one organization changes and tests another organization's payment gateway

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/account/GatewayConnections.js:172-420`, `:303-312`, `:624-632`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/admin/ConfigurationConnections.js:287-306`, `:1377-1403`; `runtime/components/base/tools/template/ConnectionParts.xml:32`, `:42`, `:52`. Report: round 3 `C8.md`.
- **Reached by:** a holder of `ADMIN_CONNECTION_MANAGE` through the Integration administrator bundle (`BOSS_ADMIN_INTEGRATIONS`), which BOSS delegates per organization, by the Admin connection pages' `storeConnection` and `testConnection` transitions with `adapterKey=paymentGateway` and another organization's gateway id as `connectionKey`.
- **What happens:** The framework's connection engine checks only the permission, and Mantle's payment gateway kind (`paymentDefinition`) has no `hidden` hook, so `get`, `store` and `test` take any `PaymentGatewayConfig` id; the list answers every gateway when no organization is named. The administrator may enter a new API login id and transaction key with the address (a payment gateway's secret is write-only but not fixed), test it, and so point another organization's Authorize.Net or PayTrace gateway, and with it that organization's Shop, Billing and renewal charges, at a merchant account of their choosing; on a production instance the address must still be the provider's host. The organization scope that the file's header promises is applied only to `store#PaymentGatewayOrganization` and to the list when it names an organization.
- **Evidence:** `GatewayConnections.js:21-22` "Reading needs ADMIN_CONNECTION_VIEW, a change ADMIN_CONNECTION_MANAGE, and an organization is one the caller administers"; `:307-308` `const organizationPartyId = text(p.organizationPartyId)` `if (!organizationPartyId) return keys`
- **Evidence:** `ConfigurationConnections.js:302-303` `const hidden = def.hidden?.(parts)` `if (hidden) return hidden` (the payment definition defines no `hidden`); `:1387-1396` `function storeConnection(p) { lib.requireManage(MANAGE) ... return run(adapter, "store", {connectionKey: p.connectionKey, values: ownNames(p.values), ...`
- **Evidence:** `ConfigurationConnections.js:684-685` `{name: "password", part, label: prefix + "Password or token"},` (not `fixed`; only `sharedSecret` is, `:686-696`)
- **Evidence:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/data/BossSetupData.xml:3311` `<moqui.security.CapabilityBundle userGroupId="BOSS_ADMIN_INTEGRATIONS" ... scopeDependency="organization" .../>`; `:2447` `userPermissionId="ADMIN_CONNECTION_MANAGE"`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/data/PaymentGatewayConnectionData.xml:20-21` `adapterKey="paymentGateway" ... creatable="Y" organizationBinding="Y"`
- **Not confirmed:** whether the Admin connection screens and `run#GatewayConnectionAdapter` are open to a delegated integration administrator (the screens' authorization rows `BOSS_ADM_INTEGRATIONS_INTEG`, `BossSetupData.xml:4643`, were not read); whether `get` answers another organization's gateway `username` (not a secret). The shipping gateway kind, instance-wide by design (`listKeys` answers none for an organization, `GatewayConnections.js:473-478`), is open to the same holder. Looked for: an organization check in `paymentDefinition` (`readOnly` is the remote's own rule, `check` the address rule, `consumers` and `extra` filter by scope only what they show), in the engine's `store` and `test`, and in `run#GatewayConnectionAdapter` (`:624-632`): permission only.
- **Suggested fix:** Give `paymentDefinition` a `hidden` that refuses a gateway bound (`PaymentGatewayOrganization`) to an organization outside `scopeIds()` unless the caller is an instance administrator, and filter `listKeys` the same way when no organization is named; keep shipping and tax gateway changes to instance administrators.
- **Re-check:** Read `account/GatewayConnections.js:303-310` (`listKeys`): with no `organizationPartyId` it lists every gateway config (500); with one it checks `scopeIds()`. Not read by the lead: the per-connection read, change and test checks.

## Forum and Smithy

#### SEC-PFS-61. A Smithy access token made for git is the account's whole login key, with no scope

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `create#UserAccessToken` writes `UserLoginKey.scope` `git` (`user::ACCESS_TOKEN_SCOPE`), and `check_login_key` reads a token made before as `git` too (`key_scope`); git over HTTP and the git REST (`/rest/s1/moqui/git/...`, `/rest/s1/moqui/hosting/...`) take it, and the web, the rest of REST, sockets, A2A and PASS pages refuse it. Making a token needs a sign-in in the last five minutes, and its `expireDays` is at most 366; Smithy's `SmithyGit.js` `reauthenticate` takes, for an account with no password, a sign-in within the same five minutes. Tests: `service::user_keys_tests` `credentials_need_a_recent_sign_in_and_an_access_token_is_for_git_alone`, `mr-mantle/test/smithy/SmithyGitAccess.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/smithy/SmithyGit.js:385-415`; `crates/moqui-core/src/service/user_keys.rs:216-247`; `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/SecurityEntities.xml:430`
- **Reached by:** whoever obtains a token an account made on `Account/GitAccess` (a CI variable, a git credential file, a remote URL), with a `login_key` header on any request the framework signs in.
- **What happens:** `createAccessToken` calls the framework's `create#UserAccessToken`, which makes an ordinary `UserLoginKey` (a `description`, an empty `scope`) valid 90 days or up to 366. Nothing limits it to git: it is the account's key on the framework's REST of the business host, and in local mode on every PASS page, where the authority's login-key rule does not run. A staff or `ADMIN` account that makes one for git hands out all of its rights.
- **Evidence:** `user_keys.rs:227` `let token = crate::user::get_login_key(Some(days * 24.0))?;` and `:238` `// a description marks the key as an access token`; `SecurityEntities.xml:430` `Empty: the account's key.`; `PassAuthority.js:378` `// ... an access token is for git and the framework's REST (design-smithy §5.5)`
- **Not confirmed:** what else the framework refuses to a key of kind `token` (only the authority's `run()` was read); whether a write on a local-mode PASS page by header key passes the session token check (another slice reports the header login there).
- **Suggested fix:** Give an access token a scope (git over HTTP and the git REST) that every other login path refuses, as `create#UserScopedKey` does for `gateway`; or refuse to make one for an account with a staff group.

#### SEC-PFS-83. A site owner's content rule makes every private message a review item that the site's staff read

Medium; confirmed (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/ForumCore.js:963`, `:1263-1270`, `:1369-1394`, `:1639`, `:1656-1663`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/Forum.js:1420`, `:1509-1517`, `:1703-1704`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/ForumModeration.js:279-326`, `:1299-1330`, `:2143-2178`, `:2180-2210`. Report: round 3 `C4.md` (read at mr-mantle `5f5cd403`).
- **Reached by:** a site owner (or an operator) through `POST rules` (`storeRule`); then any site owner or site moderator through `GET reviews` and `GET reviews/{reviewId}`. The rule fires on every private message any member sends or edits (`POST conversations`, `POST posts`, `PATCH posts/{postId}`).
- **What happens:** `gate()` runs the site's rules on a private post too. A rule whose action is `flag` (or `approve`) sets the gate's `flag` for a private post, which is then never held. `publishPost` and `updatePost` file a review item when `flag` is set, with the conversation's category id, which for a private topic is null. `reviewStaff` with no record and a null category asks `staff(siteId, null)`: any site owner or moderator. `getReview` answers the post's cleaned body and, for a private topic, the approved post before and after it, ten each side once an escalation is approved; the owner may both ask for and approve the escalation, since nothing compares the approver with `requestedBy`. A rule that matches everything (a `regex` rule `[\s\S]`, which `validRule` allows, or a `word` rule for a common word) so opens every private message of the site to its staff, with no member's report. The design says the opposite (`mr-pass/plan/design-forum.md:703-706`: staff cannot browse all private messages; a report grants a scoped reviewer the reported post and the minimum adjacent context), and so do the code's own comments.
- **Evidence:** `ForumCore.js:1390` `else if (r.action === 'flag') out.flag = true`
- **Evidence:** `ForumCore.js:1639` `ruleHold = !g.isPrivate && approve && !mod,` and `:1660` `flag: flag || (!!g.isPrivate && approve),`
- **Evidence:** `Forum.js:1420` `// Private messages are not queued for staff by default; a held rule still files a scoped review.`; `ForumCore.js:904` `// Private messages reach staff only through a member's report, never through a classifier.`
- **Evidence:** `Forum.js:1509-1516` `if (gated.flag)` / `review(p.siteId, a.category.contactListId, 'post', postId, 1, 'rule',)`; `:1703-1704` the same for an edit
- **Evidence:** `ForumCore.js:963` `contactListId: null,` (the category `conversationAccess` gives a private topic); `:1270` `if (!t) return staff(siteId, r.contactListId)`
- **Evidence:** `ForumModeration.js:303-305` `body: StringUtilities.cleanHtml(event.body || '',),`; `:322-323` `return t?.kind === 'private' ? privateContext(r, st)`; `:2147` `const span = detail.escalation?.approvedBy ? 10 : 1`; `:2196` `site(p.siteId, true)` (the approval)
- **Not confirmed:** what the mr-pass review pages show of a private post. Looked for and not found: a bar on rule review for `isPrivate` in `gate()`, `publishPost` or `updatePost`; a participant or reporter check in `reviewStaff` or `getReview`; a limit on what a rule may match (`validRule` refuses only nested repetition and back references, `ForumModeration.js:1305-1312`).
- **Suggested fix:** Let rules on a private post only `block` (or `censor`); file a review of a private post only from a participant's report; require an escalation's approver to be another person than its requester.
- **Re-check:** Read `ForumCore.js:1656-1663` (the rule gate answers `flag: flag || (!!g.isPrivate && approve)`, so a private message under a rule that approves is flagged) and `Forum.js:1509-1517` (a flagged post is filed with `review(..., 'post', postId, 1, 'rule')`). Confirmed for the path. Not read by the lead: `ForumModeration.js:279-326` and `:2143-2178` (what the review item shows with it).

#### SEC-PFS-84. A site owner exports the forum topics of private Smithy groups and repositories it may not view

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/ForumOperations.js:264-269`, `:288-335`, `:352-363`, `:518-560`, `:1245-1259`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/ForumCore.js:280-282`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/smithy/SmithyCore.js:361-371`, `:398-426`. Report: round 3 `C4.md` (read at mr-mantle `5f5cd403`).
- **Reached by:** a site owner who is not an operator: `POST operations` with `{operation: 'export-site'}` (optionally a `contactListId`), then `GET exports/{exportId}`.
- **What happens:** On every page a category bound to a private Smithy group or repository is read only by who views that scope, and a private scope needs a role of Guest or higher; site staff get no rank there, so a site owner has no read and no moderation in such a category. The site export takes every `kind: 'category'` topic of the site (or of the category named) and writes every post of it: the body, every visibility (pending, rejected, deleted), each revision's `sourceText` (the text as typed, before censor rules), and author and editor ids. It asks no category access. `getExport` then asks only that the caller made the export and is still the site's owner.
- **Evidence:** `ForumCore.js:282` `if (bound && !bound.canView) read = own = moderate = false`
- **Evidence:** `SmithyCore.js:368-370` `if (visibility === 1 || access.operator) return true` / `if (visibility === 2) return access.joined` / `return rank >= RANK.SmrGuest`
- **Evidence:** `ForumOperations.js:264-268` `find(F + 'ForumTopic', { siteId, kind: 'category', ...(contactListId ? { contactListId } : {}), })`; `:523` `if (!t || t.siteId !== siteId || t.kind !== 'category') return`
- **Evidence:** `ForumOperations.js:543` `visibility: st.visibility,`; `:546` `body: event?.body || '',`; `:558` `sourceText: r.sourceText || '',`
- **Evidence:** `ForumOperations.js:1251` `if (!x || x.partyId !== requireUser() || x.state !== 'complete')`; `:1254` `if (x.kind === 'site') site(p.siteId, true)`
- **Not confirmed:** whether the design means the site export to cover private scopes; the export's later steps (`ForumOperations.js:587-656`) were read by a sub-reader only. Looked for and not found: a `categoryAccess` or scope check in `exportTopic`, the run's item finder or `getExport`; a rank for site staff in a private scope (`SmithyCore.js:366` opens a hidden scope to site staff, a private one only by role).
- **Suggested fix:** In `exportTopic` skip a topic whose category `categoryAccess(contactListId, run.partyId)` does not moderate, and do the same in `exportReport`'s moderation report.
- **Re-check:** Read `ForumOperations.js:264-269` (`topicFind`: every `category` topic of the site) and `:521-524` (`exportTopic` checks only the site and the kind); no visibility test in these lines. Not read by the lead: how a private Smithy group's topics are marked.

#### SEC-PFS-85. A site owner routes any address of a route domain, a person's hosted mailbox included, and that mailbox's mail is then posted or held in the owner's forum

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/Forum.js:4882-4928`, `:5197-5217`, `:5223-5264`, `:5370-5400`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/ForumCore.js:156-169`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/forum/ForumLists.js:1059-1101`; `crates/moqui-mail/src/delivery.rs:19-21`, `:1139-1153`. Reports: round 3 `C4.md` and `C5.md` (read at mr-mantle `5f5cd403`).
- **Reached by:** the owner of any site (or an operator), through `POST mail/routes` (`storeRoute`, the Mail settings page) and `PATCH categories/{id}` (mode `list`); then mail from outside senders to that address, through the `ForumInbound` email rule. It needs the operator to list in `pass@route-domains` a domain that also holds personal mailboxes on the host.
- **What happens:** `routeAddress` accepts any local part on a domain of `pass@route-domains`, and `storeRoute` refuses only an address another forum route holds. The listener runs the email rules on every account's new mail, with that account's id. `forumAccount` accepts any `EakHosted` or `EakShared` account without a store host whose address or `EmailIdentity` is the route's, which is what a person's hosted mailbox is; it does not ask that the account be the forum's inbound account or belong to the site. So once site B's owner routes `alice@example.org` to a category of site B in list mode, a copy of each message to alice is site B's: from a sender whose domain the gateway authenticated and who has no verified account, a held message whose review item shows site B's moderators the From, the Subject and 3,500 characters of the text; from a verified account that may post in the category, a post in site B as its sender. The receipts show site B's owner each sender's masked address. Alice still gets the mail.
- **Evidence:** `ForumCore.js:166-167` `if (!domains.includes(m[2])) invalid('The route domain is not registered for this deployment')`
- **Evidence:** `Forum.js:4885` `const address = routeAddress(p.address)`; `:4900-4902` `const clash = one(F + 'ForumMailRoute', { address })` / `if (clash && clash.routeId !== r?.routeId) fail('Conflict', 'That address is already routed')`
- **Evidence:** `Forum.js:5201` `if (!account || !['EakHosted', 'EakShared'].includes(account.accountKindEnumId))`; `:5211` `String(account.emailAddress || '').toLowerCase() === address ||` (or one of its `EmailIdentity` rows, `:5210-5215`)
- **Evidence:** `delivery.rs:19` `//! Routing: a recipient is an account of kind EakHosted or EakShared that is not removed and has no incoming (IMAP or`; `:1139-1143` `step(account_id, "email ECAs", || { run_stored_ecas(` with `email_message_id: &id`
- **Evidence:** `Forum.js:5383` `!spam && authenticated && !tag && body && lists.holds(route)`; `ForumLists.js:1091-1100` `detail: ("From: " ... ">\nSubject: " + m.subject + "\n\n" + plainText(m.html)).slice(0, 3500)`
- **Not confirmed:** how an operator provisions the forum's inbound account (one account with an identity per route, or several); whether any deployment lists a domain with personal mailboxes; which header a hosted delivery carries (`Delivered-To`, else the `To` and `Cc` fallback at `Forum.js:5235-5236`); how the mr-pass pages show a held item. Looked for and not found: an owner or account kind bound to a route (`ForumMailRoute` has only `siteId`); a check in `storeRoute` that the address is no mail account's, identity's or user's; an operator's approval of a route address; a mark in `forumAccount` for the forum's own inbound account. mr-pass `docs/Deployment.md:964-965` asks for "a domain you receive mail for", gives a lists subdomain as the example, and does not warn about personal mailboxes in it.
- **Suggested fix:** Bind each route to the forum's inbound account by an operator (a conf list or a flag on `EmailAccount`) and have `forumAccount` require that binding, or have the operator approve each route address; and refuse in `storeRoute` an address that is another mail account's, identity's or user's address.

## BOSS access model; Admin, Commons, People, Accounting

#### SEC-BOS-01. Organization row filters do not apply below a sub-area ALWAYS grant

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: each of the 137 role-group `AUTHZT_ALWAYS` rows on mr-boss screen artifact groups, and the two of the Products import services, names the `MANTLE_USER_ORG` and `MANTLE_PRIVATE_CONTACT` filter sets as the app roots do, so a role user's direct finds under an area are filtered. The 47 ALWAYS rows left without them are on no screen (the report engine applies its own organization filter). Test: `mr-boss/test/AppOrganizationFilters.spec.js`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-core/src/user/filter.rs:144 and runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/data/BossAccountingSetupData.xml:209
- **Reached by:** a member of a role group (for example `BOSS_ACCOUNTING_AR`, `BOSS_PEOPLE_PAYROLL`, `BOSS_PEOPLE_HR`) on any request under that group's area screen
- **What happens:** Entity filters come only from the record that authorized the top frame. A role group's `AUTHZT_ALWAYS` row on an area screen becomes that record for the area and everything below it, and those rows carry no filter set, so `MANTLE_USER_ORG` and `MANTLE_PRIVATE_CONTACT` stop applying exactly where the money and payroll screens are. Organization isolation there rests on each service's own check alone; the seed comment says role users carry `MANTLE_USER_ORG`.
- **Evidence:** `// The filters come from the record that authorized this find, the frame on top of the stack.` and `<authz artifactAuthzId="BOSS_ACCT_Receivables_AR" userGroupId="BOSS_ACCOUNTING_AR" authzTypeEnumId="AUTHZT_ALWAYS" authzActionEnumId="AUTHZA_ALL"/>` and `<authz artifactAuthzId="BOSS_PPL_PAYROLL_OPERATOR" userGroupId="BOSS_PEOPLE_PAYROLL" authzTypeEnumId="AUTHZT_ALWAYS" authzActionEnumId="AUTHZA_ALL"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** no read that returns another organization's rows was found; the few direct finds sampled under `screen/Accounting` and `screen/People/Payroll` go through services that check the organization. The other apps' sub-area rows and the multi-line Accounting rows were not read.
- **Suggested fix:** Put the organization and private-contact filter sets on every sub-area ALWAYS row (or make them ALLOW rows that keep the root's record), and add a test that a role user's direct find under an area is filtered.

#### SEC-BOS-02. App administrators and ACCOUNTING_ADMIN are not bound to an organization

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: Accounting's every-organization reach takes the new `ACCOUNTING_ALL_ORGANIZATIONS` (seeded on `ADMIN` only) in place of `ACCOUNTING_ADMIN` (`AccountingPolicy.js` `access`, `InvoiceWorkflowLib.js` `organization`, `get#PartyOrganizationInfo`, `ProductionCostClose.js`, `CostingRates.js`), so an `ACCOUNTING_ADMIN` holder without it works in their own organizations and cannot make another active. Exchange rates, a shared labor costing row and a new organization's Accounting setup now need `ADMIN` or that permission. Test: `mr-mantle/test/party/OrganizationRowFilter.spec.js`.
- **Where:** runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/ledger/AccountingPolicy.js:83 and runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/party/PartyServices.xml:626
- **Reached by:** a holder of `ACCOUNTING_ADMIN` (group `BOSS_ACCOUNTING_ADMIN`), and at the entity level any `BOSS_<APP>_ADMIN` or `ADMIN` member, on any Accounting or app request
- **What happens:** An Accounting administrator's scope is every party with the role `OrgInternal`, and they may select any internal organization as active, whatever organization they work for. The `ADMIN` and app-administrator ALWAYS rows carry only `MANTLE_ACTIVE_ORG`, which does not apply when no active organization is selected, so their finds are unfiltered by organization. One organization's Accounting administrator therefore reads and posts in every organization of the instance.
- **Evidence:** `if (c.admin) { ids = Array.from(find("mantle.party.PartyRole", {roleTypeId: "OrgInternal"})...` and `<if condition="activeOrgId &amp;&amp; partyId == ec.user.userAccount?.partyId &amp;&amp; (ec.user.isInGroup('ADMIN') || ec.user.hasPermission('ACCOUNTING_ADMIN'))">`
- **Not confirmed:** a code comment says this is intended ("Finance administrators may select another internal organization"); whether an organization-scoped access administrator can grant `BOSS_ACCOUNTING_ADMIN` (its delegable permission) was not read. People and Sales have separate `*_ALL_ORGANIZATIONS` permissions; Accounting has none.
- **Suggested fix:** Add an `ACCOUNTING_ALL_ORGANIZATIONS` permission as People and Sales have, and bind `ACCOUNTING_ADMIN` without it to the caller's own organizations; state the instance-wide reach of the administrator bundles where they are granted.

#### SEC-BOS-03. An invitation is accepted by a bare GET for a signed-in user

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16 by the framework rule of SEC-WEB-04: `acceptExisting` runs actions and is not read-only, so a GET another site started is refused; the page's Accept button posts.
- **Where:** runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/AcceptInvitation.xml:35
- **Reached by:** an identity operator (`ADMIN_IDENTITY_MANAGE`) who issued the invitation and knows its link, through a signed-in victim's click or page load of `/i/<token>/acceptExisting`
- **What happens:** `acceptExisting` has no `method`, so a GET with no query string runs `accept#Invitation`. A signed-in user whose address is the invited one gets an organization relationship and group memberships without pressing Accept, and becomes an identity of the inviter's organization.
- **Evidence:** `<transition name="acceptExisting"><actions><service-call name="mantle.party.IdentityServices.accept#Invitation" in-map="{token:(token || '')}" out-map="accepted"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** that the token-route dispatch applies the same GET rule as an ordinary screen was taken from the brief's confirmed facts, not re-read; what the inviter can then do to that account (`Identity.js` scope rules) was not traced. `identityRefusal` blocks an account that already works for an organization outside the inviter's scope.
- **Suggested fix:** Set `method="post"` on `acceptExisting`, `createAccount` and `confirmFactor`.

## BOSS business apps and the Mantle REST root

#### SEC-BAP-01. Calendar subscription makes the server fetch any URL, private addresses included

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `subscribe#Calendar` takes an `https` or `webcal` URL only, and `fetch#CalendarSubscription` fetches over https with `.denyInternal()` (each redirect too) and stores a fixed error text, logging the answer's status or the connection error. A stored `http://` subscription fails each fetch until it is made again with https. Test: `mr-mantle/test/work/CalendarServices.spec.js`.
- **Where:** runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/work/CalendarServices.xml:785-822 and 898-925; runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Commons/Settings/Calendars.xml:64
- **Reached by:** any signed-in member of `BOSS_USERS`, the subscribe transition of Commons/Settings/Calendars
- **What happens:** `subscribe#Calendar` accepts any `http`, `https` or `webcal` URL with no host check and starts a background fetch at once; `fetch#CalendarSubscription` GETs it without `denyInternal`, every 5 minutes at the least. The status line of a failed fetch is stored in `lastFetchError` and a body that parses as iCalendar becomes the user's calendar events, so a staff user can probe loopback and LAN services from the server.
- **Evidence:** `const match = /^(https?|webcal):\/\/([^\/?#:]+)/i.exec(text)` ... `ec.service.rest().method('GET').uri(uri).contentType('text/calendar').timeout(60).call()`; Attack-Surface-Outbound.md: `ec.service.rest()` internal-address guard "Off unless the request has `denyInternal: true`"
- **Not confirmed:** whether the client follows redirects, whether `lastFetchError` is shown to the subscriber, and whether a guard outside the script (a fence, a conf default) refuses private addresses.
- **Suggested fix:** call `.denyInternal()` on the subscription fetch (and on redirects), require https, and store a generic error instead of the status text.

#### SEC-BAP-02. A bank account number is copied whole into the payment method description

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `create#BankAccount` and `update#BankAccount` label a bank payment method with the bank name and `•••• ` and the last four digits, and `BankAccount.accountNumber` is `encrypt="true"`; `migrate#BankAccountNumbers` (a seed load) encrypts a plain number and masks a description holding it. No find compares by the number. Test: `mr-mantle/test/account/PaymentMethodServices.spec.js`.
- **Where:** runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/service/mantle/account/PaymentMethodServices.xml:509 and :555; runtime/components/addon/mr-mantle/entity/AccountingAccountEntities.xml:1144-1145
- **Reached by:** whoever creates or replaces a bank account payment method without a description (BOSS Accounting, the Mantle REST root); read by anyone who sees a payment method's description
- **What happens:** `BankAccount.routingNumber` and `accountNumber` are stored in plain text, and the default `PaymentMethod.description` is the bank name plus the full account number. The description is the label of a payment method in lists and pickers, so the number reaches users and logs that have no need for it; the card path masks to the last four digits.
- **Evidence:** `<if condition="!description"><set field="description" value="${bankName || 'Bank Account'} ${accountNumber || ''}"/></if>`; `<field name="accountNumber" type="text-medium"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** which screens, exports and notifications show the description, and to which groups.
- **Suggested fix:** build the description from the last four digits, and set `encrypt="true"` on `accountNumber` (and consider `routingNumber`).

## Clients

#### SEC-CLI-01. The sidecar's loopback web listener is open to every local user and has no Host check seen

Medium; reported (to check); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: a request straight to a listener whose `Host` is not in the new `webapp_allowed_hosts` is answered 421 (`serve.rs` `host_allowed`), and the Desktop node sets it to `127.0.0.1:<port>` and `localhost:<port>`; the first administrator comes only from this machine or with the startup log's setup secret (SEC-WEB-08). Test: `serve_tests.rs` `a_listener_with_allowed_hosts_answers_only_for_them`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-desktop/src/node/mod.rs:271, crates/moqui-core/src/web/origin.rs:127, runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/Login.xml:116
- **Reached by:** another local user or process of the machine (any request to `127.0.0.1:<port>`), and possibly a web page in the user's browser through DNS rebinding; sidecar mode, or the node in process with the tray's ports item on
- **What happens:** The node's ordinary web listener is bound on loopback with plain HTTP, so every local account reaches the sign-in and, while no user exists, the anonymous first-administrator form. The server's Origin check counts the request's own `Host` header as its own origin, so a rebinding page's `Origin` matches its `Host` and passes; a new session needs no session token.
- **Evidence:** `("webapp_http_bind", "127.0.0.1".to_string()), ("webapp_http_port", port.to_string())`; `let site = request_site(request.https, believed, request.header("host"), ...); own_origins(factory, site)`; `<transition name="createInitialAdminAccount"><service-call name="org.moqui.impl.UserServices.create#InitialAdminAccount"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** whether a Host allow-list exists elsewhere in the listener; whether a desktop node ever runs with no user account; the rebinding path was not tested. The node in process with ports off has no listener and is not affected.
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse a request on the desktop node's listener whose `Host` is not `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, and have the app create the first administrator (or hold a one-time setup secret) so the form is never open on the port.

#### SEC-CLI-81. Every server's pages in the apps share the origin `http://moqui-app.localhost`, so one server's page queues a chat send that another account's page then sends as that user, and may register a service worker there

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/lib.rs:62`; `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/scheme.rs:208`, `:415-426`; `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/outbox.rs:40`; `crates/moqui-client/src/sync/outbox.rs:300-314`; `crates/moqui-client/src/sync/flush.rs:430`, `:1067-1103`; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/client/windows.rs:390`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootChat.js:454`, `:499`, `:571-588`, `:1079`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/MoquiLib.js:355-357`, `:410`, `:451-466`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootPush.js:112-116`. Reports: round 3 `B13.md` (the outbox and the drafts) and `B14.md` (the service worker).
- **Reached by:** the operator of a server the user has signed in to on this device, or anyone who gets script into a page of that server (a stored XSS by another user of it). The victim is the same device user's account on another server. On the desktop through any app window `remote-<n>`; on the phone through the one webview.
- **What happens:** In the desktop client and the phone app every account's server pages are on one origin, `http://moqui-app.localhost`. The pages keep their browser storage apart only by a key prefix they choose to use, the account id the host writes into the window (`moqui.scopedKey`), and the host never clears the origin's storage between accounts (`Cleared` is only for a removed account). Server A's script lists `localStorage`, where B's chat keys name B's scope, user and rooms (`bannerKey`, `heldKey`), and writes `<B scope>:moqui.chat.outbox.<B user>` with a chat send of its choosing. When the user next opens any `/qapps` page of B in an app window, its shell root starts chat, and with the host's outbox `importOutbox` moves every item of that key into the host. `outbox_add` keeps it for the calling page's account, B, and checks only its shape (a plain `clientRef`, a room id, any app's `/Chat/send` path, plain params); the flusher posts it to B's server with B's key, so the message is posted as B in a room of B's. The same import moves `boss.chat.draft.*` and `ironstone.compose.*` items into B's drafts, so A can also plant a compose draft (recipients and text) that B's Compose shows as the user's own. A's script can also read what B's pages keep on the origin and clear it. On this origin the scheme forwards every request to the current account's server, a `Sec-Fetch-Dest: serviceworker` fetch included; only the shell's origin refuses worker and service-worker destinations. If the runtime registers and runs a service worker for this scheme-handled origin, A's page can register a script of A's at scope `/`, and after a switch to account B that worker would see and answer every request of B's pages, which the proxy signs with B's device key. The stock pages register no worker in the apps, and the stock `sw.js` keeps `/libs/` answers cache first. The chat crypto store is on the shell's origin on the desktop, so A does not reach the E2E keys this way.
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/lib.rs:62` `pub const APP_ORIGIN: &str = "http://moqui-app.localhost";`; `MoquiLib.js:351-352` "in the desktop client, whose pages of every server and account share the app's origin, the account (window.moquiDesktop.storageScope ...)"; `crates/moqui-desktop/src/client/windows.rs:390` `"window.__moquiStorageScope = {scope};..."`
- **Evidence:** `WebrootChat.js:1079` `outboxKey() { return moqui.scopedKey("moqui.chat.outbox." + (chat.me || "")); }`; `:1945` `bannerKey(roomId) { return moqui.scopedKey("moqui.chat.banner." + (chat.me || "") + "." + roomId); }`; `:575-577` read that key and keep every item with a `clientRef` and `chatRoomId`
- **Evidence:** `WebrootVue.qvt.js:10701` `if (moqui.chat) moqui.chat.start(this);`; `WebrootChat.js:454` `if (host) chat.startHost(host);`, `:499` `return chat.importOutbox(host)...`, `:588` `return list.reduce((previous, item) => previous.then(() => host.add("chat-send", item)...`; `MoquiLib.js:466` `return host.put(key, value, [])` (the drafts)
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-client-tauri/src/outbox.rs:40` `.page_account(webview.label())`; `crates/moqui-client/src/sync/outbox.rs:303` `if !comms_path(url, "/Chat/send") {`; `sync/flush.rs:430` `let sent = post_form(job, url, &params);` and `:1071` `let Some(key) = client.keys.get(&account.id) else {`
- **Evidence:** `scheme.rs:208` `if origin == SHELL_ORIGIN && !forwarded_on_shell(request.headers()) {`; `scheme_tests.rs:79` `"serviceworker",` (refused on the shell only); `WebrootPush.js:115` `return nav.serviceWorker.register("/sw.js", {scope: "/"})`; `WebrootVue.qvt.js:9878` `pushOn: !!(config.userId && moqui.push && !root.moquiDesktop)`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. The service-worker part is to check: whether `tauri-runtime-cef` and Android's WebView register and keep a service worker for a URL their scheme handler answers (`tauri-runtime-cef` alpha.7 was not in the local registry and was not read; on Android the wry patch sets no `ServiceWorkerClient`, so a worker's fetch would go to the network and fail). It would be High if the runtime runs a worker there. Whether B's pages always leave keys that name the scope, user and a room (a guessed room id also works); the runtime's storage being one per origin is the browser's rule. The mobile bridge's `outbox.add` (`crates/moqui-mobile/src/bridge.js:201`, seen by search only). Looked for and not found: a per-account origin or webview profile (one constant; no `data_directory` or incognito in the apps); the host clearing the app origin's storage, caches or service workers at a switch (a search of `crates/moqui-desktop/src` and `crates/moqui-mobile/src` for browsing data, site data, service worker and data directory, and of `moqui-client`, `moqui-client-tauri` and `moqui-desktop` for `serviceworker` and `sw.js`); a check in `outbox_add` that an item was made by this page or this session (`check_with` checks shape only); a guard that limits `importOutbox` to items of the web (it runs whenever the host outbox exists); a refusal of `serviceworker` or the `Service-Worker` header on the app origin.
- **Suggested fix:** Give each account its own origin or webview profile (a host name per account under `.localhost`, or a data directory per account), so the browser keeps them apart. Until then: stop the pages' one-time imports (`importOutbox`, `drafts.importLocal`) in the apps, or run them only on the shell's origin; have the host clear the app origin's site data (storage, Cache Storage, service workers) when the current account changes; refuse `Sec-Fetch-Dest` `serviceworker`, `sharedworker` and `worker` (and a `Service-Worker` header) on the app origin in `scheme::answer`.
- **Re-check:** For `B13.md` the lead read `moqui-client-tauri/src/lib.rs:61-62` (every server's pages are on one origin, `APP_ORIGIN` `http://moqui-app.localhost`), `scheme.rs:206-213` (the server's pages are kept off the shell's origin only) and `WebrootChat.js:571-590` (`importOutbox` reads the `localStorage` key `chat.outboxKey()` and hands each item to the host's `chat-send`, which sends as the account whose window it is). Not read by the lead: `outboxKey()`'s prefix, the host outbox, whether a hostile server's page can write another account's key (the reviewer says the prefix is the page's own choice). `B14.md`'s service-worker item ("High if it holds, to check") has the same cause and is joined to this entry; the lead did not check it, and it would be High if CEF runs a worker on the app origin.

#### SEC-CLI-82. Rich text keeps any image source, so a same-site image in a Chatter entry runs a GET with the reader's session, and an outside one tells the writer who read it

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-lib/src/safe_html.rs:31-36`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/js/WebrootVue.qvt.js:4183`, `:4572`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/screen/Shared/Chatter.qvue:302`; `runtime/components/addon/mr-boss/service/ironstone/shared/ChatterServices.xml:494-497`, `:708-714`; `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2768-2782`; `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:664-665`; `crates/moqui-dav/src/elfinder.rs:578-587`. Report: round 3 `B14.md`.
- **Reached by:** any user who may save `allow-html="safe"` text that another user then views in the app: a Chatter note or message on a record (mr-boss `ChatterServices.xml`; a client portal user may send a `Message`, `:713`), whose body is shown to staff; and, by the same render, an inbound email the mail import links to a record (`type === 'Email'`, `:708-714`; the linking was not traced). The victim only opens the record.
- **What happens:** The safe set is ammonia's defaults plus three `span` attributes; only the chat cleaner narrows `img` (`clean_chat`, SEC-COM-02). The editor itself inserts an image of any `https://`, `//` or `/` address. Chatter shows the cleaned body with `v-html` on the app's origin, so the reader's browser fetches each `src`: an outside host learns the reader's address and the time, and a path of this site is a same-origin GET with the session cookie. The GET guard of SEC-WEB-04 refuses only a request another site started, and an image on the app's own page is `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, so a writing transition with no parameter runs: `/Login/logout` signs the reader out (and, per SEC-WEB-04, their other sessions). `/resources` is `read-only` and so takes URL parameters, and its write commands are refused only cross-site (SEC-RTE-05), so for an `ADMIN` reader `<img src="/resources?cmd=rm&targets[]=…">`, or `cmd=mkdir` or `mkfile`, runs as that administrator on the root the URL names (a target hash is the volume id and the base64url of the path, `elfinder.rs:132-133`).
- **Evidence:** `safe_html.rs:33-34` `let mut builder = ammonia::Builder::default(); builder.add_tag_attributes("span", &["data-mention-id", "data-record-entity", "data-record-id"]);` and its test `:90-91` `// the safe set keeps an outside image, as any allow-html="safe" text does` / `assert!(clean(r#"<img src="https://host.example/x.png">"#).contains("src="));`
- **Evidence:** `WebrootVue.qvt.js:4572` `if (/^(https?:)?\/\//i.test(value) || value.startsWith("/")) editor.chain().focus().setImage({ src: value }).run();`
- **Evidence:** `ChatterServices.xml:494-497` `<service verb="clean" noun="Html" allow-remote="false"> ... <parameter name="html" allow-html="safe"/>`; `:708` `const parts = bodyParts(entry.body, entry.contentType)`; `Chatter.qvue:302` `... v-html="entry.bodyHtml"></div>`
- **Evidence:** `screen/render.rs:2778-2781` `if !web::started_by_another_site(job.request) { return Ok(false); } Ok(!session_token_presented(job)?)`; `web/mod.rs:664-665` `return !site.eq_ignore_ascii_case("same-origin") && !site.eq_ignore_ascii_case("none");`; `Login.xml:77-78` `<transition name="logout"> <actions>`
- **Evidence:** `elfinder.rs:578-582` `let cross_site = moqui_core::web::current_request().is_some_and(|request| moqui_core::web::started_by_another_site(&request)); let write_refused = |response| { if cross_site {`; `webroot.xml:268` `<transition name="resources" read-only="true">`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. That ammonia passes a relative `src` with its query (its documented default `UrlRelative::PassThrough`; not read in the crate). How an inbound email becomes a Chatter entry. Which other screens show safe HTML with `v-html` or a read-only editor (wiki, notes, support cases and the forum were not listed). Looked for and not found: an image rule outside chat (`safe_html.rs` has `chat_image_src` for `clean_chat` only; `clean#Html` uses the plain safe set); a client-side image rule in Chatter (none in `Chatter.qvue`, unlike `Chat.qvue:441` `ownImages`); a `Sec-Fetch-Dest` test in `cross_site_write` or `write_refused`; an `img-src` policy (the default screen CSP has none).
- **Suggested fix:** Give the safe set chat's image rule (this server's `/richTextImage/<id>` only), or a separate `clean_rich` used by every rich-text parameter, and make the editor refuse any other image `src`; in `cross_site_write` and elFinder's `write_refused` also refuse a request whose `Sec-Fetch-Dest` is not `document` (or `empty` for the page's own fetch).
- **Re-check:** The lead read `moqui-lib/src/safe_html.rs:30-37` (the rich-text cleaner is ammonia's default builder plus three span attributes; only the chat cleaner below it narrows images, SEC-COM-02), `screen/render.rs:2770-2781` (a GET to a transition that runs actions is refused only when `web::started_by_another_site`, which a same-origin image request is not) and mr-boss `Shared/Chatter.qvue:302` (a stored body rendered with `v-html`). Not read by the lead: ammonia's default URL rule for a relative `src` (the reviewer says a relative one passes), the transitions named.

#### SEC-CLI-83. A workbook's frozen-row count sizes a loop and an allocation in the terminal's calc grid on every frame

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-tui/src/components/calc.rs:340-343`, `:369-371`, `:1164-1171`; `crates/moqui-calc/src/fill.rs:504`; `crates/moqui-calc/src/service.rs:594`; IronCalc (rev `dbecebb`) `xlsx/src/import/worksheets.rs:885-888`, `:900-904`. Report: round 3 `B15.md`.
- **Reached by:** whoever makes or edits a CalcGrid document (`CALC_GRID_AUTHOR` for a document from an xlsx file, `MonitoringSecurityData.xml:11-30`; an editor of the room for the `freeze` edit), against anyone who opens it in an SSH `apps` session or the browser terminal, the author included. Those sessions run in the server process. In `moqui tui --server` the same grid costs only the client's own memory.
- **What happens:** The room's sheet layout carries `frozenRows` from the worksheet as it is. IronCalc's xlsx import reads `ySplit` with no bound (`2147483647` gives `i32::MAX`); an edit through `set_frozen_rows` allows up to 1,048,575. The terminal's grid collects `1..=frozen_rows` into a `Vec<i32>` (8 GiB for `i32::MAX`, whose `frozen_rows + 1` also wraps in a release build) and then builds a line with a span per shown column for each of them, on every frame. An allocation that fails aborts the process (Rust's default allocation-error handler), and one that succeeds is followed by billions of lines. At 1,048,575 rows each frame builds about ten million spans, and the sheet is polled every 1.5 seconds.
- **Evidence:** `calc.rs:342` `(int(&layout["frozenRows"]).max(0), int(&layout["frozenColumns"]).max(0))`; `calc.rs:1166-1168` `let first = view.top.max(frozen_rows + 1);` `let rows: Vec<i32> = (1..=frozen_rows)` `.chain(first..first + view.rows_shown(terminal_rows))`
- **Evidence:** `fill.rs:504` `"frozenRows": worksheet.frozen_rows,`; `service.rs:594` `out.insert("sheets".to_string(), from_json(&room.layout()));`; `crates/moqui-core/src/workbook.rs:271` `ironcalc::import::load_from_xlsx_bytes(bytes, name, "en", "UTC")`
- **Evidence:** `worksheets.rs:888` `frozen_rows = get_split(pane[0], "ySplit");`; `worksheets.rs:901-903` `.and_then(|value| value.parse::<f64>().ok()) .map_or(0, |split| split.round() as i32)`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. IronCalc `Model::from_workbook` was not read for a later check, nor the calc room services between the import and `fill::layout`; that the `workbookLocation` service path reaches `open_xlsx` with a user's file was read from `service.rs:620-621` and the service description only. Looked for and not found: a clamp in `frozen_counts` or `grid_lines` (`width_of` is clamped, `:353`); a bound in the import (`get_split` has none; `set_frozen_rows` has one, `base/src/worksheet.rs:375-376`, but the import does not call it); a clamp on the server in `fill::layout`.
- **Suggested fix:** Clamp `frozen_counts` to the rows and columns shown (and to `LAST_ROW`) in the TUI, draw only the frozen rows that fit, and clamp `frozenRows` and `frozenColumns` in `fill::layout` or after the import.

#### SEC-CLI-84. On Windows a server's `Content-Type` puts a `moqui tui --server` download outside the downloads folder

Medium; reported (likely).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-tui/src/ui/run.rs:201-236`; `crates/moqui-host/src/tui.rs:291-294`, `:870-882`; `crates/moqui-tui/src/session/mod.rs:482-485`. Report: round 3 `B15.md`.
- **Reached by:** the server a user reaches with `moqui tui --server` (or `--server ... --agent`) on Windows, which `docs/Run-Deploy.md:29` supports: a hostile server, or one whose attachment answers carry an outside `Content-Type`. No click is needed: the first screen request may be answered with the attachment.
- **What happens:** A screen or transition answered with `Content-Disposition: attachment` becomes a `RawBody` with the response's `Content-Type` as given, and the local loop saves it with no question. `save_download` cleans the file name, but when it is empty (or only dots and underscores) it names the file `export.<second part of the Content-Type>`, cut at `/` and `;` only. On Windows `\` is a separator, so `Content-Type: x/\..\..\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\a.cmd` with no file name joins to a path that leaves `Downloads`, and `std::fs::write` creates it.
- **Evidence:** `run.rs:215-217` `let name = if name.trim_matches(['.', '_']).is_empty() {` `let extension = body.content_type.split(['/', ';']).nth(1).unwrap_or("bin");` `format!("export.{}", extension.trim())`; `run.rs:224` `let mut path = dir.join(&name);`; `run.rs:235` `std::fs::write(&path, &body.bytes)`
- **Evidence:** `tui.rs:879` `content_type: output.header("Content-Type").unwrap_or("").to_string(),`; `session/mod.rs:485` `Ok(body) => self.downloads.push(body),`
- **Not confirmed:** not run on Windows, so the path handling of an `export.\..\..` name is reasoned. Whether a server of this project ever sends a user-supplied `Content-Type` on an attachment (the in-process route on a Windows server) was not traced. Looked for and not found: a check of the extension's characters or of the final path's parent; a question to the user before a save. Not affected: the report export, which allows four content types only (`session/structure.rs:597-605`), and the session loop of SSH and the browser terminal, which refuses downloads (`session_loop.rs:285-289`).
- **Suggested fix:** Take the extension only when it is ASCII alphanumeric and short (else `bin`), and check that the final path's parent is the downloads directory; consider asking before a download the user did not start.

## Script engine, templates and parsers

#### SEC-SCR-02. `print#ScreenDocument` evaluates its `screenParametersStr` parameter as a script

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `print#ScreenDocument` (`service/tools.rs` `print_screen_document`) reads `screenParametersStr` as a JSON object and refuses any other text before the render; nothing is evaluated. Test: `service/tools/tests.rs` `screen_parameters_text_is_json_and_never_a_script`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-core/src/service/tools.rs:204-208; runtime/components/base/tools/service/moqui.rest.xml:147-148
- **Reached by:** a user granted the `/moqui` REST path (the `ADMIN` group by default), `POST` to the `moqui` API resource `print/printers/{networkPrinterId}/print/screen`; and any other caller that passes request text to the service.
- **What happens:** The text is evaluated with `eval_map` on the script runtime, so it is a server script with the full host table. An `ADMIN` who does not hold `GROOVY_SHELL_WEB` gets script execution, and so a host process and the secret properties.
- **Evidence:** `let expression = param_text(parameters, "screenParametersStr"); if !expression.is_empty() { let evaluated = crate::script::eval_map(&expression)`
- **Not confirmed:** The callers in mr-mantle (`mantle/facility/Printing.js`, `PrintServices.xml`) were not read; whether a screen transition also passes this parameter from a request was not traced.
- **Suggested fix:** Drop `screenParametersStr` (the `screenParameters` map already carries the values) or parse it as JSON.

#### SEC-SCR-03. `System.getProperty` returns secret properties and the whole environment to any script

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `System.getProperty` answers null for a secret property (`context/identity.rs` `script_refused_property`): one the conf marks `is-secret`, a name `secret_parameter` calls a secret's, or one ending in `_pass` or `_pw` or holding `crypt`. New host functions say whether one is set or compare with it without returning it (`System.isPropertySet`, `System.propertyMatches`, `System.secretCodeHash`), and the framework's scripts use them. `pass_client_secret` is refused too (commit 8f743252d): a script sends it with `RestClient.addPropertyHeader(headerName, propertyName, baseUrlPropertyName)`, which the host adds at send time only to a URL under the base URL property, never on a redirect and never returned to the script; mr-pass `Transport.js` sends `x-pass-client-secret` so (`.addPropertyHeader('x-pass-client-secret', 'pass_client_secret', 'pass_backend_url')`, `System.isPropertySet('pass_client_secret')`). Tests: `identity.rs` `scripts_read_no_secret_property`, `script/runtime_tests.rs` `a_script_reads_no_secret_property`, `service::script_ports_tests` `a_property_header_goes_only_under_its_base_and_the_script_never_reads_it`, `moqui-lib` `http::tests` `a_url_is_under_a_base_only_on_its_origin_and_below_its_path`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/globals.rs:562-565; crates/moqui-lib/src/env.rs:62-80, 281-295
- **Reached by:** any server script, and so any expression of C1, C2 and C5.
- **What happens:** The lookup reads the property map, then the process environment, then `.env`, with no filter. `entity_ds_crypt_pass` (field encryption) and `session_cookie_key` are `default-property` elements marked `is-secret="true"`, and no Rust code outside tests reads that attribute. An expression injection therefore yields the keys that decrypt stored fields and sign sessions.
- **Evidence:** `fn get_property(call: &mut Call<'_>) ... let found = moqui_lib::env::get_prop_or_env(&call.text_or_empty(0));`
- **Not confirmed:** Whether secret default properties are kept out of the property map at load was not read; they can still arrive through the environment, which is read next.
- **Suggested fix:** Refuse names marked `is-secret` (and a deny-list of environment names) in `System.getProperty`; give the few scripts that need a secret a named host function.

#### SEC-SCR-04. `ec.resource.runTokenCommand` starts a host program from any server script

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16 with SEC-TLS-03 (the same weakness): no script starts a host program; the registry token command is a conf entry run by one Rust service under `INSTANCE_ADMIN`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:721 and :2603-2620
- **Reached by:** any server script; with C1, C2 or C5, an admin without the script permission or whoever controls the injected text.
- **What happens:** The function takes any command line, splits it on whitespace and starts it with `std::process::Command`. The only check is the effect fence, which a normal request does not enter. It is meant for one caller (a registry token command of an instance image), yet it is on `ec.resource` for every script.
- **Evidence:** `ec.resource.runTokenCommand = (command) => host.runTokenCommand(String(command));` and `crate::entity::fence::check_effect(crate::entity::fence::Effect::Process, program)?; let mut child = Command::new(program).args(words)`
- **Not confirmed:** Whether the thread's `restrict_host_calls` list or another wrapper limits it for some script classes beyond the load runner.
- **Suggested fix:** Move it off `ec.resource` into the one Rust service that needs it, or take a record id (`InstanceImage`) and read the command from the record under a permission check.

#### SEC-SCR-06. Records whose text is evaluated as script are writable without the script permission

Medium; reported (to check); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: a translation of a text that is not a message key may add no `${}` expression to its original (`l10n.rs` `translation_adds_expression`; the original is used, with a warning), and `Security.eecas.xml` rules require `DATABASE_ADMIN` to write an expression into `LocalizedMessage`, the `EmailTemplate` sender and subject, an `EmailTemplateAttachment` file name, condition or loop, `SystemMessageRemote.sendUrl`, the `AuthFlowFieldMap` expressions, the `filterMap` and `applyCond` fields of artifact groups, entity filters and authz filters, and `SubscreensItem.menuTitle`; a data load with authorization off is not checked. Tests: `l10n_tests.rs` `a_translation_that_adds_an_expression_is_not_used`, `framework/test/StoredScript.spec.js`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-core/src/script/oxide/resource.rs:227-237 (localized text expanded); crates/moqui-mail/src/implementation.rs:558-562, 1118, 1136; crates/moqui-core/src/service/system_sync/message.rs:104; system_sync/entity_sync.rs:344; service/sso.rs:634; user/authz.rs:626; service/rule_actions.rs:50
- **Reached by:** a user who may write `LocalizedMessage`, `EmailTemplate`, `EmailTemplateAttachment`, a system message remote, an entity sync detail, an identity provider mapping or an authorization filter (administration screens, the entity REST API, a data import).
- **What happens:** Each of these fields is evaluated as an expression or a `${}` template on the script runtime. Writing one is equal to holding `GROOVY_SHELL_WEB`, but the sealed-permission rule guards only the `UserGroupPermission` rows. A translation editor is the weakest case: a `LocalizedMessage` whose text holds `${...}` runs wherever its original is expanded.
- **Evidence:** `let text = if localize { crate::script::runtime::localized(&template)... }` then `run_here(scope, &literal, hash, ...)`
- **Not confirmed:** Who may write each record and whether any write path checks a permission was not traced; a delegated SSO mapping is described in a comment as stored as a plain claim name.
- **Suggested fix:** Do not expand localized text as a template (substitute named values only), and require the script permission to write the expression-bearing fields.

#### SEC-SCR-82. A template with nested template literals overflows the native stack in the tokenizer, which ends the server process

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/script/template.rs:827-836` (`scan`), reached from `:980-982` (`tokens_at`), `:1043` (`tag`) and `:238-240` (`markdown_fancy_source_with_base`); `crates/moqui-core/src/script/theme/package.rs:33`, `:1044`. Report: round 3 `B9.md`.
- **Reached by:** anyone whose text is compiled as a fancy template, trusted or locked: a theme author (a file of a package given to `ec.resource.theme.validate` or `render`, up to 1 MiB, tokenized at `package.rs:1044`), a message template author (`mr-mantle/service/mantle/party/MessageTemplates.js:232`), a wiki editor (a page of type `fancy-locked` or `html.fancy-locked` is enough, since the locked renderer tokenizes the same way).
- **What happens:** The scanner that finds the end of a `${...}` or of a tag's expression calls itself for each `${` inside a backtick string, with no depth count. A source of `${` followed by the three bytes `` `${ `` repeated N times recurses N deep before any parser sees it. A Rust stack overflow is not an error a caller can catch: the process aborts. A request worker's stack is 8 MiB (the comment at `script/run.rs:101`), and a service, job or scheduler worker's 2 MiB in a release build (`service/pool.rs:60` `WORKER_STACK`), so a template rendered by a job overflows sooner; 300 KB of source is 100,000 frames, and a 1 MiB theme file about 350,000. One stored text stops the server for everyone each time it is rendered or checked; no data is read.
- **Evidence:** `template.rs:827-834` `` b'`' => { `` / `index += 1;` / `` while index < bytes.len() && bytes[index] != b'`' { `` / ... / `} else if bytes[index] == b'$' && bytes.get(index + 1) == Some(&b'{') {` / `index = scan(source, index + 2, b'}')?;`
- **Evidence:** `template.rs:981-982` `scan_expression(source, index + 2, b'}').ok_or_else(|| fail(source, index, "${ has no closing }"))?;`
- **Not confirmed:** not run; the frame size of `scan` in a release build, so the least depth that overflows, was reasoned from sizes and not measured. Looked for and not found: a depth argument or counter in `scan`; a length limit on a template source before `tokens_at` (the theme's 1 MiB a file is the only one; `LockedRenderer::render` and `FancyRenderer::render` take any length). The same question stands for `oxc_parser` 0.158.0, which every expression and generated program is parsed with (`script/parse.rs:72`, `:90`, `:106`; `theme/expr.rs:87`; `template.rs:760`): it describes itself as recursive descent and a grep of its `src/` found no depth limit, `stacker` or stack check, so a deeply nested expression may overflow the same way; to check with a test.
- **Suggested fix:** Count the depth in `scan` and fail past a small limit (64). Refuse a template source over a fixed size before tokenizing. Check nesting depth with a linear pre-scan of brackets before handing untrusted text to `oxc_parser`, or parse on a thread with a known stack and a guard.
- **Re-check:** Read `script/template.rs:827-834`: inside a template literal a `${` calls `scan` again, with no depth count in these lines. Not run.

#### SEC-SCR-83. A locked template leaves promise jobs in its thread's sandbox, never run or dropped, until every locked render on that thread fails for memory

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/script/oxide/templates.rs:345-366` (`render_locked`), `:284-306` (the sandbox is kept for the thread); `crates/moqui-core/src/script/theme/expr.rs:33-69`, `:184-189`. Report: round 3 `B9.md`.
- **Reached by:** the author of a locked template: a Shop theme author, through any page of the store (public traffic renders it); a message template author; a wiki editor with a `fancy-locked` page.
- **What happens:** Each thread keeps its sandbox runtime across renders. The lock-down freezes objects, so a template cannot keep a value in one, but `Promise.resolve(v).then(x => x)` queues a job in the runtime, and no code runs the queue: `run_jobs` and `run_pending_job` have no caller outside a test. The theme's expression check lets this through: it refuses `new`, function bodies and a list of names, not `Promise` or a call, and an arrow with an expression body is allowed. A render that queues a job holding a large string (`"x".repeat(1000000)`) leaves about that much of the sandbox's 32 MiB taken after it ends. After some tens of renders on a thread the heap is full and every render in that sandbox fails with `out of memory`, other stores' pages included, until the server restarts. The out-of-memory path clears the compiled functions and collects, which does not empty the job queue.
- **Evidence:** `templates.rs:348` `context.call(&function, Value::Undefined, &[Value::String(data)])` (nothing after it drains jobs); `:359-362` `RuntimeError::OutOfMemory => {` / `sandbox.functions.borrow_mut().clear();` / `let _ = sandbox.runtime.run_gc();`
- **Evidence:** quickjs-oxide `src/engine/api/core/runtime.rs:624-627` `pub fn run_jobs(&self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {` / `while self.run_pending_job()?.executed() {}` (the embedder must call it); `grep -rn "run_jobs\|run_pending_job\|execute_pending_job" crates` finds only `script/oxide/engine.rs:829` `pub(crate) fn run_jobs_for_tests()`.
- **Evidence:** `theme/expr.rs:184-189`: only a `BlockStatement` arrow body is refused.
- **Not confirmed:** not run. That quickjs-oxide roots queued jobs (it must, to run them later); how many renders fill a sandbox; whether `FinalizationRegistry` or `WeakRef` give the same effect (they need `new`, which a theme expression reaches only through a `Function` made at run time). Looked for and not found: a drain or a clear of the job queue after a locked render or at the end of a script run (`run.rs` `DepthGuard::drop` releases resource objects only); a rebuild of the sandbox after a render; `Promise` in `DENIED`.
- **Suggested fix:** After every locked render drop the pending jobs, or drop the sandbox when the queue is not empty. Remove `Promise`, `FinalizationRegistry`, `WeakRef` and the async function constructors from the sandbox's global object before the freeze, and add `Promise` to `DENIED`.
- **Re-check:** Read `oxide/templates.rs:345-366`: the render calls the function and turns the result to text; nothing in these lines runs or drops queued jobs. Not read by the lead: the sandbox's job queue elsewhere.

#### SEC-SCR-84. `wikiType` is joined into a new page's location unchecked, so on file storage a page editor writes a file outside the wiki space

Medium; likely (lead).

- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/resource/WikiServices.xml:205`, `:592`, `:817`; `crates/moqui-core/src/context/resource.rs:542-546`, `:1093`. Report: round 3 `B9.md` (unit A4 left the question open).
- **Reached by:** as SEC-SCR-81, a user who may add a page, through `update#WikiPage`; it needs a wiki space whose root is a `file`, `runtime` or `component` location, which an administrator sets. The editor then writes a file of their choice with their text.
- **What happens:** `pagePath` segments are checked for `.` and `..`; `wikiType` is not. With `wikiType=/../../../service/x/Y.js` the new page's location is `<dir>/<name>./../../../service/x/Y.js`. A component location is the component's root joined with the rest, with no normalization, and `write_bytes` creates the parent directories first (which also creates `<name>.`), so the operating system resolves the `..` parts and the text lands outside the wiki's directory: a service script, a screen file, or a template under a screen.
- **Evidence:** `WikiServices.xml:205` (quoted under SEC-SCR-81)
- **Evidence:** `resource.rs:1093` `let path = if relative.is_empty() { root } else { root.join(relative) };`
- **Evidence:** `resource.rs:543-546` `if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {` / `std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|err| io_error(location, &err))?;` / `}` / `std::fs::write(&path, bytes).map_err(|err| io_error(location, &err))`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. What a `dbresource://` or `git` location does with a `..` segment; which wiki spaces of a default installation are on file storage. Looked for and not found: a check of `wikiType` in `update#WikiPage`, `get#WikiPageFile` and `create#WikiSpace` (`:817` `rootPageDirectory.replace(/\/+$/, '') + '/' + wikiSpaceId + '.' + wikiType`); a `..` refusal in `component_ref`, `file_ref`, `runtime_ref` or `writable_path`.
- **Suggested fix:** The fixed list of types of SEC-SCR-81's fix. In the resource facade, refuse a `..` segment in a `component`, `runtime` and `dbresource` location.
- **Re-check:** Read `WikiServices.xml:205` (builds `${dirLocation}/${name}.${wikiType}`), `context/resource.rs:1093` (a component location's rest is joined with `root.join(relative)`) and `:542-547` (the write follows `create_dir_all(parent)`, which makes the missing directory that a `..` then climbs out of). Not read by the lead: the `file` and `runtime` paths, `dbresource`, `git`.

## Outbound requests, processes and packaging

#### SEC-OUT-01. SMTP credentials go out without TLS when the server offers no STARTTLS, or when neither TLS flag is set

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `smtp_tls_mode` requires TLS for a session that signs in (a password or an OAuth token) unless `smtpSsl` is set or the host is loopback; an unauthenticated relay upgrades when STARTTLS is offered. Test: the `smtp_tls_mode` cases of moqui-mail `implementation::tests::a_server_not_allowed_the_private_network_is_refused_on_production_and_credentials_need_tls`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-mail/src/implementation.rs:1916
- **Reached by:** a network attacker between the server and an `EmailServer` whose `smtpStartTls` is `Y` (strips STARTTLS), or any observer when both flags are empty
- **What happens:** `smtpStartTls` builds `Tls::Opportunistic`, so the session continues in cleartext when the EHLO answer has no STARTTLS, and the password or XOAUTH2 token is then sent. With neither flag `builder_dangerous` keeps no TLS at all.
- **Evidence:** `builder = builder.tls(Tls::Opportunistic(smtp_tls(&host)?));` ... `SmtpAuth::Password(user, password) => builder.credentials(Credentials::new(user.clone(), password.clone()))`
- **Not confirmed:** whether lettre refuses AUTH on a cleartext session (not read); whether the UI forces one of the flags.
- **Suggested fix:** Use `Tls::Required` when the row has credentials (keep opportunistic only for unauthenticated relays), and refuse credentials on a session without TLS unless the host is loopback.

#### SEC-OUT-02. The address guard fails open on stored rows: it applies only when `allowPrivateNetwork` is exactly `N`

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `outbound::deny_private_network` denies an internal address unless the row's `allowPrivateNetwork` is Y on a production instance (N only, elsewhere), for `AuthFlow`, `SystemMessageRemote`, network printers, `EmailServer`, `ScrapeTarget`, `SyntheticCheck` and `EntitySync`; the Admin connection screens show and test with the same rule. On production a local relay, LAN printer or internal identity provider with the flag empty now needs Y. Tests: `outbound::http_tests::a_row_that_does_not_say_y_is_denied_on_production`, moqui-mail `implementation::tests::a_server_not_allowed_the_private_network_is_refused_on_production_and_credentials_need_tls`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-core/src/service/sso.rs:237
- **Reached by:** whoever can create or edit an `AuthFlow`, `SystemMessageRemote`, printer or `EmailServer` row by a path other than the admin Configuration services (a data file, an entity REST write, an older row)
- **What happens:** A row with an empty `allowPrivateNetwork` is sent to loopback, private addresses and `169.254.169.254`. Only `ConfigurationProviders.js`/`ConfigurationConnections.js` set `N`, and only when `instance_purpose` is `production`. Remote agent and MCP rows fail closed (guard on unless `Y`), so the two families disagree.
- **Evidence:** `let deny_internal = field(&flow, "allowPrivateNetwork") == "N";` and `deny_internal: field(&remote, "allowPrivateNetwork") == "N",` (system_sync/message.rs:173); `deny_internal: map_text(printer, "allowPrivateNetwork") == "N",` (service/tools.rs:295)
- **Not confirmed:** which other services write these rows and with what authorization; whether an entity default sets `N`.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat anything but `Y` as deny on a production instance, as `moqui-llm/src/remote.rs` does.

#### SEC-OUT-03. Scrape targets, synthetic checks, EntitySync, `remote-rest` and resource locations never use the guard

Medium; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: `ScrapeTarget`, `SyntheticCheck` and `EntitySync` have `allowPrivateNetwork` under the row rule of SEC-OUT-02; setting it Y on a target or check, or moving such a row's URL, needs the new `MONITOR_PRIVATE_NETWORK` (`TelemetryServices.store#ScrapeTarget`, `store#SyntheticCheck`; both entities are refused to generic writes). On production `remote-rest` refuses an internal host a call parameter fills (`remote.rs` `caller_names_host`), and an `http(s)://` resource location is public only. Every request through the shared client refuses link-local and metadata addresses. Tests: `tools/test/MonitorChecks.spec.js`, `remote::tests::on_production_a_host_from_a_parameter_is_refused_when_internal`, `outbound::http_tests::deny_internal_refuses_an_internal_address`.
- **Where:** crates/moqui-core/src/telemetry/scrape.rs:557
- **Reached by:** a user who can create a scrape target or synthetic check row (a monitoring administrator per the comment), or who can supply a resource location or `remote-rest` parameter
- **What happens:** The request goes to any address, internal included. A scrape stores the body's metrics and sends the row's bearer token; a synthetic check reports status and whether the body contains `expectBodyContains`, which reads an internal page one guess at a time.
- **Evidence:** `// Targets are set by a monitoring administrator, and an exporter on this host or network is the usual case.` `deny_internal: false,` (scrape.rs:557 and :712); `deny_internal: false,` (remote.rs:70, resource.rs:82, entity_sync.rs:502)
- **Not confirmed:** who is authorized to write the target and check rows; whether any request path passes a caller's text as a resource location.
- **Suggested fix:** Give these rows the same `allowPrivateNetwork` field, default deny on production, and refuse `169.254.0.0/16` always.

#### SEC-OUT-07. Compose files default the database password to `moqui` with TLS off and the port published on the host

Medium; reported (confirmed in code); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-16: the three external-database compose files take `${MOQUI_DB_PASSWORD:?...}` (and `MOQUI_DB_ROOT_PASSWORD` for MySQL), so a start with no password fails, `podman/env.example` leaves both empty, and the database `ports` lines are commented out. `entity_ds_sslmode: disable` is unchanged (the compose network only). Checked with `docker compose ... config`; no spec (packaging files).
- **Where:** podman/moqui-postgres-compose.yml:32
- **Reached by:** a local user of the container host, or anything that can reach `127.0.0.1:5432`/`3306` there, when the administrator did not write `podman/.env`
- **What happens:** `${MOQUI_DB_PASSWORD:-moqui}` gives the server and the database the password `moqui` (root `moquiroot` for MySQL) on an instance marked `instance_purpose: production`, with `entity_ds_sslmode: disable`. The OpenSearch containers run as `0:0` with the security plugin off.
- **Evidence:** `entity_ds_password: ${MOQUI_DB_PASSWORD:-moqui}`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${MOQUI_DB_PASSWORD:-moqui}`, `- "127.0.0.1:5432:5432"`, `DISABLE_SECURITY_PLUGIN: "true"`
- **Not confirmed:** whether port 9200 is published; `podman/README.md` warnings were not read.
- **Suggested fix:** Use `${MOQUI_DB_PASSWORD:?set it in .env}` so a start without a password fails, and do not publish the database port by default.

#### SEC-OUT-08. A stored field is run as a program: `runTokenCommand(instanceImage.authTokenCmd)`; the Docker API defaults to plain HTTP on 2375

Medium; reported (to check); fixed.
